# Orbital Overhaul — STATUS

Last updated: 2026-08-11 · Build version: **CS026 P5 — THE LEVEL BANNER'S FOUR LOOK-CALLS ARE GLOBAL KNOBS; THE GATE IS WRITTEN AND OPEN** (`GAME_VERSION` **"1.0.0.25"**, unchanged — P6 owns the next bump). **CS026 is the open changeset; all eight opening forks are already resolved by Paul, see `## Next up`.**

**CS026 P5 — THE LEVEL BANNER CAN FINALLY BE ANSWERED WITH A NUMBER, AND THE BLOCKING GATE IS NOW WRITTEN.** Per `IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-CS026.md` P5 / `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS026.md` §6. CS025 P5 built the level banner (critical voice line + large fading centre-screen "Level N") out of that changeset's own gate answer (Q6), so it arrived **after** that gate closed — nobody has ever seen it in motion, and its four constants (`LEVEL_BANNER_TIME` 2.2, `LEVEL_BANNER_FADE` 0.5, `LEVEL_BANNER_SIZE` 72, `LEVEL_BANNER_Y` 24) were first-guess numbers with no registry row, which collides with the house rule that a gate reports a number. This phase promotes all four to GLOBAL debug knobs and then writes the CS026 playtest gate itself.

**(1) Four new GLOBAL registry rows, appended after `startLevel`: `levelBannerTime` (s, def 2.2, 0–8, step 0.1), `levelBannerFade` (s, def 0.5, 0–3, step 0.1), `levelBannerSize` (px, def 72, 16–160, step 4), `levelBannerY` (px, def 24, −200–200, step 4). Registry 81 → 85.** Each constant stays in place as the row's `def` — the standing "retune the const, never the `def`" convention. None of the four is a lever: no floor/ceil/steps triple, no `▼`/`↳`, no `carriesTo`, no `LEVERS` entry — a banner's size and duration are look-calls, not pressure axes.

**(2) `nextWave()` and `drawLevelBanner()` BOTH now read `DEBUG.levelBannerTime` — never one reading the constant while the other reads the knob.** `drawLevelBanner()` derives `elapsed` by subtracting `game.levelBanner.life` from the SAME value `nextWave()` seeded it with; if the two sites ever diverged, a mid-level slider drag would send `elapsed` negative or make it jump, and the banner would flicker or vanish. The existing `Math.max(0, ...)` guard is what makes a mismatch (or, now, a genuine mid-level retune) degrade gracefully instead — `test-cs026-p5.js` §C reproduces the drag directly (seed life under one `levelBannerTime`, change the knob, confirm alpha stays finite and in [0, 1] in both directions) rather than trusting the guard exists.

**(3) `DIFFICULTY-LEVERS.md` §4 gains one not-a-lever row covering all four ids — the one design-doc edit this phase makes.** No GDD edit: the banner is already in §2.8 as shipped behaviour, and its *values* were never the GDD's subject.

**(4) THE SUITE COST: THE REGISTRY BUMP (81 → 85) TOUCHED 24 FILES, ALL COUNT/ORDER/ALLOW-LIST REPOINTS, NO BEHAVIOURAL CHANGES.** Same repoint-and-annotate idiom every registry-growing phase this changeset has used: every live "the registry holds 81 rows" pin, every GLOBAL-section membership list, `test-cs024-p6b.js`'s collapsed-order comparison (which needed its `levelBannerTime..Y` strip added *before* the existing `,startLevel$` strip, since the four new rows are now the true tail), `test-cs024-p6c.js`'s non-lever count (27 → 31), and every `LATER`/`LATER_ROWS` allow-list (`test-cs025-p1/p2/p5.js`, `test-cs026-p2/p3/p4.js`) now names the four `levelBanner*` ids explicitly. The whole suite (105 `scratchpad/test-*.js` files) runs green after the repoint pass.

**(5) THE GATE HANDOFF IS WRITTEN, PER §10, AND IT IS OPEN AND BLOCKING.** All eight questions from `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS026.md` §10 are now in `## Playtest asks`, verbatim, with the full P2–P5 briefing, the play range (levels 1 → 12, including at least one level in the 8–11 band), and the standing report-the-number instruction. **P6 must not run until the answers are recorded there.**

**CS026 P4 — DELIVERY FEEDBACK IS LEGIBLE AGAIN: THE FLOATERS SEPARATE, THE HUD `COMBO` ROW IS GONE, AND INCIDENTALS ARE QUIETED, NOT FOLDED IN.** Per `IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-CS026.md` P4 / `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS026.md` §3, FORK-CS026-E → **spread the existing floaters** (not a single updating tally — see the correction below), FORK-CS026-F → **drop** the HUD row, FORK-CS026-G → **quieter**, never folded in. The defect: `DOCK_OFFLOAD_INTERVAL` dropped from 0.13 to 0.05 in v3.4 P1 (20 canisters/sec) but `FloatText`'s rise (30 px/s) and life (1.1 s) were never re-tuned, so consecutive delivery floaters were born **1.5 px** apart — a 12-canister haul stacked twelve of them inside a 33 px band. Nothing had ever been removed; the escalating `+50/+75/+100` floater still fired every delivery, it was just unreadable.

**(1) `FloatText` gained trailing, optional `rise`/`life` params — `constructor(text, x, y, color, size = 16, rise = 30, life = 1.1)`.** All ten pre-existing call sites (SCOOP ±1/MAX, powerup pickups, the auto-shield penalty, GUARDED, SALVAGE BONUS, MAX HAUL) omit them and stay byte-identical — the CS012 P3 trailing-`size` precedent, repeated. **⛔ THE TRAP WAS THE ALPHA DIVISOR.** `draw()` used to divide by the literal `1.1`; if `life` became a parameter without also changing the divisor, a short-lived floater would fade in at a fraction of full opacity and never reach 1. Fixed by storing the constructed value as `this.life0` and dividing by that instead — `test-cs026-p4.js` §C pins the trap directly (a `life=0.2` floater reaches alpha 1.0 at birth, not ~0.18) and states the mutant it catches inline.

**(2) Two new DELIVERY registry knobs, right after `dockComboGrace`: `deliveryFloatRise` (px/s, def **300**, 30–600, step 10) and `deliveryFloatLife` (s, def **0.55**, 0.2–2.0, step 0.05). Registry 79 → 81.** Both are first-guess numbers — the playtest gate is what settles them, which is the whole reason they are knobs rather than constants. At the shipped defaults, consecutive floaters separate by **15 px** (was 1.5) and each travels **165 px** before expiring (was 33) — a 12-canister haul now reads as a rising column instead of a smear. `test-cs026-p4.js` §E measures this off a REAL 24-canister visit driven through the actual offload block (not computed): the two nearest towed floaters' actual birth-frame gap, fed through the real `FloatText.update()`, separate by exactly `rise × gap`.

**(3) ⛔ THE INCIDENTAL BRANCH IS QUIETED, NOT FOLDED IN.** There are two floater pushes in the offload block: the *towed* branch (`COLOR.dock`, size 16) and the *incidental* branch (a piece hooked while already parked at the dock — `COLOR.dim`, size 12 as of this phase). Both now read the same live `deliveryFloatRise`/`deliveryFloatLife` knobs so a Magnet-parked run of incidentals separates too, but they share **neither colour nor tally** — CS020 P1 fixed the delivery counter precisely so an incidental is not part of the haul, and merging their *feedback* would re-merge the two concepts at the exact point they were separated. `AudioSys.deliver(1)`'s flat pitch is untouched.

**(4) THE HUD `COMBO n/24` READOUT IS REMOVED — WHICH CLOSES FLAG-CS020-i, THE READOUT, NOT THE CLAIM IT WAS BUILT TO ANSWER.** `HUD_COMBO_X/Y/SIZE` and the `game.deliveryCount > 0` block are gone from `drawHUD()`. The bet: legible dock feedback (item 2, above) now carries "where am I in this haul" better than a duplicate HUD line. **⛔ THE RECORDED RISK: the readout was the only thing showing the DENOMINATOR (`/cargoMax`) — the dock floaters show none.** If the gate finds the cargo ceiling illegible without it, this is the first thing to reconsider; it should not have to be rediscovered. `test-cs021-p4.js` (CS021 P4's own test, which proved the readout's presence) is kept as living documentation rather than deleted or left broken — same precedent as `test-cs017-p6.js` §G for a fully-retired feature — its every scenario (a full skirt-and-return, incidentals, window expiry, ship death) now proves the readout's **absence**, while still driving the real counter to confirm CS020 P1/P1b's tracking machinery is untouched underneath.

**(5) ⛔ A CORRECTION TO THIS SESSION'S OWN PRIOR `## Next up` SUMMARY: FORK-CS026-E SHIPPED AS "SPREAD," NOT "ONE UPDATING TALLY."** Earlier entries in this file described the scoped work as "one updating tally" — that was `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS026.md` §3.3's option **(a)**, the doc author's own suggested reading, not Paul's actual resolution. **Paul's resolution (§0.1) and the buildable phase prompt (`IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-CS026.md` P4) both say "spread the existing floaters,"** which is what shipped: one `FloatText` per canister, separated by tuned `rise`/`life`, not a single object whose text is rewritten in place. Flagging this now so the "one tally" phrasing doesn't get treated as ground truth by a future session skimming old paragraphs.

**(6) THE OFFLOAD BLOCK'S LOGIC IS BYTE-UNCHANGED — THIS PHASE CHANGED HOW A DELIVERY LOOKS, NOT WHAT IT PAYS.** `deliveryCount`, the towed/incidental test, the 8/12/16/20 reward tiers, Heavy Hauler, Maxed Out, and the Super Mega Delivery trigger all fire exactly as before; `AudioSys.deliver()` is untouched. `test-cs026-p4.js` §E drives a full 24-canister visit through the real offload block and checks every one of those latches against the unchanged `DOCK_BASE_SCORE`/`DOCK_BONUS_STEP` formula, alongside `SALVAGE BONUS`/`MAX HAUL` keeping their **old** default rise/life (30/1.1) — deconfliction, spec §3.6, both stay anchored at the dock and untouched by this phase.

**(7) THE SUITE COST: THE REGISTRY BUMP (79 → 81) TOUCHED TWENTY-SIX FILES, PLUS TWO REAL BEHAVIOURAL REPOINTS.** Same repoint-and-annotate idiom CS026 P3 used for its own bump: every live "the registry holds 79 rows" pin, every "N non-lever knobs," every registry-order collapse comparison, and every `LATER`/`LATER_ROWS` allow-list (`test-cs025-p1/p2/p5.js`, `test-cs026-p2.js`) now names `deliveryFloatRise`/`deliveryFloatLife` explicitly rather than being wildcarded. Two files needed a real behavioural update, not just a count: `test-cs020-p1.js`'s incidental-floater string pin (now `COLOR.dim`/size 12/the new knobs) and `test-cs021-p4.js` (item 4, above). The whole suite (all `scratchpad/test-*.js`) runs green after the repoint pass.

**(8) DOC OBLIGATIONS: NONE THIS PHASE.** No design doc was touched — `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS026.md` §3 already carries this spec, and the GDD §2.10 delivery-feedback rewrite is P6's, same as P2/P3 deferred their sections. `DIFFICULTY-LEVERS.md` needs nothing — neither new knob is a lever; `LEVERS`/`leverState` are byte-identical to the parent at every level 1..200 (`test-cs026-p4.js` §F).

**CS026 P3 — A 1920×1080 WORLD FOR LEVELS 1–5, AND `resizeWorld()` FIRES IN A REAL RUN FOR THE FIRST TIME SINCE CS024 P1.** Per `IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-CS026.md` P3 / `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS026.md` §2, FORK-CS026-D. Three things landed and no more: `const WORLD_SIZE_EARLY = 2.25` (a third entry in the size table), `worldSizeFor(level)` returning it while `level <= DEBUG.earlyWorldLevels`, and one GLOBAL registry knob. The problem it answers is the **tail**, not the combat: at 2560×1440 the viewport shows 25% of the world and the farthest a body can be from the ship is 1,469 px, so the last two or three smalls of an early level are a search rather than a fight. At 1920×1080 it is 44% and 1,101 px. **No combat number moved** — not a lever, not a speed, not a count.

**(1) ⛔ 2.25 IS THE FLOOR OF THE SIZE TABLE AND THE BOUND IS A RENDERING ONE, RECORDED HERE SO NOBODY TRIES SMALLER LATER.** `drawEntity()` renders each body at **exactly one** wrapped image, and `onScreen()`'s reach is `VIEW/2 + CULL_MARGIN(100) + radius` — so for the largest body (a size-3 satellite, `DEBRIS_RADII[3]` = 46) the world's **half-period must clear 786 × 506** or bodies clip at the seam instead of crossing it. At 1920×1080 the half-period is 960 × 540 and clears both. At **1600×900** (size 1.5625) it is 800 × **450**, and 450 < 506: bodies clip at the top and bottom seams. At **1280×720** (size 1) they clip **and** a `SPAWN_MAX_DIST` spawn folds to **80 px** from the ship — inside `SPAWN_MIN_DIST` (220) and inside `DOCK_RADIUS` (88) — so a fully-visible world would need the flat spawn/dock rings scaled too, which is a different and much larger change. `test-cs026-p3.js` §A re-derives all of this from `CULL_MARGIN`/`DEBRIS_RADII` rather than quoting it, and asserts the two smaller sizes **fail**, so the floor is a live check and not a comment.

**(2) THE KNOB IS `earlyWorldLevels`, AND ITS 0 IS THE GATE'S CLEAN A/B.** GLOBAL section, `def: 5, min: 0, max: 20, step: 1`, inserted after `debrisBounceRestitution` and **before** P6d's `startLevel` so the gate-tooling knob keeps reading as the registry's trailing dev tool. **Registry 78 → 79.** No shipped constant backs the default — the `chainGuardIntercepts` idiom, so the entry itself is the source of truth. **Not a lever:** no floor/ceil/steps triple, no `▼`/`↳`/`(inv)`, no `carriesTo`, no `LEVERS` entry — the world period is a **stability/legibility property, not a difficulty axis**, and TRAP 3 pins `LEVERS` and `leverState` byte-identical to the parent at every level 1..200. At **0 no level satisfies `level <= 0`**, so `worldSizeFor` returns `WORLD_SIZE_FIELD` for everything, `resizeWorld()` never fires, and the build behaves exactly as CS025 shipped — driven for real in §D as a twelve-level climb that never leaves 2560×1440, not reasoned about. It is read **live at the wave boundary**, never cached: §D drags the band down mid-run and the very next level is the field world, then widens it and the level after is small again.

**(3) ⛔ THE SIZE DELIBERATELY DOES NOT FOLLOW THE ODOMETER'S SAWTOOTH, AND THAT IS THE ANSWER TO §2.3(ii)'s ODD QUESTION.** Under the odometer level 11 and level 21 are identical to level 1 in every lever, so a reader will eventually ask why the world is not. Because it is not a lever: the small world is a **one-time on-ramp**, not a difficulty rung, and making it wrap would re-shrink the world at level 11 for a player who has been in the big one for five levels. `test-cs026-p3.js` §A states this as an assertion (`worldSizeFor(1) !== worldSizeFor(11)`, `worldSizeFor(11) === worldSizeFor(21)`) rather than a comment, so a future "tidy it into the sawtooth" change fails loudly.

**(4) THE FLAT RINGS WERE CHECKED AND LEFT FLAT — AND THE OBVIOUS INSTRUMENT FOR CHECKING THEM IS THE WRONG ONE.** `SPAWN_MIN/MAX` 220/640 and `DOCK_MIN/MAX` 260/620 do not scale with the world. The tempting test is `max <= min(W,H)/2 - 60`, which **both maxima fail** at this size (that figure is 480 here) — and that would be the wrong conclusion, because `min(W,H)/2 - 60` is `resizeWorld()`'s **`dmax`**, the radius a *carried* body is clamped to when the period changes, not a bound on a *spawn*. A spawn is not clamped, it is **wrapped**: a nominal offset longer than the half-period folds back the short way. The worst case is a purely vertical offset, where 640 folds to `1080 - 640 = 440` against `SPAWN_MIN_DIST` 220, and 620 folds to 460 against `DOCK_MIN_DIST` 260 — both comfortably clear, and neither maximum even reaches the *horizontal* half-period (960), so only one axis ever folds at all. §C sweeps the whole circle analytically and then measures **hundreds of real `spawnFieldSatellites()` and 200 real `Dock` placements** in the small world. Leaving the rings flat is a checked decision, and the `dmax`-is-not-the-instrument point is recorded at the assertion so it is not "fixed" later.

**(5) ⛔ THE PHASE'S REAL SUBJECT WAS THE LEVEL 5 → 6 TRANSITION, AND IT IS DRIVEN FOR REAL ACROSS A POPULATED FIELD.** `worldSizeFor()` had returned a constant since CS024 P1 removed the orbit archetype, so `resizeWorld()` — the six-step carried-entity re-homing pass — **had not fired in live play for two changesets**; it was exercised only by `test-cs024-p1.js` §E, and *tested* and *shipped* are different claims. §E parks the ship near a corner (deliberately: **with the ship at world centre nothing can straddle a seam at all**, so staging it there would have tested step 1's old-period snapshot vacuously), loads the board with Hunters, garbage, powerups, particles, floaters, six seam-hugging pieces and a ten-node tow with a real verlet velocity, then crosses the boundary through the **real `nextWave()`**. Nothing is stranded outside the new period (bodies, chain nodes, ship, the new dock), the ship is re-centred and the camera synced, the chain's link spacing is preserved **to 1e-9** and every node's implied verlet velocity survives, and 240 real frames afterwards throw nothing.

**⛔ (5a) "NOTHING ENDS UP CLOSER TO THE SHIP" IS THE WRONG CLAIM AND WRITING IT FIRST IS HOW THE RIGHT ONE GOT FOUND.** Step 4 keeps each body's **bearing** and sets its magnitude to `min(d, dmax)`, so an over-range body **is** pulled inward, by design — FLAG-CS022-k's shell at exactly `dmax`. The checkable claim is the arithmetic itself: **new distance === `min(old distance, dmax)`, body by body, bearing unmoved to 1e-9**, with controls proving both branches ran (some bodies clamped, some did not). The player-facing version — nothing is *touching* the ship afterwards — follows from `dmax` being 660 in the new world, and is asserted separately.

**⛔ (5b) DEBRIS IS NEVER CARRIED ACROSS A TRANSITION, BY CONSTRUCTION, AND THE TEST SAYS SO RATHER THAN QUIETLY OMITTING IT.** Wave clear is `game.debris.length === 0` — debris only — so at a real transition the debris array is empty and there has never been a satellite to re-home. `resizeWorld()` still snapshots `game.debris`, which costs nothing and is what makes it safe if that ever changes. The first draft of §E populated debris and then watched the wave-clear step drop it on the floor before the resize ran, which is exactly the shape of a vacuous test; the premise is now an assertion.

**(6) `startGame()` NEEDED NO CHANGE, AND THAT IS CONFIRMED RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** It still calls `applyWorldSize(WORLD_SIZE_FIELD)` and then `nextWave()`, which compares `worldSizeFor(1)` against `game.worldSize` and resizes — on an **empty** field, where `resizeWorld()` is trivially safe. §F pins that its executable source is byte-identical to the parent and that it never mentions `WORLD_SIZE_EARLY`, that the resize precedes `new Dock()` in `nextWave()` (the order is load-bearing — the dock is placed ship-relative in the *new* period), and then drives a fresh run to confirm every satellite and the dock land in their rings measured in the world they are actually in. `resizeWorld()` and `applyWorldSize()` are pinned **byte-identical including comments**: this phase gave `resizeWorld` a caller, not an edit.

**(7) THE STARFIELD WAS NOT TOUCHED, AND `WORLD_SIZE_EARLY` IS DELIBERATELY ABSENT FROM `WORLD_SIZE_MAX`.** `WORLD_SIZE_MAX` still reads `Math.max(WORLD_SIZE_FIELD, WORLD_SIZE_ORBIT)`, unedited — 2.25 is below both operands so adding it could not change the answer, and the "generate once at the biggest size, filter per world" contract treats a *smaller* world as the easy case. §G pins the declaration's text, `STAR_COUNT`/`STAR_DENSITY`/`stars.length` against the parent, and the property that actually matters: the small world's active stars are a strict **subset** of the field world's, not a fresh roll — which is what makes the transition look stable rather than like the sky changing.

**(8) THE SUITE COST: 28 FILES REPOINTED, AND FOUR CLASSES OF THEM — ONE OF WHICH WAS A REAL FLAKE THIS PHASE CREATED AND FIXED.** Measured the CS026 P2 way, not guessed: the suite was run whole against a `git worktree` at this phase's parent first (**102 files, fully green, zero skips**), then against the working tree, so every failure was provably caused by this phase. **(a) The registry count**, 78 → 79, in twenty-one places across sixteen files — the established repoint-and-annotate idiom. **(b) Four "no later phase added a row" pins** (`test-cs018-p6.js` §B's GLOBAL membership, `test-cs024-p6b.js` §G's collapsed order string, `test-cs024-p6c.js` §G's non-lever count 24 → 25, and the `LATER`/`LATER_ROWS` allow-lists in `test-cs025-p1/p2/p5`) — all widened by **naming** CS026 P3, never by wildcarding. **⛔ `test-cs026-p2.js` §J got the narrowing it had itself applied to three predecessors one phase earlier**: its "exactly THREE rows were added" was a claim about the working tree, and is now a claim about P2. **(c) Two expected mirror-image repoints**, `test-v31-world.js` §A/§B and `test-cs024-p1.js` §D, both inverted at the same strength — and the surviving half of CS024 P1's claim is now stated separately and explicitly, because **a level-number band is not an archetype revival**: levels 3, 6, 9 and 12 no longer differ from their neighbours by *type*, and "no level runs at `WORLD_SIZE_ORBIT`" is asserted level by level exactly as before. **(d) Nine world-geometry files**, below.

**⛔ (8a) THE NINE GEOMETRY FILES SPLIT INTO TWO TREATMENTS, AND CHOOSING BETWEEN THEM PER FILE IS THE WHOLE JUDGEMENT.** Every one of them was staging boards off the **module-load `WORLD_W`/`WORLD_H` snapshot** — 2560×1440, the world the game boots in — which stopped being the world a fresh run is in. **Where the file's subject IS the geometry, it was repointed to the LIVE period** (`worldDims(game.worldSize)`, the CS022 P1 idiom): `test-cs015-p3.js` (the wrap antipode and the world centre), `test-cs025-p2.js` §D/§J (the seam the magnet push is measured across — `WORLD_W - 20` had been putting the ship 620 px *outside* the world), `test-v31-coalesce.js` §4/§27, `test-v33-p3.js` §2 (whose `captured()` now takes its ship position as a **function of the live period**, because the period is not knowable at the call site — `beginPlaying()` is what sets it), and `test-v34-p5.js` §E. **Where the file's subject is world-INDEPENDENT and its boards are pinned to exact counts, the feature was switched OFF at `build()` with the reason inline** — `test-cs020-p1.js`, `test-cs020-p1b.js` (both **cross-build** comparisons against the pre-fix build at `09d443f`, which predates the world-size seam entirely, and whose `quiet()` parks the dock at the snapshot centre *precisely so both builds agree on the point*), `test-cs024-p3.js` (the garbage density ceiling — `GARBAGE_SOFT_MAX`/`HARD_MAX` are counts that do not scale, and its 300-piece grid folded onto itself and reported 32,802 pair visits instead of the derived C(300,2) = 44,850), and `test-cs023-p2.js` §H's 720-body sandbox alone (not the whole file). **That is what the knob's 0 is for**, and using it beats re-laying boards to test a claim the world size has no bearing on.

**⛔ (8b) `test-f3.js` HAD A GENUINE ~1-IN-20 FLAKE, IT WAS THIS PHASE'S FAULT, AND IT IS THE BEST ARGUMENT FOR THE LIVE-DIMS TREATMENT.** `cx`/`cy` — where `resetShip()` parks the ship — was the snapshot centre `(1280, 720)`, which in a 1920×1080 world is an **arbitrary point 367 px off-centre**. The dock is placed on a random `DOCK_MIN_DIST`..`DOCK_MAX_DIST` ring around the **spawn** point, so roughly one run in twenty put the dock (radius 88) on top of that arbitrary point and §C's canister was swallowed by the dock instead of hooked onto the chain. Measured before and after: **2 failures in 25 runs** on the working tree beforehand, **0 in 60** after parking at the live centre, and **0 in 40** on the parent (so it is new, not inherited). The invariant the file was written against is restored rather than papered over: the spawn point can never be inside the dock, because `DOCK_MIN_DIST` is three times the dock radius. `test-v34-p5.js` §G had a milder version of the same class — a plain-object dock stub with no `draw()`, which never mattered while `drawEntity()` had no *wrapped* image to draw for it, and became a `TypeError` the moment the smaller world gave it one.

**(9) THE SUITE IS GREEN AND SILENT — 103 FILES, ZERO FAILURES, ZERO SKIPS, and the new file is byte-deterministic.** `test-cs026-p3.js` is 563 assertions across eight sections (A)–(H) and produced **identical output across 25 consecutive runs** (`installSeed(20260812)` at the top of the file, before the first build, per CS026 P1 §5.2 — this file samples hundreds of real spawns and drives thousands of real frames). Its no-git path was exercised for real, in a copy of the tree with no `.git`: **2 loud, counted skips, zero failures, exit 0.**

**(10) DOC OBLIGATIONS DEFERRED TO P6.** No design doc was touched (§2 already carries this spec; the test pins that both ways — no `.md` in the diff, and the GDD passage still present). P6 owes the **GDD §2.11.1 world-size rewrite**: the GDD currently describes the world as a single 2560×1440 torus, which is now true only from level 6 on. `DIFFICULTY-LEVERS.md` needs **nothing** — `earlyWorldLevels` is not a lever, and that is the point of §2 above.

**CS026 P2 — `junkSplit` JOINS THE ODOMETER AS A CARRIED LEVER; THE DEBRIS TREE HALVES THROUGH LEVEL 10.** Per `IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-CS026.md` P2 / `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS026.md` §1, FORK-CS026-A → **(b)** and FORK-CS026-B → **(a)**. `{ id: "junkSplit", floor: 2, ceil: 3, steps: 2 }` joins `LEVERS` immediately after `junkSpeedSmall`, and `"junkSplit"` is appended to `junkCount`'s `carriesTo`, which becomes four entries. **The only `LEVERS` diff is that one new row and that one array element** — pinned per entry against the phase's own parent, so a retuned floor/ceiling/step count anywhere else in the table fails rather than being absorbed by a count. `destroyDebris()`'s hardcoded `for (let i = 0; i < 3; i++)` becomes a loop over `Math.round(lv.junkSplit)`, off the **same** `liveLevers(game.wave)` resolution the child speed already used.

**(1) ⛔ CARRIED, NOT A DRIVER — AND THE RULE IS ENFORCED AT LOAD TIME, NOT BY CONVENTION.** Only a driver may wrap (CS024 P6b); `buildLeverOrder()` throws on a lever that declares `carriesTo` without `everyNLevels`, and `test-cs026-p2.js` §F exercises that throw against a deliberately malformed copy of the shipped table rather than trusting it. A **wrapping** split count would take children *back* at every wrap — the same "an odometer must never revoke capability" objection that keeps `payloadSlots` outside it entirely. Carried means it **plateaus**, and the plateau is the design: `junkCount` wraps every ten levels, so one carry arrives at level 11 and the lever sits at its ceiling forever after. **2 for levels 1–10, 3 from level 11 on, and never 2 again.**

**(2) THE SHAPE, RE-ASSERTED AT EVERY LEVEL 1..40 AND DRAINED OFF A REAL FIELD RATHER THAN COMPUTED.** §B starts a build at each level through the real `startLevel` knob + `startGame()`, then drains the field the real `nextWave()` spawned with the real `destroyDebris()`, counting bodies by tier. Level 1 = 21 bodies (was 39), level 10 = 84 (was 156), level 11 = 39, level 20 = 156 — the four rungs the prompt names, plus the 36 it does not. **The early levels were the SHORTEST in the game by body count and this does not change that**; it halves the whole sawtooth, so a 2-way level 10 (84) is still busier than a 3-way level 1 was (39). The plateau is checked out to level 1000 on the pure odometer, and the 10/11 boundary is pinned exactly on both sides.

**(3) ⛔ `destroyHunter()` KEEPS ITS OWN HARDCODED `i < 3`, AND NOW SAYS WHY AT THE LOOP.** It sits forty lines below the debris one and reads almost identically, which is exactly the hazard. `ACH_LINEAGE_FULL = 13` is `1 + 3 + 9`; a two-way Hunter split makes a lineage **seven** bodies and **Hunter's Bane structurally unreachable** — an unwinnable achievement, not a difficulty tweak. The comment names `ACH_LINEAGE_FULL` and says that a future changeset wanting a Hunter split lever must derive that constant from it **in the same edit**. Pinned three ways: three children at every sampled level, three children *with the `junkSplit` panel knob dragged to 7* (the check that would catch a "unify the two loops" refactor), and a full 13-body lineage driven through the real `destroyHunter` that really sets `hunterLineComplete`. `destroyHunter`'s **executable** source is byte-identical to the parent — only a comment was added, and a non-vacuity check proves the comment is really there.

**(4) `DEBRIS_MASS` — THE COMMENT MOVED, NOT ONE NUMBER (FORK-CS026-B → (a), PAUL'S CALL).** CS023 P2 derived 9/3/1 from mass being *conserved* through the 3-way split; under a 2-way split a large of 9 becomes 12 and then 4, so that derivation is simply false now. **Conservation is recorded as a RETIRED property, with the changeset that retired it, and the ratio is restated as FITTED** — chosen so a small ricochets off a large instead of shoving it. The numbers stayed because **nothing in the game reads a total mass**: `debrisBounce()` reads `DEBRIS_MASS` on exactly one line, and that line takes the mass of the two bodies in contact (§G pins both facts). Retuning to a conserving 4/2/1 would have reopened every satellite-vs-satellite bounce — a CS023 mechanic that came through its own gate clean — and flattened the ricochet ratio from 9:1 to 4:1. `DEBRIS_SCORE` is likewise untouched (FORK-CS026-C → (a)): Paul accepted the leaner curve, and compensating is explicitly not this phase's job.

**(5) THE PANEL AND THE LOG.** Three rows via `leverKnob("junkSplit", "Junk split", "")`, in **JUNK**, immediately after the three `junkSpeed*` triples so the chain reads in table order; min/max/step are **derived** by the helper off the row (span 1 → a 1–4 range at step 1), and the label wears `↳` because the table says it is carried. **Registry 75 → 78** — the first repoint since P6c to move the *lever* half of that count (51 → 54) rather than the non-lever half. `DIFFLOG_FIELDS` gains a `junkSplit` column beside the other JUNK-chain entries, logged **unrounded** like every other lever column (`junkCount` is the one exception, and only because `nextWave()` hands its already-rounded local in verbatim).

**(6) ⛔ THE REAL COST OF THIS PHASE WAS NOT THE LEVER — IT WAS TWENTY-SEVEN SUITE FILES, AND THE PATTERN IS THE ONE CS026 P1 EXISTS TO NAME.** The change is ~15 lines of game code; a green suite took repointing pins in **twenty-six other test files**. Measured, not guessed: the suite was run whole against a `git worktree` at the phase's parent first (**fully green**), then against the working tree, so every failure below is provably caused by this phase and not inherited. Four classes: (a) the **registry count** literal, 75 → 78, in fifteen files — the established repoint-and-annotate idiom, unchanged; (b) the **lever count**, 17 → 18, in five; (c) the **split shape** — `test-f3.js`, `test-v33-p3.js`, `test-cs017-p3.js`, `test-cs023-p2.js`, `test-cs023-p3.js` all pinned a literal `3` — repointed **off the lever** (`Math.round(liveLevers(game.wave).junkSplit)`) rather than onto a new literal, so a future retune moves them with it instead of breaking them; and (d) **eight "byte-identical to the parent/HEAD" pins**, which is the interesting class.

**⛔ (6a) THREE PINS WERE WRITTEN AGAINST `HEAD` AND HAD BEEN PASSING VACUOUSLY EVER SINCE THEIR OWN PHASE COMMITTED.** `test-cs024-p6c.js` §J, `test-cs024-p6d.js` §H and `test-cs024-p6f.js` §L each resolved their reference as literal `"HEAD"`. P6c's own comment argued the case explicitly — *"this phase pins a build it must NOT change, so HEAD is the correct reference and stays correct after the commit lands"* — and that is **true of the phase it was written in and false of every phase after it, in both directions**: once the phase landed, `HEAD` followed whatever committed most recently, so the pin compared the live build **against itself**; and the first phase to legitimately touch the odometer made it fail for a change the section has no opinion about. All three are repointed to **their own commit's literal SHA** (`273dbb2`, `c4f924c`, `82eae5a`), which is §4.1's rule applied to the exact defect it was written for. This is the tenth-through-thirteenth repair of this class and the first where the pin was *silently vacuous* rather than noisily wrong.

**⛔ (6b) THE "LEVERS IS BYTE-IDENTICAL" PINS WERE NARROWED, NOT DELETED — AND THE NARROWING IS THE SAME ONE, FIVE TIMES.** `test-cs024-p6b/p6c/p6f` and `test-cs025-p1/p2/p5` each compared the whole `LEVERS` table (or its whole `leverState` output) to a fixed parent. **A whole-table byte pin says "no phase may ever add a lever", which was never any of their claims** — each claims only that *its own* phase added none and moved none. All six now assert, per lever: every lever the reference shipped is still present, **in the reference's order**, field for field, with the one documented exception (`junkSplit` appended to `junkCount`'s `carriesTo`). An **added** lever passes; a moved, renamed, deleted or reordered one still fails, and so does a changed floor, ceiling or step count. Same shape for `leverState`, walked over the reference's keys. `test-cs023-p3.js` §H got the mirror treatment: it compared the live child count to a pre-CS023 build's, and now asserts each side against **its own source** — the lever live, the hardcoded 3 in the reference — so the one legitimate difference is *stated* rather than absorbed.

**⛔ (6c) `test-cs026-p1.js`'s OWN TRAPs 3 AND 4 WERE THE SAME DEFECT, IN THE FILE THAT INTRODUCED THE FIX.** They claimed "P1 changed no game code" and "P1 added no knob" — both **measured on the live build**, which turns a claim about one phase into a claim about every phase after it. Both now resolve **P1's own commit** via `ownCommit(PARENT_SHA, "cs-26 p1:")` and build from it, which is exactly the decomposition §4.1 prescribes, using the helper P1 itself shipped. Before P1 commits, `own` is null and both fall back to the live build — which *is* P1's build then. Two smaller repairs came out of the same sweep: `test-cs024-p6b.js` §G **crashed** (`TypeError`, not a failure) on a lever that post-dates its reference, now guarded; and the standing comment-strip idiom (`replace(/\s\/\/.*$/)` **then** `startsWith("//")`) turns a whole-line comment into a *whitespace-only line* rather than removing it, so an "executable source is identical" pin has to drop blank lines too or an added comment still reads as an added line.

**(7) THE SUITE IS GREEN AND SILENT — 27 files touched, zero failures, ZERO SKIPS, and the new file is byte-deterministic.** `test-cs026-p2.js` is 587 assertions and produced **identical output across 25 consecutive runs**; §K's long real run was measured flaking **2 in 12** unseeded (it reached level 10 rather than crossing the plateau, because the ship's fate varies), so it uses P1's `installSeed(20260811)` **at the top of the file, before the first build** — the game's own module-load randomness is spent inside the factory, so a seed installed after it fixes nothing. Its no-git path was exercised for real, in a copy of the tree with no `.git`: **2 loud, counted skips, zero failures, exit 0.** ⛔ **AND ITS TRAP-2 SCOPE PIN SKIPS WHEN `ownCommits` RETURNS `null`, WHICH `test-cs026-p1.js`'s TWIN DOES NOT** — there, a missing-history run leaves the scope pin neither checked nor counted, which is the silent-vacuum FORK-CS026-H exists to abolish. Left alone deliberately (one phase, one scope); **P6 should fold it into the same sweep as the ten shallow-clone files.**

**(8) DOC OBLIGATIONS DEFERRED TO P6 — RECORDED HERE SO THE SWEEP DOES NOT REDISCOVER THEM.** No design doc was touched this phase (§1 already carries the spec, and the test pins that fact both ways — no `.md` in the diff, and the target passages still present). P6 owes: **(a)** a `DIFFICULTY-LEVERS.md` **§3 row** for `junkSplit` (there is none — the test asserts its absence, so P6 adding one will trip that assertion and should repoint it); **(b)** the GDD's **four present-tense "3-way split" claims** — `ORBITAL-OVERHAUL-GDD.md` lines **55** (§2.4 debris splits, plus the "1 large → 3 mediums → 9 smalls = 13 kills → 52 canisters" arithmetic in line **56** that follows from it), **67** and **78** (Hunter — **these two are still CORRECT and must NOT be changed**), **118** (wave-clear "a large → 3 mediums, +2") and **204** (the sweep's "normal 3-way split" — Hunter, also still correct); and **(c)** GDD §3's Flow-functions row (line **547**), whose right-hand column says `destroyDebris` *"pushes 3 children if `size > 1`"* — that one is now wrong, while the `destroyHunter` *"pushes 3 homing children"* beside it is right. **The debris/Hunter distinction is the whole job here: half these passages are still true.**

**CS026 P1 — TWO SCRATCHPAD UTILITIES AND FIVE PINNED TEST PATHS. NO GAME CODE CHANGED.** Per `IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-CS026.md` P1 / `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS026.md` §4 and §5. `asteroids-deluxe.html` is byte-identical to this phase's parent (`0927743`), and it is pinned **by diff rather than by eye, twice over** — absent from the phase's own file list, and its `<script>` block compared byte-for-byte against the parent's (`test-cs026-p1.js` §H). Two new files, both Node CommonJS: `scratchpad/_phase-ref.js` and `scratchpad/_seeded-random.js`. **This is not a no-modules violation** — that non-negotiable binds `asteroids-deluxe.html`, which never loads either; `scratchpad/` has been CommonJS with `require` since the first headless test.

**(1) THE PHASE-REFERENCE HELPER, AND ITS DECOMPOSITION IS THE OPPOSITE OF THE OBVIOUS ONE.** Ten repairs across four changesets were pins written against a moving reference. The plumbing that gets it right was already inlined in `test-cs025-p1/p2`; it now lives in one place, lifted verbatim rather than re-derived (`test-cs025-p2.js` :174, :1185, :1191). **⛔ NOT `parentOf(subjectPrefix)`.** The **parent is a hardcoded literal SHA** — a fixed reference, and knowable at write time because it is simply `HEAD` before the phase commits — and it is the phase's **own commit** that is resolved dynamically, by subject, inside the bounded `PARENT_SHA..HEAD` range. Exports: `parentSource(sha)`, `ownCommit(parentSha, subjectPrefix)`, `changedFiles(fromSha, toSha)`, plus two additions each earning their place. **`ownCommits` (plural) is the raw list**, because the count carries three meanings the singular cannot: `1` is the normal reading, `0` is a pre-commit run, and `>1` is AMBIGUOUS and must stay a **failure**, not a skip — folding it into `ownCommit` would hide it behind a `null` that also means "not committed yet", which is the opposite situation. **`SKIP_TAG` is one exported literal** so every skipping file prints the same string and the closing phase has exactly one thing to grep for. `changedFiles(from, **null**)` means **the working tree**, untracked files included — the pre-commit fallback that same site already carried, folded in under a natural signature. **THE RETURN CONTRACT IS THE PART TO PRESERVE: `null` always means "could not ask"; an empty array is a real answer meaning "nothing matched".**

**(2) ⛔ FORK-CS026-H RESOLVED AS (c) — SKIP, LOUDLY; THE CLOSING PHASE ASSERTS ZERO SKIPS. THE INCONSISTENCY WAS REPRODUCED FIRST, ON A REAL `git clone --depth 1`, BEFORE ANYTHING WAS CHANGED.** Confirmed exactly as `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS026.md` §4.2 describes: `test-cs025-p1.js` and `-p2.js` skipped their git-dependent pins **silently** and passed (279 / 454 assertions), while `test-cs025-p5.js` §G **hard-failed** — `FAIL: G: the parent commit (cs-25 p4) resolved`, 88 passed / 1 failed. All three are now uniform: the pin **skips**, but prints `SKIPPED (no git history)` and counts the skip in its own summary line (`N passed, N failed, N skipped`), so a vacuous run is **visible** instead of silent. Re-verified on the same shallow clone afterwards: **4 / 4 / 1 skips, zero failures, all three exit 0**. Assertion counts on a full checkout are unchanged (373 / 554) except `test-cs025-p5.js` 94 → 93, which is the `assert(ps, …)` line **becoming** the skip. **⛔ AND `test-cs025-p5.js`'s PARENT IS NOW A LITERAL SHA (`fa9a543`, cs-25 p4).** It was the one file resolving its parent by **subject search** across all of history — precisely the shape §4.1 corrects — so it was repointed to the literal while the rest of the file was being touched. Same commit on a full checkout; the difference is that a literal cannot drift onto a future commit that happens to share a subject.

**(3) ⛔ THE SEED HAD TO BE INSTALLED AROUND THE FACTORY INVOCATION, AND THAT IS THE WHOLE POINT OF THE PHASE.** `test-starfield.js` and `test-p5.js` contain **zero** `Math.random` calls of their own — their nondeterminism is the **game's**, and `stars`/`starsNear` are built at **module load**, inside the factory (`starsNear.push({ x: Math.random() * STAR_NEAR_TILE_W, … })`, `asteroids-deluxe.html:8896`). A star's position is fixed the instant `new Function(...)(...)` is invoked, so a seed installed after it fixes nothing. `_seeded-random.js` ships `mulberry32` (pure, seeded, stable — int32 ops only) plus **two** entry points. `withSeed(seed, fn)` is the scoped form §5.3 names. **`installSeed(seed)` is the unscoped sibling, and all five pinned files use it, for a measured reason rather than a theoretical one:** in every one of the five the test *drives the game after the build*, so randomness lands on both sides of the factory. `test-cs018-p4.js` §I asserts twice per surviving Hunter after a 30-level driven run — its count is decided inside `update()`, long after the build. And `test-starfield.js` was **tried** with the seed scoped to the factory alone: §D settled, but §E still failed once in 200 runs (the ship met something that had spawned at random). Unscoped: 0 in 200. **Both entry points restore whatever `Math.random` they found, never a captured "real" one, so the existing `withRandom`/`withPinnedRandom` sites in `test-cs017-p1/p3` and `test-cs018-p4` nest inside the seeded stream unchanged.**

**(4) THE FIVE PATHS, MEASURED BEFORE AND AFTER RATHER THAN ASSERTED.** Before (on this phase's parent): `test-cs017-p3.js` 50/50 passing but the count split **1570 ×42 / 1569 ×8**; `test-cs018-p4.js` 50/50 passing, **541 ×23 / 535 ×27**; `test-starfield.js` **49/50**, the failure being §D's `a fractional camera shift moves the near layer by STAR_PARALLAX_FACTOR x the shift` (a second probe hit it at run 63 of 200); `test-cs017-p1.js` **399/400**, failing at run 101 with `F: level 10: forcing the spawn timer produced exactly one saucer`; `test-p5.js` **400/400 at a constant 102** — see `## Known issues`, its listed §C flake did not reproduce and appears already defused. After: **every one of the five is byte-identical across repeated runs** — `test-cs017-p3` 1570 ×100, `test-cs018-p4` 541 ×100, `test-starfield` 23 ×400, `test-p5` 102 ×400, `test-cs017-p1` 461 ×400, zero failures anywhere.

**(5) ⛔ SEEDING IS NOT THE SAME AS FIXING, AND `test-starfield.js` §D IS WHERE THE DIFFERENCE HAS TEETH — SO THE SEED CHOICE IS NOW AN ASSERTION, NOT A COINCIDENCE.** `expectedNearScreenX()` returns `null` when no tile offset puts `starsNear[0]` on screen and the assertion then fails on a `null` comparison, so a seed that merely lands the star somewhere convenient would make the test pass **without the assertion becoming meaningful**. The arithmetic was worked out rather than assumed: `STAR_NEAR_TILE_W === VIEW_W === 1280` and `STAR_PARALLAX_FACTOR === 0.5`, so at `camA = 1000` the offset is 500 and at `camA + 37` it is 518.5; the two lookups pick **different tiles** — failing by a whole tile width — for exactly `starsNear[0].x ∈ [496, 514.5)`, which is 18.5 of 1280, **~1 in 69** (observed 1 in 63 and 1 in 50). Under `SEED = 1` the star sits at **x = 796.60**, 282 px clear of that band and rendering at screen x = 296.60 — genuinely on screen and genuinely in the same tile at both camera positions. **Two new assertions in §D fail loudly if a later reseed moves it**, and `test-cs026-p1.js` §E re-derives the same claim from the live build so the two cannot drift apart silently. `test-cs017-p1.js` §F got the same treatment in comment form: its failure mechanism was **already on record** (`test-cs017-p3.js`:576 characterised the identical class) — §F pins `Math.random` to 0, fixing the spawned saucer's entry position to one point, and CS023 P3's UFO↔debris pass destroys it inside the same `update(0)` if a randomly-scattered satellite happens to sit there. `test-cs017-p3` could clear debris to defuse it; §F cannot, because it is asserting on the junk count `nextWave()` just spawned — so the **scatter** is what got pinned instead.

**(6) ⛔ A TRAP THE PROMPT DID NOT FLAG, FOUND BY THIS PHASE'S OWN TEST: TEN MORE SUITE FILES HARD-FAIL ON A SHALLOW CLONE, AND THE CLOSING PHASE NEEDS THE LIST.** `test-cs026-p1.js` §F runs each pinned file as a **subprocess**, which is how `test-cs017-p3.js` was caught crashing outright on `git clone --depth 1` (`fatal: invalid object name '683de82'` — its §F builds a pre-P3 reference commit). That file was already being edited by this phase, so its §F was routed through the same skip convention; **the other ten were deliberately left alone — one phase, one scope** — and are inventoried in `## Known issues` for P6, which owes FORK-H's zero-skips assertion and therefore needs the real list rather than the three-file one.

**CS026 P0 — DOCS AND TEST-COMMENT REPOINTING ONLY, NO GAME CODE CHANGED.** Per `IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-CS026.md` P0 / `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS026.md` §0.2. `asteroids-deluxe.html` is byte-identical to this phase's parent (`8fcea68`).

**(1) THE CS025 PLANNING PAIR IS ARCHIVED.** `git mv PLANNED-FEATURES-CS025.md IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-CS025.md archive/`, the CS023/CS024 `git mv` precedent — not copy-delete. `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS026.md` and `IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-CS026.md` were already committed at repo root ("Doc changes for CS026") ahead of this session.

**⛔ (2a) THE ARCHIVE MOVE ITSELF TRIPPED AN EIGHTH, UNLISTED PIN — THE EXACT "MOVING REFERENCE" LESSON THIS CODEBASE KEEPS RE-LEARNING.** `test-cs025-p5.js` §G carried `eq(git diff --name-only 2cd73e8 -- archive/, "")`, a true statement about CS025 P5's own scope (that phase didn't touch `archive/`) and a false one the instant a later changeset legitimately archives its own superseded planning pair — which git-mv-ing the CS025 pair into `archive/` in step (1) above necessarily does. Confirmed pre-existing-shape rather than a new defect: same class as `test-cs024-p6e.js` §G (CS025 P1) and the nine pins CS024 P7 retired at once. **Retired in place with the reason inline**, following that precedent — the TRAP 3 reference-resolution sweep two sections above it (untouched) is what actually still protects the invariant that matters (no live reference points at a path that doesn't exist).

The two forward-looking `STATUS.md` sections carrying it as a live mandate — `## Next up` and `## Known issues` — are rewritten to record the decision; the historical mentions in `## Playtest asks` (Gate B Q13, verbatim) and `## Working / verified` (CS024 P7's gate-outcome record) are deliberately left unedited, per the same forward-looking-vs-record distinction CS025 P0 drew for its own `-old` disambiguation.
