The rule prunes when the oldest covered changeset is *more than* ~3 rounds behind the one just shipped; CS023 is exactly **3** rounds behind CS026 (023 → 024 → 025 → 026), which is at the boundary, not past it, so no prune is due this phase. **The next changeset to land should re-check this first** — one more changeset shipping without a prune would put CS023 four rounds behind, which does cross the line.

**CS025 P5 — THE CLOSING PHASE: A COMPLETELY CLEAN GATE ON EVERY NUMBER, TWO GATE OUTCOMES THAT WERE NOT NUMBERS, `GAME_VERSION` 1.0.0.25, AND THE FULL DOC SWEEP. CS025 IS COMPLETE.** Per `IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-CS025.md` P5. **⛔ TWO OF THE SIX GATE ANSWERS WENT PAST "REPORT A NUMBER", BOTH WERE PUT TO PAUL BEFORE ANY CODE WAS WRITTEN, AND BOTH WERE APPROVED** — the phase prompt was written before the gate returned and assumed a retune-only outcome, so proceeding on either without asking would have been inventing design.

**(1) THE RETUNE — NOTHING MOVED. THE FOURTH CLEAN-GATE CLOSING PHASE ON RECORD** (CS020 P2, CS022 P4, CS024 P7, and now this one). Q1 confirmed `magnetResumeDelay` at **250 ms**, Q2 confirmed `magnetPushKick` **120 px/s** and `magnetPushSpread` **45°**, Q3 reported the Hunter-on-top-of-you problem *"has mostly gone away"* — the changeset's central bet, and the only question that could have invalidated it — and Q5 reported the defensive fill loop reads fine. **Both Q1 and Q2 asked for debug knobs "if I don't already have one"; all three already existed** as POWERUPS registry rows from P1/P2, so nothing was added. `test-cs025-p5.js` §F pins all three defaults *and* their panel presence, so "clean gate" is a checked claim rather than a remembered one. **⛔ Q6 did NOT report a stale `cargo_full`** (*"This is working well"*), so the phase prompt's inherited-defect clause never fired.

**(2) ⛔ Q4 BACKED OUT CS025 P3 ENTIRELY — A FEATURE SHIPPED AND REMOVED INSIDE ONE CHANGESET, ON PAUL'S EXPLICIT INSTRUCTION.** *"The scoop energy tell is not working. It is impossible to tell the difference between charged or not. I would like to just get rid of this function altogether. So this is backing out of a change I originally asked for in cs025. We just don't need another visual thing on the player ship."* The named risk in Q4's own wording is exactly what happened: magnet-blue `#8ab6ff` sits too close to `COLOR.ship` `#9fd8ff` to read as *charged*. **This is not the "the hue is wrong" decision the prompt anticipated** — it is a removal, and it directly contradicts prompt item (4)'s instruction that GDD §2.14.1 "gains the energized state", so it was confirmed with Paul (full backout, now, in P5) rather than interpreted. Removed: the `Ship.draw()` render branch, the `scoopLevel` 0 nose-V, the four `SCOOP_MAGNET_*` look-call constants, and **`drawPoly()`'s two trailing width/blur params** — their only two callers were in the deleted branch, and an unused optional param on the shared render primitive is cruft that invites a literal default. **Executable source for `drawPoly()` and the scoop arm is byte-identical to P3's parent (`914e5a6`), verified by diff rather than by eye.** **⛔ THE MECHANIC IS NOT BACKED OUT, ONLY ITS TELL:** P1's suppression and P2's burst both ship, and `magnetPulling()` is alive with its two real consumers. `test-cs025-p3.js` was **rewritten into a backout pin** whose §F is an explicit over-revert guard, because "P3 was reverted" is exactly the sentence that would get P1 deleted next.

**(3) Q6 MADE THE LEVEL ANNOUNCEMENT UNMISSABLE, IN TWO DELIBERATELY INDEPENDENT HALVES.** *"we need to add the announcement of the next level as a critical line... Minimum is to have that 'Level 2' show as a caption for speach. Ideally a 'Level 2' would appear briefly in the middle of the screen, large, easy to read, with some effect of fading in or out."* Both halves were built (Paul chose "both, in P5" over deferring to CS026). **The audible half:** `"level"` joins `VOICE_CRITICAL`, which is the whole of the wiring — `sayLevel()` now passes the event, so the queue and re-validation come along for free. It was losing the gate routinely because `nextWave()` is a crowded instant. **The visual half:** `game.levelBanner` + `drawLevelBanner()`, a sibling of `drawCaption()`. **⛔ THE INDEPENDENCE IS THE DESIGN, NOT A CONVENIENCE.** The banner is set unconditionally in `nextWave()` — no `AudioSys.ctx` guard, not gated by `settings.captions`, not by `voiceEnabled()`, never routed through the voice channel. Paul's minimum bar was *"we definitely SEE it"*, and routing the visual through the audio gate would have put that bar at the mercy of whatever Dan happened to be saying, which is the exact failure Q6 was raised about. §B of the new test proves the banner shows with **no audio context at all**. The two share a string and nothing else.

**(4) TWO CONSEQUENCES OF MAKING `level` CRITICAL THAT WERE FOUND BY RUNNING THE SUITE, NOT BY READING THE CODE — AND THE SECOND IS A REAL CORRECTNESS FIX.** **(a) `VOICE_QUEUE_MAX` 3 → 4.** CS025 P4's own comment called the cap "a guard for the day a fourth critical event is added without anyone thinking about depth" — the fourth arrived, so the cap moved with it and the guard stays *structural* rather than becoming live logic that silently eats a real line. **(b) `_enqueue`'s dedupe now REPLACES in place instead of ignoring the newcomer, keeping the FIFO slot.** Ignoring is harmless for P4's three events (a second `health_low` genuinely *is* the same crisis, and the entries differ only in which random alternative was rolled) but **wrong the moment an event's line carries DATA**: a parked "Level 4" still waiting when the player clears into 5 would swallow the "Level 5" trigger as a duplicate, then fail its own re-validation and be discarded — **so neither level would be announced.** Replacing means the queue always holds the current truth for an event, and depth behaviour is unchanged. `VOICE_STILL_TRUE` predicates now receive their queue entry `q`; the three P4 predicates ignore it and are behaviourally untouched (pinned via `.length === 0`).

**(5) ⛔ THREE PINS IN `test-cs025-p4.js` HAD TO BE REPOINTED AND ONE OF THEM WAS A MEASUREMENT BUG RATHER THAN A STALE LITERAL — WHICH IS THE MOST USEFUL THING THIS PHASE FOUND.** §E's and §F's "exactly the three named events" / "3 and 3" pins are the ordinary moving-reference case: true statements about P4, impossible about a build with a fourth critical, now restated against the **live tables** (every critical event carries a predicate; `#critical <= VOICE_QUEUE_MAX`) so they never need repointing again and now also fail the *opposite* way — adding a critical without raising the cap trips them. **§G was different.** It asserted `_enqueue` is *"NEVER reached from the drain"* but **counted every `_enqueue` call in 600 frames**, which was a valid proxy only while nothing else could enqueue. With `level` critical, 600 frames on an empty field clear a wave, so `nextWave()`'s announcement parks legitimately at **trigger** time and the counter read 1. **The guard was never broken — the instrumentation was.** It now counts only enqueues occurring *inside* `VoiceSys.update()`, which is what the assertion always claimed, plus a non-vacuity check that trigger-time enqueues did occur, so a zero can no longer come from an empty run.

**(6) THE VERSION — `"1.0.0.24"` → `"1.0.0.25"`, and the repo was grepped whole rather than trusted to a list.** The pins split three ways again. **Six live `=== "1.0.0.24"` pins repointed:** `test-cs010-p0` (×6 — the constant plus the `HighScores.add()` build stamp), `test-cs013-p4`, `test-cs015-p7`, `test-cs016-p5`, `test-cs017-p7`, `test-cs018-p10`. **Four CS025 phase pins flipped to their standing MIRROR IMAGE** (`assert(GAME_VERSION !== "1.0.0.24")`, the `test-cs021-p4.js` precedent): `test-cs025-p1/p2/p3/p4`, each with an inline note not to re-point it to a literal again. **⛔ TWO OF THOSE WERE NOT LITERAL PINS AT ALL, AND THEY ARE THE INTERESTING ONES:** `test-cs025-p1.js` §H and `-p2.js` §L each compared `GAME_VERSION` against **their own parent commit** — the *correct* form of reference, which is exactly why it is worth recording that they still needed flipping. **The reference was fine; the SUBJECT was the problem.** Every sibling claim in those blocks (`LEVERS`, `leverState` 1..200, nine function bodies) is about something the phase promised not to touch and nothing later would; the version is the one thing the closing phase changes **by instruction**, so "same as parent" was always going to invert here. Both are now `!==`, permanently true, following CS024 P6e's two named exceptions. **The twelve CS023/CS024 pins flipped by CS024 P7 needed nothing** — they already read `!== "1.0.0.22"` and stay correct.

**(7) TRAP 1 HELD, AND THE AUDIT IS WORTH RECORDING BECAUSE THREE OF THE FOUR PHASES GOT IT RIGHT.** A "no design doc was touched this phase" pin cannot survive P5 by construction — P5 rewrites four documents by instruction, which is what retired nine such pins in CS024 P7. **`test-cs025-p1.js` and `-p2.js` wrote theirs against their OWN PARENT SHA and resolve their own commit by subject, exactly as instructed; both were left untouched and both are green after the full sweep.** `test-cs025-p4.js` has **no doc pin at all**, so nothing applied. **`test-cs025-p3.js` was the exception: its §G ran `git diff --name-only HEAD`.** That is the **tenth** repair the moving-reference lesson has cost. It is **retired in place with the reason inline** (the file was being rewritten for the backout anyway), and what it protected is not lost — P3's no-design-doc rule was a TRAP in its own phase prompt and its diff (`4d720ab`) is in git history.

**(8) THE DOC SWEEP — FOUR DROP-NOT-QUEUE PASSAGES REWRITTEN, AND A FIFTH THE PROMPT DID NOT NAME.** `CLAUDE.md`'s VoiceSys rule **(3)**, GDD §2.8's *Cooldown & priority* bullet, GDD §2.8's *Captions* bullet, and the §3 Architecture Map's VoiceSys row — each rewritten to carry **the reasoning from §4.6, not just the new behaviour**: the old rule was **OVER-BROAD, NOT WRONG**, its concern is real, and **re-validation is what answers it** rather than overriding it. **Every one of the four also records the second cause explicitly** — the **priority-blind cooldown branch** that ate even `health_low` at priority 3 — because it is the half a reader would never guess had been broken. **§2.8's priority ladder was NOT edited:** the ladder is still correct and `cargo_full` is still priority 1, so criticality is **added as an orthogonal concept** (two questions, two tables) exactly as instructed. **The fifth passage is `CLAUDE.md`'s rule (4)**, which also asserted the caption obeys drop-not-queue; leaving it stale would have been the very defect this sweep exists to remove, so it was rewritten too — and the point it makes is that the one-gate invariant **needed no change** to survive P4, which is the invariant working. Also swept: GDD §2.14 (the Magnet bullet gains suppression, the resume delay, the burst, the re-arm, and an explicit "there is no on-ship tell, and that is a decision"), §2.14.1 (the backout, stating **in the GDD itself** that CS025 changed **colour/width/blur only** and that the **V-vs-box trade is NOT reopened**), §2.8 (the new level-banner bullet), §2.19 (72 → **75**), the GDD header, and the §3 Architecture Map's `draw()` row. `DIFFICULTY-LEVERS.md` §4 gained a not-a-lever row for all three magnet knobs and §6 gained this gate's outcome. `CLAUDE.md`'s code map gained `magnetPulling`/`magnetPushBurst` and `_enqueue`/`update`. One consolidated **CS025 (P0–P5)** entry appended to `GDD-VERSION-HISTORY.md` — opened only to append, never read for context.

**(9) `STATUS.md` SIZE CHECK: NO PRUNE DUE, AND IT WAS CHECKED RATHER THAN ASSUMED.** The rolling recap covers **CS023 + CS024 + CS025** — three changesets, which is exactly the window. The rule prunes when the oldest covered changeset is more than ~3 rounds behind the one just shipped; CS023 is **2** rounds behind CS025, so the threshold is not met and nothing was moved. `archive/STATUS-HISTORY.md` is untouched this phase.

**(10) TRAP 2 AND TRAP 3 HELD.** No gameplay change beyond the gate's own outcomes — **no new mechanic beyond what Paul explicitly asked for in Q6, no new knob, and the registry does not move (75, pinned)**. `LEVERS` is byte-identical to the parent and **`leverState` is byte-identical at every level 1..200, every lever** — pinned three times over (`test-cs025-p1.js` §H, `-p2.js` §L, and `-p5.js` §G). The archived `…-CS025-old.md` pair is untouched (`git diff` against CS025 P0 over `archive/` is empty), and P0's reference check was re-run: **every CS025-pair reference in a live file resolves on disk.** ⛔ That check is deliberately scoped to the *living* doc set — a bare repo-wide grep also hits **prose about** the old path in `STATUS.md`'s own P0 entry and its DISAMBIGUATION paragraph, which says in bold that those mentions were left unedited on purpose; "fixing" them would falsify a record that explicitly explains itself.

**New test `scratchpad/test-cs025-p5.js` (95 assertions, seven sections A–G).** `test-cs025-p3.js` rewritten (84 assertions, eight sections A–H, now a backout pin); `test-cs025-p4.js` repointed (111 assertions).

**CS025 P4 — `health_low`, `health_relief` AND `cargo_full` MAY NOW WAIT ON A THREE-DEEP QUEUE, AND ARE SPOKEN ONLY IF THEIR OWN TRIGGERING CONDITION IS STILL TRUE WHEN THE CHANNEL FREES.** Per `IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-CS025.md` P4 / `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS025.md` §4. **⛔ THIS OVERTURNS A DOCUMENTED NON-NEGOTIABLE WITH PAUL'S EXPLICIT SIGN-OFF, AND THE FOUR PASSAGES THAT STILL ASSERT THE OLD RULE ARE DELIBERATELY LEFT STALE** — `CLAUDE.md`'s VoiceSys rule (3), GDD §2.8's *Cooldown & priority* and *Captions* bullets, and the §3 Architecture Map's VoiceSys row. **P5 owns all four rewrites** (`PLANNED-FEATURES-CS025.md` §6 C1), and per §4.6 the rewrite must carry *what the old rule got right* — its concern (Dan narrating an event that finished ten seconds ago) is real and is answered by re-validation, not overridden. The rule was **over-broad, not wrong**.

**(1) THE DEFECT HAD TWO CAUSES AND ONLY ONE OF THEM WAS THE OBVIOUS ONE.** `cargo_full` is not in `VOICE_PRIORITY` at all, so it defaults to 1 and *any* line in flight dropped it — that is cause 1. Cause 2 is that `_emit`'s **second** gate branch, the post-line `VOICE_COOLDOWN` gap, was **priority-blind**: it returned null unconditionally, so **even `health_low` at priority 3 was silently eaten inside that 1.2 s window**. No priority in the system could survive it. Fixing only cause 1 would have left the game's single most urgent line still losable, which is why §C of the new test tests the gap directly rather than through the queue.

**(2) TWO QUESTIONS, TWO TABLES — `VOICE_PRIORITY` IS NOT TOUCHED AND `cargo_full` STAYS PRIORITY 1.** The new `VOICE_CRITICAL = { health_low, health_relief, cargo_full }` is **orthogonal to priority**: criticality answers *may this line wait?*, priority answers *may this line interrupt?*. Promoting `cargo_full` to 3 to make it "critical" would also have granted it the power to **pre-empt** the health tier — a truck-full bark cutting off "hull integrity is critical", which is exactly backwards. `test-cs025-p4.js` §K pins `VOICE_PRIORITY` byte-identical to the parent and asserts `cargo_full` is still absent from it.

**(3) PRE-EMPTION IS UNCHANGED, AND THAT IS A DELIBERATE CHOICE RATHER THAN AN OVERSIGHT.** `health_low` (3) still cuts off anything at priority ≤ 2 exactly as it did before. **The queue is purely ADDITIVE — it catches lines that would have been DROPPED and changes nothing about lines that currently SPEAK.** Making criticals "always wait" would have made `health_low` *slower* than it is today. This is also what made the phase's central prediction testable: **`test-cs010-p9.js` §D and `test-cs011-p2.js` §D were run BEFORE any new test was written and both passed byte-identically, unmodified** — every drop assertion in them fires on `collect_rapid`/`collect_triple` or a bare `_emit` with no event (all non-critical), and their one `health_low` call is the pre-empt case this phase preserves. §A of the new file re-runs both **and** asserts they are unmodified relative to HEAD, so a later phase cannot "fix" the test instead of the code.

**(4) RE-VALIDATION AT DRAIN TIME IS WHAT ANSWERS THE OLD RULE, AND `health_relief`'s `!game.ship.dead` TERM IS THE LOAD-BEARING ONE.** `VOICE_STILL_TRUE` restates each trigger's own condition — `health_low` mirrors the rising-edge test, `cargo_full` mirrors the pickup gate, and `health_relief` mirrors the falling edge **including its `!game.ship.dead` guard**, which GDD §2.12 calls load-bearing. **A queue makes that failure MORE reachable, not less**: the line can now sit parked across the very frames in which the ship dies, so without the mirrored guard the game would play a cheerful "you're okay now" at the instant the player is killed. Mirroring it is what keeps CS010 P3's FLAG-CS010-a resolution true under the new mechanism, and §E stages that exact case. A line failing its predicate is **discarded silently** — no audio, no caption, `busyUntil`/`curPriority` untouched — never spoken late.

**(5) THE DRAIN TAKES NO `dt`, AND THAT IS A CONSEQUENCE OF CHOOSING RE-VALIDATION OVER A TTL RATHER THAN AN OMISSION.** A TTL would have ticked on the **game** clock while `busyUntil` lives on the **audio** clock (`ctx.currentTime`, which does not pause) — a mismatch that would have expired queued lines during a long pause. Option (c) removes the hazard instead of managing it; the comment at the site says so, because the obvious later "improvement" is to add a TTL as well. `VoiceSys.update()` is called from the **very end of `update()`'s playing body**, after every pass that can `say()`, so this frame's live events get first claim on an idle channel and the queue only fills the gaps they leave. It never runs while paused, during the `dying` spectacle, or on a menu — **which is why the only teardown site is `VoiceSys.reset()`** (already called from `startGame()`); `quitToTitle()` and `killShip()` deliberately get nothing, and the comment says why so nobody adds dead code there later.

**(6) THE `now >= busyUntil` GUARD IS WHAT MAKES THE ONE LOOPING SHAPE IMPOSSIBLE.** It renders `_emit`'s busy branch unreachable from the drain, so a drained line can never re-queue itself. §G proves it positively rather than by inspection: 600 real `update(1/60)` frames against a permanently-busy channel with a wrapped `_enqueue` counter — **0 calls, depth never above `VOICE_QUEUE_MAX`, entries still parked.** The queue itself is FIFO, deduped by event, capped at `VOICE_QUEUE_MAX` 3; with three critical events and dedupe **the cap is structurally unreachable today** and is a guard for the day a fourth critical event is added without anyone thinking about depth — §F proves both halves (the shipped three can never exceed 3, and a synthetic fourth is rejected at 3). The line is picked at **trigger** time, not drain time.

**Nothing else moved.** `VOICE_LINES` is not edited at all, so **the standing voice-line gate does not apply** (§4.7) — all three critical events already ship with phon strings verified in `tools/voice-robot-lab.html`; no session should believe itself blocked on it. `game.lowHpVoiced` needs **no change** and this was checked rather than assumed: a queued `health_low`'s discard condition (hp back above `LOW_HP_THRESHOLD`) is the *same edge* that already clears the latch in the falling-edge branch, so it self-heals (§4.8). **Registry stays at 75 (TRAP 4)** — no knob this phase. `GAME_VERSION` stays `"1.0.0.24"` (TRAP 1). **No design doc touched (TRAP 2).** The ported-verbatim engine — `_schedule`, `buildUtterance`, `buildPitch`, `parsePhonTokens`, `PH`, `VOICE_STYLES` — is pinned `toString()`/JSON-identical to the parent, and `_emit`'s arithmetic **outside the two named branches** is pinned byte-identical by slicing both versions from `const utt = buildUtterance` onward (TRAP 5). New test `scratchpad/test-cs025-p4.js` (101 assertions, eleven sections A–K).

**CS025 P3 — WHILE `magnetPulling()` IS TRUE, THE SCOOP MOUTH IS THE TELL, NOT THE HUD.** Per `IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-CS025.md` P3 / `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS025.md` §3, landed against P1's `magnetPulling()`. **(1) `drawPoly(points, x, y, angle, color, closed = true, width, blur)` gains two trailing params, left undefined for every pre-existing call site** so `glowStroke`'s own defaults (1.6, 10) apply and stay the ONE place those numbers live — no literal `1.6`/`10` was duplicated into `drawPoly`'s own signature. **(2) `Ship.draw()`'s scoop arm branches on `magnetPulling()`, not `powerActive("magnet")`** — a banked-but-suppressed Magnet (cargo full, or still inside the resume delay) draws the mouth in the ordinary `COLOR.dock`, at the ordinary width/blur; only while genuinely pulling does it recolour to `POWERUP_COLOR.magnet` and re-stroke at `SCOOP_MAGNET_W`/`SCOOP_MAGNET_BLUR`. **Geometry at `scoopLevel` 1–5 is untouched** — same `[[d,-hw],[16,0],[d,hw]]` corners off `SCOOP_WIDTH[lvl]`/`SCOOP_DEPTH[lvl]`, same `closed = false`, no fill. **At `scoopLevel` 0, nothing draws unless pulling**, in which case a small fixed nose-V (`SCOOP_MAGNET_NOSE_W`/`SCOOP_MAGNET_NOSE_D`) appears in the same magnet colour/width/blur — the tell survives at level 0, where the mouth itself has no geometry to recolour. **(3) Four LOOK-CALL constants** (`SCOOP_MAGNET_W` 2.6, `SCOOP_MAGNET_BLUR` 18, `SCOOP_MAGNET_NOSE_W` 10, `SCOOP_MAGNET_NOSE_D` 22) join the SCOOP block, deliberately not debug knobs — the `HELD_CLUMP_RING_PAD`/`GUARD_CHAIN_WIDTH`/`_BLUR` convention. **Registry stays at 75 (TRAP 3).** `SCOOP_WIDTH`/`SCOOP_DEPTH`/`SCOOP_CONFIG`/`buildScoopSteps`/the `SCOOP_WIDTH[0] !== 0` load-time invariant are all untouched (TRAP 4), and `inScoopBox()`'s capture math never reads colour, width or blur so it needed no change. `GAME_VERSION` stays `"1.0.0.24"` (TRAP 1). No design doc touched (TRAP 2) — §3 already carried this phase's spec. New test `scratchpad/test-cs025-p3.js` (71 assertions, seven sections A–G): drawPoly's byte-identity for old-arity calls; the colour discriminator (banked+active-but-not-pulling stays `COLOR.dock`, only a genuinely live pull goes magnet-blue); the level-0 nose V appearing only while pulling; geometry unchanged at every level 1–5 in both pulling states; `inScoopBox()`'s level-0-always-false invariant undisturbed; no new fill in `Ship.draw()`; and the TRAPs.

**CS025 P2 — ON THE FRAME THE TOW CHAIN BECOMES FULL WHILE THE MAGNET IS ACTIVE, EVERY GARBAGE PIECE WITHIN `MAGNET_RANGE` IS PUSHED OUTWARD ON A RANDOMLY-FANNED VECTOR AND HAS ITS `coalesceDelay` RE-ARMED.** Per `IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-CS025.md` P2 / `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS025.md` §2 and the two kick rows of §5. **What P1 left undone:** suppressing the pull stops the Magnet *adding* to the cloud; it does nothing about the cloud already sitting on the ship at the instant the chain fills — and that cloud is the one that converts into a Hunter at the player's own position. **A third instance of a shipped idiom, not a new mechanic shape:** `shatterClump()` and the partial-scoop leftover respill both already do outward-kick-plus-re-armed-delay, and `shatterClump`'s own comment states why — *"a full re-armed `coalesceDelay` (so the burst disperses before it can start re-clumping)"*.

**(1) ⛔ THE RE-ARM IS WHAT SOLVES THE DEFECT; THE VELOCITY IS WHAT MAKES IT LEGIBLE — AND THAT IS PINNED AS THREE ARMS RATHER THAN ASSERTED.** Conversion happens at exactly one place, `coalesceGarbage()`'s merge branch, which requires `coalesceDelay <= 0` on **both** sides, so a re-armed piece cannot merge whichever way it drifts. §H of the new test stages the defect itself — twelve pieces piled on the ship with their inert window already spent, at the moment cargo fills — and runs it three ways through the **real** `coalesceGarbage()`: **shipped → no Hunter**; **`magnetPushKick` at 0, re-arm intact → still no Hunter** (the re-arm alone is sufficient); **kick 0 *and* the re-arm undone every frame → a Hunter is produced, within 60 px of the ship.** The third arm is what makes the first two non-vacuous, and it reproduces the defect this changeset exists to remove.

**(2) THE EDGE DETECTOR IS A PLAIN STORED BOOLEAN — `game.cargoWasFull` — AND THE INFERENCE IT REFUSES IS THE INTERESTING PART.** Inferring the rising edge from `game.magnetHoldT < DEBUG.magnetResumeDelay` looks equivalent (P1's writer pins the hold at the delay on every full frame, so it reads below only on the first one) and breaks on one specific action: **dragging the `magnetResumeDelay` slider upward while parked at full** instantly makes the stale hold read "below the delay" again and re-fires the burst — at the playtest gate, in the exact session where that knob is being tuned. **§B of the test carries its own teeth:** it asserts the inference's condition is *true* on the retune frame before asserting that no burst fires, so the section fails rather than passes vacuously if anyone swaps the boolean back out. Two assignments in the whole build (the per-frame latch, `startGame()`'s reset), one reader, and the block's order — read → burst → arm/tick → latch — is pinned line by line.

**(3) THE GATE IS `powerActive("magnet")`, NOT `magnetPulling()`, AND AT THE RISING EDGE THE TWO AGREE.** The hold has not been set yet on that frame, so the choice is behaviourally invisible and is made for what it *says*: this is a **magnet** mechanic, not a general cargo-full effect — with no Magnet running there is no gathered cloud to disperse. §C proves it with no budget banked: no burst, no velocity change (exact zero, frame after frame), no `coalesceDelay` write. Structurally pinned too, since a mutation to `magnetPulling()` would pass §C unchanged.

**(4) `magnetPushBurst()` — A NEW FLOW FUNCTION AT THE END OF THE GARBAGE-PIPELINE CLUSTER, AND EVERY LINE IN IT IS A PLACE A PLAUSIBLE EDIT IS SUBTLY WRONG.** `shortDelta`, never subtraction, **ship → piece** so the impulse is outward (reversing the argument order produces a stronger *implosion* — the defect amplified rather than fixed); `+=`, never `=` (an impulse, not a teleport); `/ Math.sqrt(g.mass)` mirroring the pull's own sqrt, so a mass-1 single is unaffected and a mass-11 clump is shoved at ~30%; the `d > 1e-4` guard, which is **the defect's own case** rather than boilerplate (`atan2(0, 0)` returns 0 and would push every piece sitting on the ship's centre dead +x in lockstep); and `liveLevers`, never `leverState`. **All five are pinned behaviourally, not just by source regex** — the wrap test places the ship 20 px inside the right edge and the piece 30 px past the left one and carries a naive-subtraction control proving the sign would be opposite; the mass test measures an exact 3:1 ratio against the knob; the impulse test gives a piece a pre-existing 70 px/s drift and proves it survives; the `liveLevers` test pins `coalescePause` flat through the panel's Floor/Ceil rows so `liveLevers` and `leverState` genuinely disagree, and watches the re-arm follow the panel.

**⛔ NINE MUTATIONS WERE RUN AGAINST THE FINISHED TEST BEFORE THE COMMIT, AND ALL NINE WERE CAUGHT** — reversed `shortDelta` args (12 failures), `=` for `+=` (3), linear mass for sqrt (6), the degenerate guard removed (4), `leverState` for `liveLevers` (4), the `magnetHoldT` inference for the boolean (6), the re-arm removed (18), the burst moved below the arm and gated on `magnetPulling()` (107), and the range test widened (1). The working tree was restored and the suite re-verified clean afterwards. This is recorded because a test whose teeth were never checked is a test that quietly stops having any.

**(5) SATURATED (HELD) CLUMPS ARE KICKED, DELIBERATELY, WITH NO SPECIAL CASE — AND THE INERT WRITE IS LABELLED AT THE SITE SO IT IS NEVER READ AS A BUG.** Shoving a *pending Hunter* away from the ship is the most valuable thing the burst does. Re-arming its `coalesceDelay` is inert, and §I proves that at **both** consumers rather than arguing it: the pair walk skips it on both sides via `saturatedClump()` (a loose single parked 4 px away is still not absorbed, thirty frames later), and `drainHeldClumps()` converts it the moment a slot opens regardless of any delay. The kicked clump is also still exempt from the cull, still **partially** scoopable through the real intake (12 → 7 against five free slots, and no longer `saturatedClump()`, because "held" is derived), and still shatterable into twelve fresh singles. **⛔ Staging a held clump at all requires OCCUPYING the large-Hunter ceiling first** — at level 1 `largeHunterCap` is 1, so with a free slot the very next frame's `drainHeldClumps()` converts the clump and there is nothing "held" to test. One live size-3 Hunter parked in the far corner (re-parked each frame so it can never reach the ship and break a chain mid-measurement) is what makes CS024 P6f's hold arm reachable from a test at all; worth recording, since the first draft of §I failed six ways for exactly this reason.

**(6) TWO KNOBS, NOT ONE (FLAG-18).** `magnetPushKick` (`px/s`, def 120, 0–600, step 10) and `magnetPushSpread` (`°`, def 45, 0–180, step 5) join **POWERUPS** after `magnetResumeDelay`. Speed and fan-out are independently wrong-feeling in different ways and one slider cannot separate them; the gate has to come back with two numbers. **`magnetPushKick` at 0 is the gate's clean A/B** — and note it disables the *velocity*, not the mechanic: the re-arm still fires, which is exactly why arm 2 of §H survives. `magnetPushSpread` is a **per-piece half-angle**, not a whole-burst rotation (§F pins 24 pieces drawing 24 distinct offsets, two-sided, magnitude untouched, against a seeded RNG stream so it is deterministic). Neither is a lever: no floor/ceil/steps triple, no `▼`/`↳`, no `carriesTo`, no `LEVERS` entry, and no shipped constant behind either default (the `chainGuardIntercepts` idiom). **Registry 73 → 75.** Persistence needed no code change — both are ordinary `DEBUG_ENTRIES` rows through the existing generic path, master override toggle included.

**Nothing else moved (TRAP 3), and it is pinned as function-source byte-identity against the parent rather than argued.** `coalesceGarbage()`, `drainHeldClumps()`, `cullGarbage()`, `saturatedClump()`, `shatterClump()`, `heldClumpCount()`, `betterCullVictim()`, `magnetPulling()` and `powerActive()` are all `toString()`-identical to `68c1ec5` — **this phase adds a PRODUCER of `coalesceDelay` writes, not a change to any consumer.** `MAGNET_RANGE` (the burst reuses the pull's own reach), `HUNTER_COALESCE_COUNT`, `GARBAGE_SOFT_MAX`/`HARD_MAX` and their knobs are unmoved, so **no frame-budget ceiling is tightened**: §J counts the burst's visits with a counting iterator installed on the array it walks (220 visits at a 220-piece field, 22 at a 22-piece one — linear, one loop, never a pair walk) and proves it runs **once per fill** with exactly one burst across 302 frames at a full field. `GAME_VERSION` stays `"1.0.0.24"` (TRAP 1). No design doc touched (TRAP 2) — §2/§5 already carried this phase's spec, and §5's `DIFFICULTY-LEVERS.md` not-a-lever row is still **P5's** to write. New test `scratchpad/test-cs025-p2.js` (554 assertions, twelve sections A–L). **⛔ Every "nothing else moved" claim in it is written against this phase's OWN PARENT COMMIT (`68c1ec5`), never against `HEAD` (TRAP 4)**, and its doc pin resolves this phase's *own commit* by subject and diffs `parent..commit`, so P5's doc sweep cannot falsify it.

**REPOINT SWEEP — 17 FILES, AND THE THREE THAT WERE NOT ABOUT THE COUNT ARE THE ONES WORTH READING.** The registry count (73 → 75) moved in `test-cs018-p4/p6/p7`, `test-cs020-p1/p1b`, `test-cs023-p2/p3`, `test-cs024-p1/p2/p3/p4/p5/p6/p6b/p6c/p6f` and `test-cs025-p1` — every one still asserts what its own phase claimed. The substantive three:

**(a) `test-cs024-p4.js` §H — THE `liveLevers` CALL-SITE CENSUS, 13 → 14, AND THE HALF THAT MUST NOT MOVE STAYS PUT.** `magnetPushBurst()`'s re-arm is a genuine thirteenth consumer, and the pin's real claim — *`leverState` appears exactly once in executable source, its own declaration* — is untouched and is what the inline note now says explicitly: a new `liveLevers` **consumer** is allowed, a `leverState` **caller** is not.

**(b) `test-cs024-p6c.js` §G — the non-lever knob census 22 → 24**, with the same reasoning every prior repoint recorded: the per-row checks below it are what pin the two new knobs as non-levers, so the count growing **is** the claim rather than a bypass of it.

**(c) ⛔ `test-cs025-p1.js` §G — TWO PINS NARROWED, ONE RE-STATED, AND ALL THREE ARE THE SAME LESSON IN A NEW COSTUME.** P1 asserted that `magnetResumeDelay` was the **last** row of POWERUPS, that **exactly one** id had been added since its parent, and that the panel grew by **exactly one** row. All three are true statements about P1 and impossible ones about any working tree after it — a later phase appending to the same section falsifies each. What P1's spec §5 actually promised is the **placement**, which is unchanged and still pinned (*inside POWERUPS, immediately after `engineMassMult`*); "last" is now "every POWERUPS row after it belongs to a later phase", "exactly one id" is now "P1's one id was added, and every other one is a later phase's", and the row-count pin is re-stated as the permanent structural truth it was always testing: **the panel grows by exactly one row per added registry entry**. The append-only ORDER pin, which is the real claim, is unweakened. **This is the seventh repoint the moving-reference lesson has cost — and the first where the moving reference was the phase's own successor rather than `HEAD`.**

**FULL REGRESSION, RUN TWICE CONSECUTIVELY: 97 files — RUN A 97 passed / 0 failed, RUN B 97 passed / 0 failed.** `test-starfield.js`'s intermittent §D flake (see `## Known issues`) did not reproduce in either run. No gameplay, physics, lever, registry, powerup or persistence assertion failed at any point in this session.

**CS025 P1 — THE MAGNET'S ATTRACTION IS INACTIVE WHILE THE TOW CHAIN IS FULL, AND RESUMES `DEBUG.magnetResumeDelay` (0.25 s) AFTER A SLOT OPENS.** Per `IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-CS025.md` P1 / `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS025.md` §1 and the `magnetResumeDelay` row of §5. **The defect:** at `game.cargoMax` the pickup gate blocks every hook, so a live Magnet drags garbage onto the ship where it has nowhere to go — and because garbage-to-garbage coalescence is a **separate system that keeps running**, that loitering cloud reaches `HUNTER_COALESCE_COUNT` and converts into a Hunter *at the player's own position*.

**(1) ONE NEW STATE FIELD WITH EXACTLY ONE WRITER, AND IT IS DERIVED — `game.magnetHoldT`.** At the top of `update()`'s pickup block: `const cargoFull = game.chain.length >= game.cargoMax;` re-arms the hold to the knob's value every full frame and counts it down on `dt` otherwise. **⛔ THE FIVE SITES THAT FREE A CARGO SLOT ARE DELIBERATELY NOT HOOKED** — `chain.pop()` at the dock, `breakChain`, `scatterChain`, `startGame`, and **`game.cargoMax = payloadSlots(game.wave)` in `nextWave()`**, where a level-up *grows* the cap so a full 8-load at L4 becomes a not-full 8-of-10 at L5 with **no delivery and no pickup at all**. Hooking five sites is five chances to miss one, and that fifth would have been missed; one per-frame read covers all five and cannot. Same derived-not-stored idiom as `saturatedClump()`. Declared in **both** the `game` literal and `startGame()`'s reset (the standing CS016 P3 rule).

**(2) `magnetPulling()` — A FUNCTION, NOT A LOCAL, and that is a requirement rather than a style choice:** CS025 P3's `Ship.draw()` needs the same answer and runs in `draw()`, which cannot see a local in `update()`. It is `powerActive("magnet") && game.magnetHoldT <= 0` — a pure read, no `dt`, no writes — and sits directly under `powerActive()` with the split documented at the site.

**(3) ⛔ THE WHOLE PHASE IS ONE NAME SPLIT INTO TWO, AND THE HALF THAT DOES **NOT** MOVE IS THE LOAD-BEARING HALF.** A single `const magnet = powerActive("magnet")` fed **three** consumers. Two move to `pulling` — the attraction force and the 1.6× `MAGNET_PICKUP_MULT` circle, which come back **together or not at all** (FORK-1). **The two budget spend sites keep reading `powerActive("magnet")` raw**, because during the resume window the *base* `GARBAGE_PICKUP` circle is still live: a piece drifting into it is genuinely hooked, and billing that hook against the suppressed predicate would make it **free** — turning "the magnet is inactive while full" into "the magnet gains **free uses** whenever you fill up", the exact opposite of the requirement. Both names are captured **once, above the loop**, exactly as the single `magnet` was: the loop itself spends magnet budget, so a per-piece re-read would let the pull switch off partway through the array and make a frame's behaviour depend on array order. §C of the new test is the discriminator that fails if anyone later tidies the two names back into one.

**(4) THE KNOB.** `magnetResumeDelay` joins the **POWERUPS** section after `engineMassMult` — `ms` shown / seconds consumed via `toNative: v => v / 1000`, the `autoShieldRegenPause`/`dockComboGrace` idiom. **No shipped constant backs it** (a resume delay has no meaning outside the knob), so the registry entry IS the source of truth for its default — the `chainGuardIntercepts` idiom. **Registry 72 → 73.** Persistence needed no code change at all: it is an ordinary `DEBUG_ENTRIES` row through the existing generic path. Not a lever (TRAP 3): no floor/ceil/steps triple, no `▼`/`↳`, no `carriesTo`, no `LEVERS` entry.

**⛔ TWO CONSEQUENCES OF THE PRESCRIBED ONE-WRITER FORM, BOTH FLAGGED HERE RATHER THAN "FIXED" — inventing a different expression would have been inventing design, and both are pinned in the test rather than left to be rediscovered.** **(a) The knob at 0 turns the WHOLE feature off, not just the delay.** The writer arms `magnetHoldT` to *this value* on every full-cargo frame, so at 0 it arms to 0 and `magnetPulling()` reads true even while full. That makes the knob the clean A/B for the gate — set 0 and the build behaves exactly as CS024 shipped — but it is not "how long the hold outlives a freed slot" alone. **(b) There is a ONE-FRAME LAG, and it is CS025 P3's problem, not a defect here.** The writer is read at the *top* of the pickup block, so on the single frame where a hook *fills* the chain, that frame legitimately began not-full: the pull was live for it, and the hold is only re-armed by the next frame's writer. Anything sampling `magnetPulling()` *between* `update()` calls — which is exactly where `draw()` runs — sees "pulling" for one frame at full cargo. Harmless for the mechanic; **P3's scoop-energy tell reads this predicate from `draw()`, so its glow will flicker on for one frame at the moment the chain fills.** P3 should decide that deliberately. Fixing it would require a second writer or a hook at the pickup sites, which is precisely what §1.3 forbids.

**Nothing else moved.** `powerActive()` itself, `game.powerBudget`, the HUD, the `expire_magnet` falling-edge latch, `game.powerVoiced`, banking, `POWERUP_DROP_TYPES` and every `MAGNET_*` constant are untouched — suppression is completely invisible to the powerup machinery, and §F pins that both behaviourally and structurally. `GAME_VERSION` stays `"1.0.0.24"` (TRAP 1). No design doc touched (TRAP 2) — `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS025.md` §1 already carried this phase's spec, and §5's `DIFFICULTY-LEVERS.md` not-a-lever row is **P5's** to write, per that phase's own item (5). New test `scratchpad/test-cs025-p1.js` (370 assertions, eight sections A–H). **⛔ Every "nothing else moved" claim in it is written against this phase's OWN PARENT COMMIT (`2cd73e8`), never against `HEAD` (TRAP 4)** — and its doc pin goes one step further, resolving this phase's *own commit* by subject and diffing `parent..commit`, so it is a fixed statement about history that P5's doc sweep cannot falsify.

The standing "grep, don't trust the list" rule held again, and this time the interesting failures were the ones the prompt could not have listed.** The count (72 → 73) moved in `test-cs018-p4/p6/p7`, `test-cs020-p1/p1b`, `test-cs023-p2/p3`, `test-cs024-p1/p2/p3/p4/p5/p6/p6b/p6c/p6f` — every one still asserts what its own phase claimed. The five substantive ones:

**(a) `test-v31-coalesce.js` (34) — A REAL INTERACTION, AND THE ONLY ONE THAT WAS ABOUT GAMEPLAY.** That test staged `game.cargoMax = 0` to block the pickup gate so a magnet-pulled piece would travel all the way in instead of hooking mid-flight. Under the new rule `chain.length (0) >= cargoMax (0)` is **true**, so an empty chain at a zero cap is FULL and the attraction it measures was suppressed — the piece never moved and the 30 s cap expired. **The semantics are right (no capacity means no room) and unreachable in a real run (`payloadSlots` never returns less than 8); the staging trick is what broke.** Repointed by measuring further out instead: `arriveAt` 20 → 40 px, outside the magnet-widened pickup radius (18 × 1.6 = 28.8), so nothing is hooked mid-measurement and the gate needs no blocking. Both builds are measured at the same 40 px, so the comparison the test exists to make is unchanged (new 1.08 s vs old-build 2.07 s).

**(b) `test-cs017-p6.js` §A + `test-cs019-p1.js` §A — THE `breakChain` CALL-SITE PIN, WHOSE OWN COMMENT PREDICTED THIS.** `test-cs019-p1.js` recorded that the regex is *"brittle by construction: any NEW comment containing the substring `breakChain(` inflates it (CS017 P7 tripped exactly this)"* — and this phase's derived-not-hooked note, which names all five slot-freeing sites in prose, tripped it a second time. **Both now strip `//` comment lines before counting, so the pin measures CALL SITES rather than occurrences of a substring** (the same line-based fix CS024 P6f used for its `LARGE_HUNTER_MAX` pin). **The `- definitions` subtraction went with them, and why is worth recording: the lookbehind already excluded `function breakChain(`, so that term was never removing the definition — it was silently cancelling the one doc-comment mention, and the two errors happened to agree at 2.** Strip the comments and the raw count IS the call-site count. Still 2.

**(c) `test-cs024-p6b.js` §G TRAP 5 — `powerActive` LEAVES THE "NO DIFF LINE MENTIONS IT" LIST AND GAINS A SHARPER PIN.** Same reason `engineBurnSeconds` left it in P7: a fixed-ref diff pin measured against a **moving working tree** cannot outlive a later phase legitimately adding a **reader**. `magnetPulling()` is declared directly under `powerActive()` and is *defined* as a call to it, so every added line mentions the symbol without disturbing it. What P6b actually promised is that the predicate itself was left alone, so that is what is checked now: **no line REMOVES it** (a body edit always produces one) **and no line redefines it**. A new caller is allowed; a redefinition is not. Its registry-ORDER pin was repointed the ordinary way (strip `magnetResumeDelay`, as it already strips `startLevel`, `debugOverride` and P6f's three).

**(d) `test-cs024-p6c.js` — two more count literals the first grep pass missed** (`Object.keys(debugShown).length`, and the non-lever knob census 21 → 22), both found by running the suite rather than by reading the list.

**CS024 P6f — AN IN-ROUND CORRECTIVE PHASE OUT OF GATE B'S OWN PLAYTEST: TOO MANY LARGE HUNTERS CAN EXIST AT LEVELS 1–3.** Per `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS024.md` §2.5. **Why the obvious fix was the wrong one:** slowing `coalescePause` governs how fast **one** clump forms while nothing limits how many form **concurrently** — every satellite's garbage lineage marches toward 12 on its own independent clock. It is a **count** problem, so the fix is count-shaped.

**(1) `LARGE_HUNTER_MAX = 100` IS DELETED AND THE CEILING SCALES.** Its own comment called it "a runaway backstop that play should never reach" — and play never did, which is precisely the defect. `largeHunterCap(wave) = min(ceil(wave / DEBUG.hunterCapLevelsPerStep), DEBUG.hunterCapMax)` — **1 large Hunter at levels 1–2, one more every two levels, plateauing at 6 from level 11.** Two new HUNTER-section knobs: **`hunterCapMax`** (def 6, 1–24) and **`hunterCapLevelsPerStep`** (def 2, 1–8). **⛔ NOT A LEVER AND MUST NEVER BECOME ONE** — no floor/ceil/steps triple, no `▼`/`↳`, no `carriesTo`, no `LEVERS` entry: a ceiling on concurrent threats is a **stability guarantee, not a difficulty axis**, and the player should never feel it move. It is a pure function of `game.wave` like everything else, but it lives **outside the odometer** (recorded in §2.5's not-a-lever list). **⛔ ALSO NOT A BREAKPOINT TABLE** — CS024 P3 deleted `HUNTER_CAP_STEPS` (11 breakpoints, 0→12) and this must not quietly restore that shape: two knobs and one `ceil`, nothing more. No constant backs the two defaults, deliberately — a per-level ceiling has no shipped-scalar meaning, so the registry entries ARE the source of truth (the `chainGuardIntercepts` idiom).

**(2) THE OVERFLOW RULE REVERSES AGAIN — THREE ARMS, NOT TWO — AND P3's REASONING IS REWRITTEN IN PLACE RATHER THAN DELETED.** P3 made a saturated clump **destroy itself** at a full cap, arguing that with decay gone a held clump would occupy 12 pieces the pipeline could never reclaim. **⛔ THAT ARGUMENT IS WRONG, AND EXACTLY WHERE IT FAILS IS THE VALUABLE PART: it assumed a held clump has no reclamation path. It has two, and both are the player's — a held clump is still garbage, so it can be SCOOPED (partially or wholly) and SHATTERED with a bullet.** Scooping a 12-piece clump for a single large payload is the game's own thesis paying out, and is the removed bonus canister's role arriving emergently instead of by dice roll. New rule: **cap has room → convert** (unchanged); **cap full and fewer than `DEBUG.heldClumpMax` saturated clumps → HOLD** (no `hunterCoalesced`, no `noteLargeHunterSpawn()`, no `AudioSys.hunterborn()` — nothing was born); **cap full AND `heldClumpMax` already held → destroy exactly as P3 does**, `boom()` in the garbage hue, `awardScore = false` semantics. New knob **`heldClumpMax`** (def 4, 1–20) is the anti-stall backstop, sized against `GARBAGE_SOFT_MAX` (220): unbounded holding would let ~18 clumps consume the whole garbage budget and starve the field.

**(3) ⛔ "HELD" IS A DERIVED CONDITION, NEVER A STORED FLAG — `saturatedClump(g)` is `!g.dead && g.pieces >= HUNTER_COALESCE_COUNT`, and it has THREE consumers reading the ONE condition** (the conversion branch, the cull exemption, the visual tell). Two shipped paths change a clump's size *after* it saturates and a flag would survive both: **scooping is PARTIAL** (`take = Math.min(room, g.pieces)`, so a held 12-clump met with 5 free chain slots gives up 5 and survives at 7) and **a bullet SHATTERS** any `pieces > 1` clump. Either would leave a 7-piece clump still marked as a pending Hunter. Derived, there is no state to clear and no way to desync — pinned directly, including a partial scoop through the real intake and a shatter, both proving the clump behaves as ordinary salvage on the very same frame.

**(4) A SATURATED CLUMP STOPS ABSORBING — the pair is skipped on BOTH sides in `coalesceGarbage()`, attraction AND merge.** Otherwise a held clump grows into a 40-piece blob that still converts to exactly one Hunter and wastes 28 pieces. **The consequence is accepted and is not a bug: loose singles near a held clump sit there rather than merging into it** — even one sitting exactly on top of it. They are still scoopable and still shootable.

**(5) `drainHeldClumps()` — THE QUEUE DRAIN, a new end-of-frame pass in `update()`'s cleanup block.** `coalesceGarbage()`'s pair walk only touches clumps near each other, so a held clump sitting alone is never re-examined and would hold forever after a Hunter died. The drain converts the **oldest by `age`** (ties by array index, strict `>`), repeating while slots remain — **it can convert several in one frame**, because one event can free several slots at once. **Conversion through it is a REAL birth**: `noteLargeHunterSpawn()`, `hunterCoalesced++`, `hunterborn()`, all of it. It sits **before** `cullGarbage()` and **after** every pass that can mark a Hunter dead — both directions load-bearing (a large destroyed this frame frees its slot immediately, since `largeHunterCount()` excludes dead-but-unfiltered hunters). `noteLargeHunterSpawn()` therefore has **two** callers now, not one — the same producer at two moments — and every birth still funnels through the one 0→1 helper.

**⛔ ONE DECISION THE PHASE PROMPT DID NOT NAME, MADE DELIBERATELY AND FLAGGED HERE: `drainHeldClumps()` HONOURS `game.sweepPause`.** CS018 P9 set that pause specifically because a Super Mega Delivery turns every large into mediums, freeing the **whole** ceiling at once, and fresh larges converting into the fragment swarm is exactly what it exists to prevent. A drain ignoring it would reintroduce that regression in a new code path. The spec's own requirement — "must be able to convert more than one in a frame — a Super Mega Delivery's sweep can free several slots at once" — is untouched: the slots are still all free when the pause lifts, so several still convert in the first frame after it does, and the test pins both halves.

**(6) TWO SMALLER RULES.** **Held clumps are exempt from the `GARBAGE_SOFT_MAX` cull** — culling a pending Hunter out from under the player would be invisible and arbitrary (the cull is silent by design, so there would be no tell at all). They still **count** toward `live` (they genuinely occupy the field), they are simply never eligible as victims; `heldClumpMax` is what bounds them instead. Both cull paths gained an empty-pool guard — the soft path returns when no victim is eligible, the hard path takes `Math.min(n, order.length)` rather than indexing off the end. **And the held state has a visual tell**, because it is a new persistent condition with no precedent: before this phase `pieces >= 12` either converted or vanished in the same frame and was never a state anything could *draw*. `Garbage.drawHeldRing()` — a full ring at `radius + HELD_CLUMP_RING_PAD` (6, the retired `BONUS_RING_PAD`'s role and value) in **`COLOR.satellite`, the Hunter's own hue on a body drawn in the salvage hue**, pulsing at the flat `HUD_PULSE_HZ` non-HP alarm rate (`drawHUD`'s cargo-gold expression, verbatim). Stroke only, through the shared `drawRingArc`/`glowStroke` path — no new fill exception. Size was already spoken for as the nearing-the-transform tell, which is why the tell needed its own two channels.

**THE DIFFICULTY LOG'S `maxLargeHunters` COLUMN IS PER-LEVEL AGAIN**, reading the same `largeHunterCap(game.wave)` its consumers read — "a column follows its consumer," so a `hunterCapMax`/`hunterCapLevelsPerStep` retune moves the CSV exactly as it moves the game.

**Registry 69 → 72** (18 → 21 non-lever knobs; the 51 lever rows are untouched, and the nine section headers are unchanged — no new section). All three new knobs carry `clampShown: v => Math.round(v)`, which is what makes "integers" structural rather than conventional: the arrows already land on whole numbers, so the hook exists for typed entry, the same job it does on the 17 lever `Steps` rows. `GAME_VERSION` untouched at `"1.0.0.22"` (TRAP 1). No `LEVERS` edit, no `leverState` change — pinned byte-identical to HEAD and identical in output at every level 1..200, every lever (TRAP 2). No design doc touched (TRAP 6) — `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS024.md` §2.5 already carried this phase's spec, including both new entries, before the session started. New test `scratchpad/test-cs024-p6f.js` (13,187 assertions, twelve sections A–L).

**REPOINT SWEEP — 15 existing test files.** Most were the registry count (69 → 72): `test-cs018-p6/p7`, `test-cs020-p1/p1b`, `test-cs023-p2/p3`, `test-cs024-p1/p2/p5/p6/p6b/p6c`. The substantive ones are the four that pinned the deleted constant or the reversed rule. **`test-cs024-p3.js` §F** — the phase whose central rule this one reverses — keeps every claim it ever made (`awardScore = false`, the `boom()` as the only tell, the ceiling never exceeded) and simply stages the held queue to `heldClumpMax` first so the DESTROY arm is the one under test; its "no stall" block now proves the bound (`<= heldClumpMax`) and **exercises the reclamation path P3 argued did not exist**, shattering a held clump and counting all twelve pieces back onto the field. **`test-cs018-p4.js`** likewise re-reverses its §E hold/destroy expectation, turns `levelForCap(n)` back into a genuine search (over the closed form, never a table), and narrows its `noteLargeHunterSpawn` census from one caller to two. **`test-cs024-p4.js`** and **`test-cs018-p4.js` §D** both pinned `largeHunterCap` as *deleted*: **narrowed, not dropped** — the identifier is live again, but what P3/P4 actually deleted was the per-level TABLE LOOKUP, and `HUNTER_CAP_STEPS` staying absent is what pins that half now (plus a new pin that `LARGE_HUNTER_MAX` is gone in its turn). `test-cs017-p2.js` and `test-v31-coalesce.js` follow the same repoint. **All 95 test files pass** except one pre-existing, unrelated failure: `test-cs024-p6b.js`'s TRAP 6 (a fixed-ref doc-diff pin against commit `79222e5`, predating legitimate later doc updates) — confirmed present on a clean `git stash` of this phase's own changes, the same disposition P6d and P6e both recorded.

**CS024 P6e — ANOTHER IN-ROUND CORRECTIVE PHASE, NOT IN THE ORIGINAL PLAN: GATE TOOLING, NOT A LEVER.** Per `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS024.md` §4.5. By P6c the registry carried 51 lever rows and there was no way to undo a debug edit short of clearing `localStorage` — an evening of dragging sliders was unrecoverable. Four additions, all confined to the debug screen and its apply path; no gameplay change, no `LEVERS` edit, no reshaped knob semantics.

**(1) `r` RESETS THE SELECTED ROW TO ITS REGISTRY DEFAULT.** `applyDebug(e.id, e.def)` — one uniform rule, **no special-casing** for a lever row, a `toNative` row or a `clampShown` row (`applyDebug` already applies those hooks identically regardless of how the value arrived). `r` is inert while a numeric entry is pending (a stray press can't fight the entry buffer) and a no-op on a header/action/Back row (nothing selected there). Confirmed free by grep before binding: CS024 P1 removed the only other raw `r` capture on this screen (the retired orbit start-angle reroll), and the numeric-entry hook only ever claims `DEBUG_ENTRY_CHARS` (`[-.0-9]`) + Backspace, never a bare letter — `r` legitimately means something different now, and the two are unrelated.

**(2 & 4) TWO NEW ACTION ROWS, NOT HIDDEN KEYS — "Reset all debug knobs to defaults" and "Reset saved scores," both reached by scrolling like any row and fired via `openModal`** (the Controls screen's "Reset all controls to defaults?" precedent, exactly). Reset All calls `resetAllDebug()` — the SAME function the module-level seed loop calls (extracted, not duplicated), which walks `DEBUG_ENTRIES` and `applyDebug`s every one back to its own `def`, override toggle included. Reset Saved Scores is a SEPARATE confirm with distinct wording, clears `HighScores.entries` **and** calls `HighScores.save()` (never a raw `removeItem` — the same guarded write path every other store uses) and clears `game.lastScoreId` so the gameover table can't highlight a now-dead id. **It is irreversible and touches `afd_scores_v1` ONLY** — `afd_achievements_v2` (lifetime tiers, weekly, unlocks) is never referenced by the wipe path, by design (not asked for). The action row itself is named "Reset saved scores," not "Reset high scores" — deliberately, to avoid tripping CS018 P2's structural "no low/normal/high identifier in executable panel code" pin, which matches on the bare word "high" anywhere in `menuDebug`'s source, comments stripped.

**(3) A MASTER OVERRIDE TOGGLE — `debugOverride`, a boolean row at the very TOP of the registry, default ON.** This is the one structural change: **`DEBUG` is now DERIVED, not co-written.** `debugShown` stays the edited, persisted store exactly as before; `DEBUG` is rebuilt from either `debugShown[id]` (toggle on) or `e.def` (toggle off) via a shared `debugNative(e, shown, on)` rule. `applyDebug` derives one field per ordinary edit (arrow, typed commit, `r`); writing the toggle itself calls the new `rebuildDebug()`, which re-derives all ~69 fields at once — that split is deliberate, not an optimization: `rebuildDebug()` only runs on the one edit that actually changes what every other row derives to. **Toggling off never touches `debugShown`** — every edit is preserved silently, so toggling back on restores the whole panel exactly as it stood. No consumer site changed: all ~70 read sites still read `DEBUG.x` and do not care where the value came from. Persisted additively in `afd_settings_v1.debug`, same generic `DEBUG_ENTRIES` round-trip every other knob already uses — no code change needed in `saveSettings`/`loadSettings` at all, since the toggle is just one more entry in the same registry. **⛔ THE TIMING LIMIT IS REAL: levers are read at the point of use** (next wave's spawn, next saucer's construction; a `Garbage` grabs its coalesce delay in its constructor), so flipping the toggle mid-wave does not change entities already on screen — **a clean A/B happens only between waves.** The panel shows the state two ways: the title bar reads "DEBUG OPTIONS — OVERRIDES OFF" while off, and every OTHER row's value column dims (the toggle row itself stays lit).

**Registry count 68 → 69** (17 → 18 non-lever knobs — `debugOverride` joins `startLevel`; both are flat, chainless knobs, one at the very top, one in GLOBAL). **DEBUG_ROWS trailer grew from 2 to 4** — Dump, Reset All, Reset Saved Scores, Back, in that order. New test `scratchpad/test-cs024-p6e.js` (204 assertions): `r` across a lever row/toNative row/clampShown row and its typing-guard; Reset All's identity with the module seed (regex-pinned: one loop, not two) plus full-registry restoration; the toggle's on/off/on round trip proving `debugShown` is untouched while off; `clampShown`-before-`toNative` order preserved through the derived path (via a synthetic registry entry carrying both hooks — no shipped row has both); the toggle's persistence round-trip; an untouched, overrides-ON panel byte-identical to HEAD (`DEBUG`/`leverState` at every id/level checked); and the score wipe emptying both the in-memory array and the stored key while leaving `afd_achievements_v2` provably byte-identical, plus `qualifies()` answering correctly immediately after.

**REPOINT SWEEP (the registry reshape touches old pinned tests, same as every prior registry-shape phase):** this phase's own repoint sweep updated pinned entry/row counts (68→69, the `+2`/`+1` trailer-row arithmetic → `+4`) across 19 existing test files (`test-cs015-p4/p5`, `test-cs017-p2/p6`, `test-cs018-p2/p4/p6/p7`, `test-cs019-p1`, `test-cs020-p1/p1b`, `test-cs023-p2/p3`, `test-cs024-p1/p2/p3/p4/p5/p6/p6b/p6c`) — every one of them still asserts what its own phase claimed, only the count/index literal moved. Two of those files (`test-cs017-p6.js`, `test-cs019-p1.js`, plus `test-cs015-p5.js` and CS018 P2's own file) pinned the registry as **strictly append-only** ("P4's/CS015's entry is still the first VALUE entry") — that claim is now **narrowed, not dropped**: the registry is append-only for every knob EXCEPT this phase's own toggle, which is deliberately inserted at the top by spec (§3), so those pins now assert `debugOverride` is first and the old anchor entry is second. `test-cs024-p1.js`'s "no `r` keybind at all" TRAP was similarly narrowed to "no orbit-reroll logic reachable from `r`" — P1 removed the reroll, P6e legitimately reuses the letter for an unrelated purpose. **All test files pass** except one pre-existing, unrelated failure: `test-cs024-p6b.js`'s TRAP 6 (a fixed-ref doc-diff pin against commit `79222e5`, predating legitimate P6c/P6d doc updates) — confirmed present on a clean `git stash` of this phase's own changes, so it is not something P6e introduced or is in scope to fix (same disposition CS024 P6d recorded for `test-cs024-p6b.js`'s TRAP 6 last round).

**CS024 P6d — GATE-TOOLING DEBUG KNOB, LANDED PRIOR ROUND.** Per `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS024.md` §4.5. Gate B needs to sample a deep level without playing thirty levels to reach it, and nothing in the build could jump the level counter — this phase gives it one knob. **`startLevel`** joins the registry's GLOBAL section (def 1, min 1, max 99, step 1) — a **plain flat knob**, not a lever: no `▼`/`↳` glyph, no `(inv)`, no floor/ceil/steps triple, no `LEVERS` entry. `startGame()` seeds `game.wave = DEBUG.startLevel - 1`, so `nextWave()` lands the first level on exactly the knob's value; everything downstream (`leverState`, `payloadSlots`, `musicIntensity`) was already a pure function of `game.wave`, so nothing else needed to change — this was verified, not assumed (`scratchpad/test-cs024-p6d.js` §B pins `leverState(N)` identical whether reached by seed or by play, at N = 1, 2, 9, 33, 60, 99).

**⛔ THE FIDELITY LIMIT IS REAL AND DELIBERATELY UNFIXED: a run started at level N gives a level-N FIELD with a level-1 SHIP.** No scoop upgrades, no banked powerups, no accumulated garbage, no towed load — it is a spawn-side sampling tool, not a simulation of having played there. Documented at the knob itself (a comment on the registry entry) so a future session doesn't mistake it for more than it is. **Explicitly not "fixed" by seeding scoop level or powerups** — that would have been unrequested design, and the phase prompt said so directly.

**A RUN STARTED ABOVE LEVEL 1 CANNOT RECORD, AND THE MECHANISM IS A STICKY FLAG READ ONCE.** `game.debugRun = DEBUG.startLevel > 1` is set exactly once, inside `startGame()`, from the knob's value at that instant — `DEBUG.startLevel` is never re-read for the rest of the run, so dragging the slider back to 1 mid-run cannot silently re-arm recording for a run that started at 33, and dragging it up mid-run cannot retroactively void a legitimate one (`test-cs024-p6d.js` §F pins both directions). Three **persistence points** are gated on it, in-memory state left alone so the HUD/gameover screen/toasts behave exactly as they would in a real run (§G): (1) the initials-entry screen (`game.entry`) never arms on a debug run regardless of score, so `HighScores.add()` — reachable only through `commitEntry()` — is never called; (2) `Achievements.save()` is the single choke point both the achievement-unlock commit (`onUnlock`) and the periodic lifetime-stats flush (`tick`) already funneled through, so gating it once inside the function covers both named persistence points at their one real call site. **A visible tell** — `"DEBUG RUN"` in `COLOR.dim`, centered top of the HUD, reflows nothing — so a good score silently failing to record is never baffling (`drawHUD()`, gated on `game.debugRun`, ahead of the score readout).

**KNOWN, ACCEPTED CONSEQUENCE OF "GATE THE PERSISTENCE POINTS, NOT THE IN-MEMORY COUNTERS" (the phase prompt's own instruction, not a guess this session made):** `Achievements.lifetime.*` counters (e.g. `maxWave`) DO advance in memory during a debug run, same as a real one — a debug run reaching level 40 really does raise `lifetime.maxWave` to 40 in memory. That write is never flushed to storage *during* the debug run, but the tainted in-memory value persists until the process reloads, so if the very next game (a real one) triggers a save before a reload, the debug run's high-water mark rides along in that legitimate save. `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS024.md` §4.5 asks for exactly this shape ("gate the three persistence points, not the in-memory counters" — this file's own wording, verbatim, in the phase prompt), so it is not treated as a bug to silently fix; noted here in case Gate B surfaces it as a confusing lifetime-stat jump.

`GAME_VERSION` untouched at `"1.0.0.22"` (TRAP 1). No lever added, no `LEVERS` edit (TRAP 3). No design doc touched (TRAP 5). New test `scratchpad/test-cs024-p6d.js` (177 assertions). **Registry count 67 → 68** (16 → 17 non-lever knobs) — this phase's own repoint sweep touched 14 existing test files' pinned counts/order strings (`test-cs018-p4/p6/p7`, `test-cs020-p1/p1b`, `test-cs023-p2/p3`, `test-cs024-p1/p2/p3/p4/p5/p6`, `test-cs024-p6b`, `test-cs024-p6c`) the same way CS024 P6c's own sweep did when it went 33 → 67 — every one of them still asserts what its own phase claimed, only the registry-count literal moved. **All test files pass** except one pre-existing, unrelated failure: `test-cs024-p6b.js`'s TRAP 6 (a fixed-ref doc-diff pin against a commit that predates later, legitimate doc updates from P6/P6c) — confirmed present on a clean `git stash` of this phase's own changes, so it is not something P6d introduced or is in scope to fix.

**CS024 P6c — AN IN-ROUND CORRECTIVE PHASE, NOT IN THE ORIGINAL PLAN. EVERY LEVER NOW HAS THREE KNOBS INSTEAD OF ONE, AND GATE B CAN ACTUALLY TUNE A RAMP.** Per `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS024.md` §2.6, rewritten for this phase. **What was wrong:** P5's instruction — "one knob per lever" — was incoherent, because **a lever is not a value.** Its shipped quantity is derived from a floor, a ceiling, a step count and `game.wave`, so the single row P5 shipped could do only one thing when touched: **pin that lever to a flat constant at every level.** It could not move the ramp's floor, its ceiling or its period; and because its `min`/`max` came from `Math.min`/`Math.max` of the shipped pair, it could not move an endpoint **past** its partner either — on the seven INVERTED levers that meant a ceiling could never be raised above where it already sat. Gate B's entire job is tuning ramps.

**(1) 51 ROWS, THREE PER LEVER — `<leverId>Floor`, `<leverId>Ceil`, `<leverId>Steps`.** `leverKnob()` returns them as an ARRAY, spread into `DEBUG_VARS`. Moving any of the three re-derives that lever's **whole ramp immediately, at every level**. **The flat pin is SUBSUMED, not lost:** setting Floor equal to Ceil pins the lever to a constant at every level, which is exactly what the retired row did — there is deliberately no fourth row for it. **Registry 33 → 67** (16 non-lever knobs untouched + 51). Section order and the nine headers are unchanged; P6's POWERUPS rows are untouched.

**(2) `leverState()` IS SPLIT, AND PURITY IS INTACT — THE SPLIT IS WHAT MAKES THE KNOBS POSSIBLE.** The arithmetic moved into **`leverValues(table, wave)`** (same closed form, taking the table it walks as an argument), and `leverState(wave)` is that applied to the shipped `LEVERS`. The panel's knobs go in through **`liveLevers(wave)`**, which is `leverValues` over a DEBUG-overridden copy of the table, and which lives **outside** the sliced odometer section because it reads `DEBUG` and `leverState` must not. **A table parameter rather than P5's `DEBUG.x ?? lv.x` at the consumers, and the reason is structural, not stylistic:** a Steps override changes a driver's **wrap period**, which changes how many carries every dependent under it receives, so it cannot be expressed as a post-hoc override of `leverState`'s OUTPUT — it has to go in before the derivation runs, and the only way in is the table. The bare-context slice test passes **unmodified**; `function leverState(wave) {` is byte-identical as a line, which is what three phases of slice tests find by prefix.

**⛔ `leverState()` NOW HAS NO IN-GAME CALLER, AND THAT IS DELIBERATE — DO NOT DELETE IT ON A CLEANUP PASS.** All twelve consumer call sites read `liveLevers`. `leverState` is what `liveLevers` is *defined against*: the odometer's pure statement of the shipped ramp, the bare-context surface the headless tests evaluate on its own, and the reference the untouched-panel invariant is pinned to (an untouched registry must make `liveLevers(w) === leverState(w)` at every level). It says so at the site.

**(3) THE RANGES SPAN BOTH DIRECTIONS.** Floor and Ceil share one range, widened by a **full span on each side** of the shipped pair (clamped at 0, since every levered quantity is a non-negative count/speed/interval/angle), so either endpoint can be dragged past the other. `coalescePause`'s pair now sliders over [0, 8.5] where P5 gave it [1.5, 5]. **Nothing asserts or clamps `floor <= ceil`, in the panel any more than in the table** — `Math.min`/`Math.max` appear in `leverKnob` only to find the shipped pair's extent for that widening, and the test pins that as the single such line in the build.

**(4) `junkCount` IS ROUNDED AT THE CONSUMER, AND THIS IS NEW.** It is the one integer-valued lever — everything else is a speed, an interval, a multiplier or an angle. At the shipped 3/12/10 every step lands on a whole number, so nothing had ever broken; **the Steps knob breaks it** (3/12/**7** interpolates to 3, 4.5, 6, 7.5, 9, 10.5, 12). `nextWave()` now spawns `Math.round(lv.junkCount)`. **Round, not floor, deliberately:** it is the nearest achievable count to the authored curve (off by at most half a satellite rather than up to a whole one), it returns both authored endpoints exactly when they are whole, it stays monotonic, and flooring would shave every interior step of every retune downward — a systematic bias making mid-ramp levels quietly easier than the number on the slider while the difficulty log lied by a full satellite in the same direction every time. Rounding at `nextWave()` rather than inside `spawnFieldSatellites()` is what keeps the log honest: it is handed the same local.

**(5) THE PANEL SHOWS THE CHAIN, NOT JUST THE ROWS — AND THE MARKERS ARE DERIVED FROM `LEVERS`, NEVER TYPED.** A **driver** wears `▼` and sits unindented ("everything indented below moves when this one wraps"); a **dependent** wears a two-space `↳`; an **inverted** lever wears ` (inv)` on all three of its rows, so a Floor numerically above its Ceil reads as correct rather than as a bug at 2am; a **non-lever knob** wears neither glyph nor indent, because it belongs to no chain. `everyNLevels` decides the first, `floor > ceil` the second — both read off the table inside `leverKnob()`, so a future table edit cannot desync the panel from the mechanism (the test proves the derivation by mutating a lever's `everyNLevels` and flipping a floor/ceil, and watching the labels follow).

**LABEL WIDTH IS A REAL CONSTRAINT AND IT COST THE LEVER LABELS THEIR LONG NAMES.** `drawDebug` neither wraps nor truncates, and the label column is **~32 monospace characters** at 18px before it reaches a selected row's `◄` chevron (documented at `DEBUG_VALUE_X` and asserted in the test). The glyphs, the indent and ` (inv)` cost up to 18 of those on their own, so the BASE labels were shortened rather than the markers dropped: the ten UFO levers dropped their "UFO " prefix (their header already says it), sizes became bare `lg`/`md`/`sm`, and `Coalescence inert delay` became `Coalesce delay`. JUNK and HUNTER kept their prefixes because both own a "speed md"/"speed sm" pair and the prefix is the only thing telling those apart once the header has scrolled off the window. Longest label in the registry: 31 characters.

**(6) THE `def: null` "auto" SENTINEL IS RETIRED, AND SO IS THE DESIGN QUESTION P5 FLAGGED.** A lever's VALUE varies per level and had no honest default; a floor, a ceiling and a step count are plain constants that do, so every one of the 51 rows ships with the shipped table field as its `def`. That makes the untouched-panel guarantee **sharper** than the sentinel did — an untouched registry reproduces `LEVERS` field for field, pinned at every level 1..200 against `leverState` and against HEAD — and it retired both null special-cases the sentinel needed (`menuDebug`'s nudge base, `drawDebug`'s `"auto"` string), which went with it. **P5's open question ("does pinning a lever flat on touch feel right?") no longer needs an answer**: pinning flat is now one specific thing you can choose (Floor = Ceil), not the only thing a slider can do.

**`GAME_VERSION` untouched at `"1.0.0.22"`. No design doc touched.** New test `scratchpad/test-cs024-p6c.js` (1,246 assertions). **The repoint sweep this caused, reported in full — 16 existing test files:** `test-cs015-p5`, `test-cs018-p3`, `test-cs018-p4`, `test-cs018-p6`, `test-cs018-p7`, `test-cs020-p1`, `test-cs020-p1b`, `test-cs023-p2`, `test-cs023-p3`, `test-cs024-p1`, `test-cs024-p2`, `test-cs024-p3`, `test-cs024-p4`, `test-cs024-p5`, `test-cs024-p6`, `test-cs024-p6b`. Most were the registry count (33 → 67); the substantive ones were `test-cs024-p4` §H (its `leverState` call-site census becomes a `liveLevers` one, and `leverState` drops to exactly one appearance — its own declaration), `test-cs024-p5` §G/§G2 (the whole lever-knob shape), and `test-cs024-p6b` §G, whose **TRAP 5 hunk-range pin had to be retired**: it required every hunk of the diff against the pre-P6b build to fall inside the odometer section's line range, which a later phase touching the registry necessarily falsifies. Its symbol-level powerup checks survive, and its registry-order claim was repointed rather than dropped — the three-rows-per-lever split is collapsed back to one id per lever before the comparison, so the order claim is still made against `79222e5`. **All 92 test files pass.**

**CS024 P6b — AN IN-ROUND CORRECTIVE PHASE, NOT IN THE ORIGINAL PLAN. IT LANDS THE FORK-CS025-A AMENDMENT P4 WAS SUPPOSED TO CARRY AND DID NOT, PLUS THE UFO STEP-COUNT RESTAGE THAT DEPENDS ON IT.** Per `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS025.md` §1 and §2. **What was wrong:** `leverState()` shipped with unrestricted carry semantics — a carried lever that itself declared `carriesTo` wrapped like any other — so `ufoFlightSpeedSmall` climbed 150 → 210 px/s by level 25 and then **reset to 150 at level 33**, a UFO genuinely slower at 33 than at 25 on one of the most legible quantities in the game, while the second-generation levers (`ufoDirChange*`, `ufoShotSpeed*`, `ufoAccuracySmall`) moved twice in 64 levels and did not reach their ceilings until level 97. **(1) THE RULE: a lever may declare `carriesTo` only if it also declares `everyNLevels`.** Every carried lever plateaus at its ceiling; there is no second carry generation. **(2) The closed form collapses** — a dependent's carry count is `floor((wave - 1) / (everyN × steps))` of its driver, computed directly. `leverState()` is now **two flat passes** (seed the drivers' clocks; sweep every carry) with no recursion, no iteration to a fixed point, and **no ordering requirement at all**. **(3) The guard is REPLACED, not deleted** — a cycle being unreachable by construction, the Kahn sort and its `"contains a cycle"` throw are gone and a stronger, cheaper **legality check** stands in their place: `carriesTo` without `everyNLevels`, or naming an unknown id, throws at load (the duplicate-id check stays — `raw` is still keyed by id). Still an **invariant in the `SCOOP_WIDTH[0] !== 0` idiom, and it says so at the site.** **(4) The UFO table is restaged** — `ufoAppearFreq` stays the driver (25 → 12, 8 steps, every level) and carries **directly to all nine** other UFO levers; neither flight-speed lever carries any more. **Floors and ceilings are unchanged; only `steps` moved.** `GAME_VERSION` untouched; no design doc touched.

**THE STAGGER IS THE DESIGN, AND THE UNEVENNESS IS THE POINT — DO NOT "TIDY" THESE INTO ONE NUMBER.** Speed first (`ufoFlightSpeedBig`/`Small`, 5 steps, ceil at **L33**), then rate of fire (`ufoFireFreqBig`/`Small`, 6, **L41**), then evasiveness (`ufoDirChangeBig`/`Small`, 7, **L49**), then shot velocity (`ufoShotSpeedBig`/`Small`, 8, **L57**), and **accuracy last** (`ufoAccuracySmall`, 9, **L65**) — the most lethal quantity in the chain creeping in over the longest span in the smallest per-carry increments (9 steps across 22° of aim error is **~2.75° per carry**). The UFOs get *faster* before they get *accurate*, deliberately. Plotted: at L25 the small UFO is 195 px/s with 21.75° of error; at L33, 210 px/s and 19°; at L65, 210 px/s and 8°. **`ufoAppearFreq` still cycles forever and never permanently tightens** — level 100 reads exactly as level 1. That is deliberate and must not be "fixed": it is the chain's driver, and a driver that stopped cycling would freeze all nine levers under it. UFO *pressure* escalates through those nine; UFO *rhythm* stays constant.

**THE JUNK AND HUNTER CHAINS ARE BYTE-IDENTICAL IN OUTPUT, AND THAT IS PROVEN AGAINST THE PRE-P6b BUILD RATHER THAN ARGUED.** Their dependents already had empty `carriesTo` lists, so the rule cannot touch them — but "cannot" is exactly the kind of claim that hides a wrong closed form, so §D of the new test builds the pre-P6b script out of git at a **fixed SHA (`79222e5`)** and compares all seven junk/hunter levers through **both builds' real `leverState`** at every level 1..400: 2,800 comparisons, zero differences, with the UFO levers checked to genuinely differ so the pass cannot be a file compared to itself.

**ONE CAPABILITY IS GENUINELY LOST AND ONE HOLE IN THE RULE IS LEFT OPEN ON PURPOSE — BOTH FLAGGED HERE RATHER THAN DISCOVERED LATER.** **(a) FLAG-CS025-d:** chains deeper than one carry generation can no longer be expressed. Nothing in the shipped tables was using that deliberately — the UFO chain got it by accident of authoring — but a future changeset that wants depth must reopen the rule explicitly rather than discovering it still works. **(b) The rule as specified is one-sided.** It forbids a *non-driver* from carrying; it does not forbid a *driver* from being someone else's carry target. Such a lever is still legal, still accumulates both its own clock ticks and its parents' bumps for its own value (`test-cs024-p4.js` §F's "BOTH" case, which still passes unchanged), but the bumps it passes **on** are computed from its own clock alone — the closed form does not deepen. In the shipped table no carrier is a carry target, so this is unreachable today; it is written down because the phase prompt named exactly two guard conditions and adding a third would have been inventing design. **If CS025 P1 wants the rule airtight, the third condition is one line: throw if a carry target itself declares `carriesTo`.**

**CS024 P6 — TIMED POWERUP EXPIRY IS DELETED FROM THE GAME. EVERY POWERUP IS A BUDGET NOW, AND ENGINE IS FUEL.** Per `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS024.md` §1.7, §3.4, §3.5, §3.7 and §4.7. **(1) The whole dual-mode apparatus is gone**: `powerMode()`, `powerDuration()`, `game.powerFx` (field AND its `startGame` reset), `POWERUP_DURATION`, `MAGNET_DURATION`, `DEBUG.chainGuardTime`, and the `shotPowerupMode` / `magnetMode` / `chainGuardMode` settings fields — all deleted outright, not merely unread. **`powerActive(type)` reduces to `game.powerBudget[type] > 0`**, one rule for every type, which turns CLAUDE.md's standing "route did-an-effect-end through `powerActive()`, never `powerFx`" rule from a discipline into a structural fact: the field it warns against no longer exists to reach for.
