## Known issues

- **CS026 P3 — THE SHALLOW-CLONE LIST IS UNCHANGED AT TEN FILES, WHICH IS WORTH SAYING BECAUSE THIS PHASE TOUCHED SIX OF THEM.** `test-cs020-p1.js`, `test-cs020-p1b.js`, `test-cs023-p2.js`, `test-cs023-p3.js`, `test-cs024-p1.js` and `test-cs024-p4.js` were all edited this phase, and **none of the edits added or removed a git dependency** — the repoints were registry counts, allow-lists and world-geometry staging. `test-cs026-p3.js` itself follows the `test-cs026-p2.js` §J shape and skips **loudly and counted** on every git-dependent pin, verified on a real no-`.git` copy of the tree (2 skips, 0 failures, exit 0). P6 still owes FORK-H's zero-skips assertion against the ten-file reality recorded in the CS026 P1 entry below, not against a shorter list.

- **⛔ CS026 P2 — `test-cs026-p1.js`'s TRAP-2 SCOPE PIN DOES NOT SKIP WHEN GIT HISTORY IS ABSENT, IT SIMPLY DOES NOT RUN.** Its `if (!changed) { if (shas !== null && shas.length <= 1) skip(...) }` guard means a `shas === null` reading (no checkout, shallow clone) leaves that pin **neither checked nor counted** — the silent-vacuum FORK-CS026-H exists to abolish, in the file that introduced the fix. `test-cs026-p2.js` §J carries the corrected shape (skip unless the *ambiguous* case already reported a failure) and is the model. **Left alone deliberately — one phase, one scope — and it is small: one guard.** P6 should fold it into the same sweep as the ten shallow-clone files, since both feed the zero-skips assertion.

- **⛔ CS026 P1 — TEN SUITE FILES HARD-FAIL ON A SHALLOW CLONE, AND P6 OWES FORK-H'S ZERO-SKIPS ASSERTION AGAINST THAT REALITY, NOT AGAINST THE THREE-FILE ONE.** Measured, not estimated: a `git clone --depth 1` of this repo runs **101 files, 91 passing, 10 failing** — `test-cs017-p6.js`, `test-cs019-p1.js`, `test-cs020-p1.js`, `test-cs020-p1b.js`, `test-cs023-p2.js`, `test-cs023-p3.js`, `test-cs024-p1.js`, `test-cs024-p2.js`, `test-cs024-p4.js`, `test-cs024-p6b.js`. Each reaches for a reference or parent commit and **throws** rather than skipping, which is the behaviour FORK-CS026-H's option (b) explicitly rejected. FORK-H was scoped to three files and those three are done; `test-cs017-p3.js` made four, because CS026 P1's own §F runs each pinned file as a subprocess and caught it crashing (`fatal: invalid object name '683de82'`) and the file was already being edited. **The remaining ten were deliberately not touched — one phase, one scope.** ⛔ **The closing phase's "zero skips" assertion is only meaningful once these can skip**, so P6 has a choice to make and should make it explicitly: convert the ten (mechanical, ~10 small edits, all the same shape as the four already done), or state that the zero-skips assertion covers only files that *can* skip and that a shallow clone still cannot run the suite. Do not let it be decided by accident.

- **CS026 P1 — `test-p5.js` §C's ~1-IN-30 FLAKE DID NOT REPRODUCE IN 400 RUNS ON THE PARENT BUILD, AND THE REASON LOOKS LIKE "ALREADY FIXED".** `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS026.md` §5.1 lists it among the five, but 400 unseeded runs on this phase's parent were **400/400 passing at a constant 102 assertions**. The file's own `isolate()` carries a `FLAKE FIX` comment (added by CS024 P3's regression sweep) that nulls `game.dock`, because `nextWave()` places the dock at a random offset and `isolate()` teleports the ship to a fixed point — on the unlucky rolls the ship landed inside the dock, the offload pass emptied the chain, and the "one hooked canister spends exactly one piece" assertion failed. That is the same mechanism §5.1 describes. **The file was seeded anyway** (it was on the list, and the seed costs nothing), and **`game.dock = null` was deliberately left in place** — the seed pins the roll, the null keeps the test honest if the seed is ever changed. Recorded so a later session does not go hunting for a flake that is already dead.

- ✅ **RESOLVED BY CS026 P1 —** **CS025 P5 — TWO FILES HAVE NON-DETERMINISTIC ASSERTION *COUNTS* (`test-cs018-p4.js` 535 or 541; `test-cs017-p3.js` 1569 or 1570), BOTH PRE-EXISTING, AND BOTH ALWAYS PASS.** Found by `diff`-ing the two consecutive full-regression runs this phase, which is exactly what that diff is for. Each reports `failed: 0` either way — it is the **count** that varies, never the outcome. **Both confirmed pre-existing on a clean `git stash` at this phase's parent**, which is worth recording because the two needed different amounts of evidence: `test-cs018-p4` showed both values within four runs there, but `test-cs017-p3` came up 1570 six times in a row and only revealed 1569 at **3 in 20 runs** — long enough to look like this phase had caused it. It had not; the working tree shows the same split (5 in 14). Cause in both: unpinned `Math.random()` (3 and 5 call sites) on a path where a random-length branch runs a variable number of assertions. **This is a cosmetic determinism gap, not a flake** — unlike `test-starfield.js` §D below, nothing ever fails. Whoever next touches either file should seed or pin the random path, the same fix the CS023 P4 entry below prescribes for the two genuine `Math.random()` flakes. Recorded rather than fixed because it is outside this phase's named scope and carries no risk.

- **CS025 P4 — `VOICE_STILL_TRUE.cargo_full` USES `>=` WHILE ITS TRIGGER SITE USES `===`, AND THAT IS CORRECT.** The trigger is `if (game.chain.length === game.cargoMax) VoiceSys.say("cargo_full")` — an exact equality, because it fires on the pickup where the chain *becomes* full. The drain predicate is `game.chain.length >= game.cargoMax`, because per `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS025.md` §4.4(d) it mirrors **the pickup gate** (`const cargoFull = game.chain.length >= game.cargoMax`), which is the thing that is still true a few frames later. The two are the same condition at the moment of triggering and the `>=` form is the one that survives a `cargoMax` that has since *shrunk*, which nothing currently does. **Flagged only because a future reader diffing the two will see a mismatch and may "fix" one of them; neither should move without re-reading §4.4(d).**

- **CS025 P4 — THE QUEUE HAS EXACTLY ONE TEARDOWN SITE ON PURPOSE, AND ADDING A SECOND WOULD BE DEAD CODE.** `VoiceSys.reset()` (called from `startGame()`) is the only place `queue.length = 0` appears. `quitToTitle()` and `killShip()` deliberately get nothing, because the drain is called from the very end of `update()`'s **playing** body: a stranded entry cannot speak while paused, during `dying`, or on a menu, and `startGame()` clears it before the next run can drain. The reasoning is in a comment at `reset()`; `test-cs025-p4.js` §I pins all three halves (reset empties, nothing survives `startGame()`, the drain runs 0 times paused / 0 times dying / exactly 1 per playing frame). **If a future phase ever calls `VoiceSys.update()` from somewhere other than the playing body, that assumption dies and both sites need teardown.**

- **✅ RESOLVED BY CS025 P5 (the P3 backout) — CS025 P1's ONE-FRAME LAG NO LONGER HAS A CONSUMER, BUT THE LAG ITSELF IS STILL REAL AND IS RECORDED HERE FOR THE NEXT THING THAT READS `magnetPulling()` FROM `draw()`.** The visible half of this issue is gone: the scoop energy tell that would have flickered was **backed out by the CS025 playtest gate (Q4)**, and `Ship.draw()` no longer calls `magnetPulling()` at all (`test-cs025-p3.js` §B pins that). **The underlying timing is unchanged and will bite the same way again.** `game.magnetHoldT`'s writer is a per-frame read taken at the **top** of `update()`'s pickup block, so on the single frame where a hook *fills* the chain, that frame legitimately began not-full: the pull was live for it, and the hold is only re-armed by the *next* frame's writer. Anything sampling `magnetPulling()` **between** `update()` calls — which is exactly where `draw()` runs — therefore sees "pulling" for one frame at full cargo. **Harmless for the mechanic** (the attraction is off from the following frame, and `test-cs025-p1.js` §H pins both halves). **But P3's scoop-energy tell reads this same predicate from `draw()`, so its glow will flicker on for a single frame at the moment the chain fills.** Options are: accept it (one frame at 60 Hz is 17 ms), or have P3 read `magnetPulling() && game.chain.length < game.cargoMax` for the *visual* only. **What must NOT happen is a second writer or a hook at the pickup sites** — that is precisely what `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS025.md` §1.3 forbids, and the whole reason the rule is derived.

- **⛔ CS025 P1 — `DEBUG.magnetResumeDelay` AT 0 DISABLES THE WHOLE SUPPRESSION, NOT JUST THE DELAY.** The one-writer form arms `game.magnetHoldT` to the knob's value on every full-cargo frame, so at 0 it arms to 0 and `magnetPulling()` reads true **even while full**. This is a consequence of the expression the spec prescribes verbatim (with `min: 0`), not a second rule, and it was flagged rather than "fixed" with an invented floor. **It is genuinely useful — it is the clean A/B for the gate, since at 0 the build behaves exactly as CS024 shipped** — but a reader who expects "0 means resume instantly" will be surprised. Documented at the registry entry and pinned in `test-cs025-p1.js` §E. If the gate wants the two behaviours separated, that needs a second knob or an explicit enable flag, which is a design decision.

- **CS025 P1 — `test-cs024-p6e.js` §G WAS RED BEFORE THIS PHASE STARTED, AND THE MOVING-REF LESSON HAS NOW COST SIX REPOINTS.** It compared an untouched panel against **`HEAD`** with two exceptions asserting `engineBurnSeconds` and `GAME_VERSION` must *differ* from it — statements about CS024 P7 that became false the instant `HEAD` advanced past P7 (the CS025 P0 docs-only commit was enough). Confirmed pre-existing by `git stash`, then repaired by pointing the reference at P6e's **own parent commit** (`7c4c6b3`) instead. **The rule is unchanged and is now on its sixth citation: a "nothing else moved" claim must name a FIXED sha — the phase's own parent — never `HEAD`.** `test-cs025-p1.js` follows it, and its doc pin goes further still: it resolves this phase's *own commit* by subject and diffs `parent..commit`, so P5's doc sweep cannot falsify it.

- **CS025 P1 — `test-starfield.js` §D IS AN INTERMITTENT PRE-EXISTING FLAKE (~1 run in 15), NOT CAUSED BY THIS PHASE AND NOT FIXED BY IT.** The failing assertion is *"D: a fractional camera shift moves the near layer by STAR_PARALLAX_FACTOR x the shift (parallax applied)"*. **Confirmed pre-existing**: 40 runs on a clean `git stash` of this phase's changes at CS025 P0 produced two failures; 200 runs of the current tree produced one, with the identical message. It is unrelated to the Magnet, the chain, the registry or anything CS025 touches. The likely cause is the same class as the `test-p5.js` flake CS024 P3 found and fixed — an unseeded star position occasionally landing where the fractional shift is not measurable — so the fix is to seed or bound the sample rather than to relax the assertion. **A full-suite run that reports 96/97 with `test-starfield.js` as the single failure is this, and should be re-run before it is investigated.**

- **CS025 P1 — `game.cargoMax = 0` IS NO LONGER A USABLE TEST-STAGING TRICK, AND ANY FUTURE TEST REACHING FOR IT SHOULD KNOW WHY.** `test-v31-coalesce.js` (34) used it to block the pickup gate so a magnet-pulled piece would travel all the way in. Under the new rule `chain.length (0) >= cargoMax (0)` is **true** — an empty chain at a zero cap is full — so the attraction the test measures was suppressed and the piece never moved. **The semantics are correct (no capacity means no room) and unreachable in a real run (`payloadSlots` never returns less than 8).** The fix was to measure further out (`arriveAt` 20 → 40 px, outside the 28.8 px magnet-widened pickup radius) rather than to weaken the rule. **To let a magnet-pulled piece fly past the ship in a future test, raise the measurement radius — do not zero the cap.**

- **✅ RESOLVED BY CS026 P0 — THE HALF-STRENGTH "MEGA DELIVERY" IS CANCELLED, NOT DEFERRED. IT WILL NOT BE RE-FILED.** CS024 P7's Gate B question 13 asked whether hitting the Super Mega Delivery at level 12 lands as the payoff it should be, and Paul's answer proposed a second, half-strength tier at levels 6–11: *"We should make a smaller 'Mega Delivery' at Level 6, persisting through Level 11. The effects should be exactly half those effects at level 12. Then Super Mega Delivery begins at Level 12, as planned."* P7 correctly declined to build it (a new mechanic, not a closing-phase retune), and CS025 P0 re-filed it as CS026's opening scope. **CS026 P0 (`PLANNED-FEATURES-CS026.md` §0.2) records that Paul has now decided against it outright — it is not in CS026 and it is not deferred to a later changeset. It is dead.** Nothing about it entered GDD §2 at any point (which describes shipped behaviour only), so no design-doc cleanup is owed either. **⛔ The `## Playtest asks` mentions of Gate B Q13 (Paul's verbatim question and answer, and the CS024 P7 gate-outcome record in `## Working / verified`) are preserved deliberately, unedited — they are the true historical record of what was asked and answered, not a live mandate.** Do not "fix" them to match the cancellation; only this entry and the corresponding `## Next up` paragraphs were rewritten.

- **CS024 P7 — NINE FIXED-REF DOC PINS RETIRED IN PLACE, AND THE PATTERN HAS NOW COST FIVE REPOINTS ACROSS THREE CHANGESETS.** `test-cs023-p2` §A, `test-cs024-p1` §F, `-p2` §F, `-p3` §H, `-p4` §H, `-p5` §H, `-p6` §H, `-p6c` §J, `-p6f` §L each asserted, against the moving `HEAD` reference, that no design doc had been touched. Every one is true of its own session and false of every working tree after it, and the doc-sweep phase falsifies all nine at once by instruction. Retired with the reason inline, following `test-cs024-p6b.js` §G. **The rule for anyone writing the next one: a "nothing else moved" trap must be written against the phase's own PARENT COMMIT (a fixed sha), in that phase's own file — never against `HEAD`.** A `HEAD`-relative trap tests whatever happens next, which is not what the phase is claiming.

- **CS024 P7 — THE GDD'S `[RETIRED …]` MARKER STYLE IS LOAD-BEARING AND A FUTURE SWEEP SHOULD NOT "CLEAN IT UP".** Grepping §2 for the names of removed systems returns dozens of hits that are correct: `[RETIRED CS024 P3] Garbage lifetime`, `[RETIRED CS024 P1] The ORBIT section`, `WORLD_SIZE_ORBIT` keeping its slot as a historical name, and so on. The style predates CS024 (`[RETIRED CS018 P7]`) and is how §2.19 in particular records what a knob *was*. **The trap is §2 asserting a dead system in the present tense, not §2 remembering one** — that distinction is what separated the six real staleness bugs this phase fixed from the ~200 legitimate hits it left alone.

- **CS024 P7 — THREE PRE-EXISTING FUSED MEGA-LINES SURVIVE IN `archive/STATUS-HISTORY.md`, DELIBERATELY NOT RE-SPLIT.** Each holds several `**Prior session…**` write-ups on one physical line, from the mid-2026 formatting bug that necessitated the archive split. Counted before and after this session's relocation: **3 and 3** — the prune did not create any, and the newly-moved CS021/CS022 material is correctly paragraph-separated. They were left alone because the archive is a faithful record and re-flowing 160 KB of fused text is a content rewrite, not a formatting fix. Noted in the archive's own preamble.

- **CS024 P7 — `ORBITAL-OVERHAUL-GDD.md` §3's Architecture Map still carries four dangling `§2.13.1` cross-references.** All four sit inside explicitly historical clauses that already carry their own `**CS024:**` correction in the same table cell (e.g. *"CS021 P1–P3: `levelDef(n)` gains an `archetype` column (§2.13.1)… **⛔ CS024:** `nextWave()` loses its archetype branch"*), so none of them misleads about the shipped build — but the section they point at no longer exists. §3 is a layered historical record by design and rewriting its history was outside this phase's scope (the prompt named the Constants / Entity classes / Flow functions / game-object rows, all four of which were updated). Left as a known cosmetic wart rather than fixed silently.

- **THE DOCS NOW DESCRIBE A MECHANISM THE BUILD NO LONGER HAS, AND P7 OWNS THE RECONCILIATION.** TRAP 6 kept P6b out of every design doc, so `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS024.md` §2 still describes the general wrapping DAG, and `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS025.md` §1 still says the rule is "BUILT IN CS024 P4" (it is built in P4's *section*, but by P6b). **`PLANNED-FEATURES-CS025.md` §2's own instruction anticipates this** — "check what CS024 shipped before editing… if that happened, CS025 P1 is a verification pass rather than an edit — report it and do not manufacture a diff." **It happened. CS025 P1 is a verification pass.** The nine step counts, the nine-way `carriesTo`, the guard and the closed form are all in the shipped build exactly as §2 tables them.

- **The nine step counts are chosen for stagger SHAPE, not tuned (FLAG-CS025-c), and Gate B's questions were written before they existed.** Gate B asks about levels 1 → 20; **every one of the nine dependents is still within its first two carries at level 20**, so the gate as written cannot see the stagger at all. `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS025.md` §4 wants levels **25 → 45 minimum** for exactly this. - **CS024 P6's repoint sweep touched TWENTY-NINE test files and the phase prompt predicted none.** Registry-count/header pins only: `test-cs018-p4`/`-p6`/`-p7`, `test-cs020-p1`/`-p1b`, `test-cs024-p1`/`-p2`/`-p3`/`-p4`/`-p5`, `test-cs023-p2`. Real repoints or inversions: `test-cs009-p4` (the whole ring-denominator/halo/low-timer subject), `test-cs009-p5` (bank badges), `test-cs010-p9` (the expiry-voice falling edge), `test-cs012-p2` (HUD rows), `test-cs013-p2` + `test-cs016-p4` (the Difficulty screen's geometry and its mid-run lock), `test-cs015-p3`, `test-cs017-p6` (four sections), `test-cs017-p7`, `test-cs018-p2` (its typed-entry and descending-trio fixtures both sat on `chainGuardTime`), `test-cs019-p1` (its whole §C), `test-cs023-p3` (a byte-pin — see below), `test-f6`, `test-firerate`, `test-p5`, `test-p6`, `test-v31-coalesce`, `test-v34-p6`. **No file was deleted.** Several claims came back STRONGER rather than merely repointed: `test-cs009-p5` §C now walks every type instead of magnet alone, `test-cs010-p9` §E5 drives the falling edge for four types instead of two, and `test-cs016-p4`'s locked/unlocked loops now DERIVE their row list from `DIFFICULTY_ROWS` instead of relisting it, so the next row change moves one line.

- **`test-cs023-p3.js`'s byte-identity pin on `breakChain` had to NARROW, and the narrowing is flagged rather than quietly widened.** CS024 P6 genuinely edits that function (the guard's per-intercept charge stops being gated on the retired count mode). Dropping it to a comment-insensitive comparison would have been a real loosening, so it moved to the strongest available form instead: **the pre-P6 body with EXACTLY the documented substitution applied must equal the current body** — i.e. that one edit is the ONLY diff — with the old text asserted present in the pinned build first so the pass cannot be vacuous. **It is pinned to a FIXED SHA (`c96a983`, CS024 P5), not the moving `HEAD`** — the `test-cs017-p3`/`test-cs024-p1` precedent for exactly this trap, since against a moving HEAD the claim evaporates into "the file equals itself" the moment P6 is committed. The other three functions in that pin stay byte-strict.

- **`test-cs019-p1.js` §B3 builds a PINNED HISTORICAL source through the same `stage()` helper as the live build, and that helper now has to probe for a field rather than set it.** The pre-fix build still has `settings.chainGuardMode` and still defaults it to `"time"`, where `applyPowerup("guard")` seeds a clock instead of a budget — so the historical control needs the count path explicitly, while the current build has no such key to set. `if ("chainGuardMode" in X.settings)` covers both without branching on a version flag. Worth knowing before anyone "tidies" that line out.

- **`test-starfield.js` flaked once across two full consecutive regression runs and is NOT this phase's responsibility.** It passed 5/5 standalone immediately after, is untouched by this session (`git diff` clean), and is the known pre-existing flake already recorded here from CS023 P4b: its own module-load starfield uses the file's one deliberately-unpinned `Math.random()` site.

- **✅ RESOLVED BY CS024 P6c — CS024 P5's DEBUG-OVERRIDE MECHANISM WAS THE WRONG SHAPE, AND IT IS REPLACED RATHER THAN CONFIRMED.** P5 flagged "moving a slider freezes that lever flat for the rest of the game" as an in-session design call needing Gate B's validation. It did not need validating; it needed replacing. **A lever is not a value** — it is a floor, a ceiling, a step count and the level number — so one row per lever could only ever pin it flat, which is a debugging tool and not a tuning one, and Gate B's job is tuning. P6c gives every lever **three** rows and retires the `def: null` sentinel with the flat rows. **The flat pin survives as one thing you can choose (Floor = Ceil), not as the only thing a slider can do.** Nothing here is outstanding; the original entry is superseded and was removed rather than left to mislead. **What Gate B should now report instead is the NUMBERS it lands on** — see Playtest asks.

- **⛔ `IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-CS024.md`'s Gate B question 10 IS NOW STALE, AND P7'S DOC SWEEP OWNS IT.** It reads "each of the 17 levers has **two** live sliders — floor and ceiling… Step counts are *not* sliders; if a lever's ramp feels the wrong *length*, say so in words and P7 changes the step count at the source." As of CS024 P6c step counts **are** sliders — the third row per lever — precisely so that Gate B questions 9 ("three chains breathing on different periods — rich or arrhythmic?") and the levels-41+ flatness question can be answered with a number instead of a sentence. `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS024.md` §2.6 is already correct (it was rewritten for P6c); §6's copy of the question is the stale one. **Docs were untouched this phase per TRAP 6**, so this is recorded rather than fixed, and the Playtest-asks briefing below states the current truth for whoever runs the gate.

- **CS024 P5 — NO `POWERUPS` SECTION HEADER EXISTS YET, ON PURPOSE.** Spec §5's target section order (`SHIP · GARBAGE · CHAIN GUARD · DELIVERY · JUNK · HUNTER · UFO · POWERUPS · GLOBAL`) is the END STATE across P5 *and* P6 combined — P5 has nothing to put under `POWERUPS` (its two new knobs, `engineBurnSeconds`/`engineMassMult`, are P6's), and the standing rule ("a header left with no entries under it renders as a stray label") forbids creating it empty ahead of time, the same way CS024 P4 retired `JUNK`/`UFO MOVEMENT`/`UFO WEAPONS` rather than leave them standing over nothing. **P6 should add the header at the same time as its first knob, not before.**

- **CS024 P5 — THE PARALLEL-AGENT REPOINT DISPATCH MISSED ONE OF THE TWENTY-THREE BROKEN FILES (`test-cs024-p3.js`), AND THE GAP WAS ONLY CAUGHT BY THE FULL-SUITE RE-RUN, NOT BY THE DISPATCH ITSELF.** Seven subagents were briefed against a triage list assembled by grepping the initial full-suite failure output; one file (`GARBAGE_COALESCE_DELAY`/`FROZEN_JUNK_COUNT` in its `RETURN` list, plus a stale `15`-value-entry registry pin) fell through a gap between two agents' file lists and wasn't assigned to anyone. Fixed directly, in the same session, once the post-sweep full-suite run surfaced it as the only remaining failure. **The lesson for the next multi-agent repoint dispatch: cross-check the union of every batch's assigned files against the original failing-file list BEFORE declaring the sweep complete, not after — a full-suite re-run catches the gap eventually, but a checklist catches it before spending a run finding out.**

- **CS024 P4 — THE REPOINT SWEEP TOUCHED TWENTY-SIX TEST FILES AND DELETED ONE, WHICH IS THE LARGEST SWEEP IN THIS CHANGESET AND WAS ENTIRELY PREDICTABLE.** Deleting the level table breaks every file that ever named `levelDef`, `stepAt`, `TIER_STEPS`, `JUNK_CYCLE`, `PHASE_LEN`, `LEVEL_MAX`, `ramp`, `difficultyFactor`, `RAMP_WAVES`, `SAUCER_SMALL_CHANCE_*` or any of the 21 tier knobs — and most of those names sit in a `RETURN` list, where a missing symbol throws out of the factory's own return statement and takes the whole file down before a single assertion runs. **`scratchpad/test-cs018-p1.js` is DELETED** (its entire subject is gone; `test-cs024-p4.js` §B inherits its bare-context convention verbatim). The other twenty-six were repointed, most by inverting a claim into its mirror image at the same strength: `test-cs012-p1.js`, `test-cs015-p5.js`, `test-cs017-p1/p2/p3/p4.js`, `test-cs018-p2/p3/p4/p5/p6/p7.js`, `test-cs019-p1.js`, `test-cs020-p1/p1b.js`, `test-cs023-p2/p3.js`, `test-cs024-p1/p2/p3.js`, `test-f4.js`, `test-f5.js`, `test-v31-coalesce.js`, `test-v31-world.js`, `test-v34-p6.js`. **The registry count alone appears in seven separate files** (36 → 15 everywhere).

- **CS024 P4 — TWO MEASUREMENT CONTROLS WERE GENUINELY WEAKENED BY THE FREEZE, AND BOTH ARE FLAGGED IN PLACE FOR P5 TO PUT BACK.** These are the only places where a repoint lost real power rather than changing shape. **(1) `test-cs023-p2.js` §H's vacuity floors dropped from 1,000 pair-checks to 100.** They were set when level 21 spawned 13 large satellites; with the count frozen at 3 the harvest cascade starts from under a quarter of the bodies and the worst frame does proportionally less work (351 checks over 28 bodies, where it used to do thousands). **The GATE CEILINGS above them were deliberately NOT lowered to match** — a bound that tracks the measurement downward stops being a bound — so only the floor moved, and the comment says so with a ⛔ marker. **(2) `test-cs017-p3.js` §G's "the ceiling is actually reached" control moved onto the odometer**, because the frozen spawn can no longer reach anything; the odometer does reach its ceiling, on exactly 13 of levels 1..130. Both revert the moment `nextWave()` reads `leverState(game.wave).junkCount`.

- **CS024 P4 — THE MOVING-GIT-REFERENCE TRAP CAUGHT THIS PHASE'S OWN NEW TEST, POST-COMMIT, AND THE FIX IS RECORDED HERE BECAUSE IT KEEPS RECURRING.** `test-cs024-p4.js` §D proves `musicIntensity` byte-identical to the **actual** retired `difficultyFactor` by pulling that function out of git rather than retyping it — and the first draft read it from **`HEAD`**. That is correct right up until the phase is committed, at which point HEAD becomes the build that no longer contains the function, and the assertion inverts. Caught by re-running the full suite *after* committing, which is why that step is worth keeping. **The reference is now the fixed SHA `887a44c`** (the commit before this phase), the same idiom and the same reasoning as `test-cs017-p3.js` §F's `PRE_P3_REF`. **THE RULE, stated once for whoever writes the next cross-build comparison: a test that compares against a previous build must name a FIXED SHA. `HEAD` is never the right reference, and it fails late — after the commit, not during the work.**

- **CS024 P4 — ONE PRE-EXISTING RED, FIXED, AND ITS CAUSE IS THE SAME TRAP, WHICH HAS NOW BITTEN FOUR TIMES.** `test-cs024-p3.js` was **already failing before this session touched anything** — measured against a `git worktree` at HEAD, not assumed. Its TRAP 4 sanity check asserted "this phase's edits are visible" by unioning `git diff --name-only HEAD` with HEAD's own file list, which goes empty the moment ANY unrelated commit lands on top — and one did: Paul's STATUS.md commit carrying Gate A's answers. That is the same moving-git-reference trap that left `test-cs024-p2.js` and `test-cs023-p2.js` red before P3's session, and that caught this phase's own new test post-commit (above). **The fix removes the git dependency entirely**: the check now looks for `cullGarbage(` in the shipped file, which is durable — P3's edits are either there or the phase did not land. `test-starfield.js` also failed once during baseline measurement and passed on two subsequent runs; it draws on `Math.random` and is **flaky, not broken** — worth a seed at some point, not this phase.

- **CS024 P4 — `DiffLog`'s `phase` and `rel` COLUMNS NOW LOG `null`, AND THE SEVEN FORMER TIER COLUMNS LOG NUMBERS.** `DIFFLOG_FIELDS` itself is deliberately **unchanged** — P5's prompt owns the repoint onto `leverState` and any list change that comes with it — but three groups of expressions had to move. `phase`/`rel` were positions inside CS018's 21-level three-phase structure, which is **deleted, not renamed**; re-deriving them would mean reintroducing `PHASE_LEN` to describe something that no longer exists, so the honest row says nothing and `Array.join` renders `null` as an empty CSV cell. The seven tier columns logged a NAME; tiers are gone, so they log the frozen NUMBER in play — a unit change, and the right direction, since P5's `leverState` values are numbers too.

- **CS024 P4 — `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS024.md` §2.5 IS NOW STALE ON TWO NUMBERS, DELIBERATELY LEFT THAT WAY (TRAP 3).** It names `GARBAGE_MAGNET_RANGE` as "180 px" and `GARBAGE_MAGNET_PULL` as "40 px/s²", which Gate A Q1 moved to 160 and 30. The spec's *classification* of both — resolved as **not levers**, remaining plain debug knobs — is unaffected and still correct. Docs are untouched this phase by instruction; **P7's doc sweep should carry the two new values into §2.5**, and into the GDD alongside the new `GARBAGE_COALESCE_DELAY` of 5.0 s.

- **CS024 P3 — THE REPOINT SWEEP TOUCHED FOURTEEN TEST FILES AND DELETED TWO, AND THE PROMPT PREDICTED ONE OF THEM.** The prompt warned that "several existing tests stage garbage and rely on it decaying"; the decay-dependent files were only three of the fourteen. **Deleted outright** (whole subject removed — the `test-v34-p2.js` precedent CS024 P2 set for `leverScale`): `test-cs017-p5.js` (the bonus canister) and `test-cs015-p6.js` (the `garbageLifetime` knob and the decay clock). **Crash-only repoints** (a `RETURN` list or destructure named a now-deleted symbol, so the file threw before asserting anything): `test-cs017-p2.js`, `test-cs018-p1.js`, `test-cs018-p3.js`, `test-cs018-p4.js`, `test-f5.js`, `test-v31-coalesce.js`, `test-v36-death.js`. **Stale registry-count pins** (34 → 36): `test-cs018-p6.js`, `test-cs018-p7.js`, `test-cs020-p1.js`, `test-cs020-p1b.js`, `test-cs023-p3.js`, `test-cs024-p1.js`, `test-cs024-p2.js`, `test-cs023-p2.js`. **Substantive rewrites:** `test-cs018-p4.js` §D (the ambient producer's whole section INVERTED into a three-surface removal proof — state field, source, and ten minutes of real frames on a starved board), §E (hold → destroy), §F (the cap-0 band inverted: a clump now converts at level 1), §G (both halves re-driven through coalescence, two `noteLargeHunterSpawn` callers → one); `test-v31-coalesce.js` §16/§21 (ageing-out → permanence) and §0 (the decay/inert-window relationship assertions retired, replaced by "no lifetime exists to get it wrong"); `test-cs018-p9.js` §G (the sweep pause's "decay clocks are NOT frozen" half re-expressed on the new `age` clock — same property, one clock later); `test-cs018-p2.js` §F/§G (the numeric-entry fixture row moved off `garbageLifetime` onto `lastStandSpeed`, and the legacy-save fixture now carries an ORPHANED `garbageLifetime` key on purpose, because "a real 1.0.0.17-era save has this key and known-value-else-default must ignore it" is a better claim than the one it replaced).

- **CS024 P3 — TWO TEST FILES WERE ALREADY RED BEFORE THIS PHASE TOUCHED ANYTHING, BOTH FOR THE SAME REASON, AND BOTH ARE NOW FIXED.** `test-cs024-p2.js` and `test-cs023-p2.js` each compared a function body against `git show HEAD:asteroids-deluxe.html`, and **a bare `HEAD` re-aims itself at whatever landed most recently** — so both went red the instant CS024 P2's own commit was made, before this session started. This is the third time the repo has hit this exact trap (CS017 P3, then `test-cs024-p1.js` in CS024 P2), and it is fixed the same way both times: pinned to a fixed SHA (`37bfcd1` for P2's reference, `300ac27` for CS023 P2's). **The pins alone were not enough, because P3 then genuinely edited both pinned functions.** Rather than delete the claims, both were NARROWED in the direction that still means something — `test-cs024-p2.js` §C now proves the attraction half of `coalesceGarbage` is byte-identical to the pre-edit body minus the dead guard (which is where the deleted clamp actually lived), and `test-cs023-p2.js`'s TRAP 2 drops whole-body identity for the timeless claim it was always carrying: `coalesceGarbage` and `class Garbage` never touch `debrisBounce`, the two bounce constants or `DEBRIS_MASS`, and no `debrisBounce` call site mentions garbage. **The lesson worth carrying to P4:** a whole-body byte pin survives exactly until the next phase edits that function, so pin against a fixed SHA AND scope the claim to the region the phase actually cared about.

- **CS024 P3 — A PRE-EXISTING FLAKE IN `test-p5.js` WAS FOUND AND FIXED (1 run in 30, NOT caused by this phase).** §G's "one hooked canister spends exactly one piece" intermittently saw an empty chain. Cause: `nextWave()` places the dock at a **random** offset from the ship's spawn point, and the file's `isolate()` helper then teleports the ship to a fixed `(1800, 1000)`; on the unlucky rolls that lands inside the dock, so the real offload pass pops each node the moment it is hooked. **CS024 P2 doubling `DOCK_RADIUS` 44 → 88 doubled the target it had to miss**, which is presumably why it surfaced now. Fixed with `game.dock = null` in `isolate()` — the established idiom (`test-cs019-p1.js`'s `quietRun` does the same) and safe because every dock read in `update()` is `if (game.dock)`-guarded. Measured 1/30 failures on the pre-P3 build, 0/60 after the fix. **Worth a look during P4/P5: any other test that teleports the ship after `startGame()` without dropping the dock has the same latent exposure.**

- **CS024 P3 — MY OWN §I SMOKE SECTION WAS INTERMITTENTLY MEASURING A DEAD GAME, AND THE FIX IS WORTH KNOWING FOR ANY FUTURE CEILING TEST.** Roughly 1 run in 4 it reported a 5,000-piece field and a convincing-looking "the ceiling failed." It had not: coalescence made Hunters, a Hunter rammed the ship, the run left `"playing"`, and **`update()` early-returns in every state but `"playing"` — so the cull stops running entirely** while the test loop kept feeding the field. The ceiling is a property of the LIVE sim and nothing else. Fixed by topping the hull up each frame and asserting the premise directly (`the run never left "playing"`), so the section fails loudly on a broken premise instead of silently measuring the wrong thing. **The general lesson for P4–P7: any long-running headless section that feeds the field must either keep the ship alive or assert the state it thinks it is in.** Two other latent instances of the same class were fixed this session (`test-p5.js`'s dock flake, and the moving-`HEAD` references) — three run-dependent test bugs in one sweep is a pattern, not a coincidence.

- **CS024 P3 — `updateDeath()` DOES NOT CULL, DELIBERATELY, AND THAT IS AN ACCEPTED ASYMMETRY.** The death spectacle has its own filter pass that mirrors `update()`'s, but `cullGarbage()` is not added to it: that sim runs no coalescence so it pays none of the O(n²) cost the ceiling exists to bound, and the run ends inside `DEATH_DURATION`. Culling there would only delete the player's salvage during the death animation for no benefit. If a future phase ever gives the death sim a coalescence pass, this decision has to be revisited.

- **CS024 P3 — LEVELS 2–4 GET A HUNTER BACK, BY A DIFFERENT ROUTE, AND NOBODY ASKED FOR THAT DIRECTLY.** CS018 P4's cap table was 0 across levels 1–4, which suppressed large Hunters there from BOTH producers; `test-cs018-p4.js` §F existed specifically to pin that band. With the schedule replaced by a flat ceiling, **a clump reaching 12 pieces now converts at level 1 exactly as at level 63.** This is a consequence of the changeset's own design (§2.4 puts Hunter pressure on `coalescePause`, not on a count cap) rather than an oversight, and it is arguably the point — the level a Hunter first appears at is now decided by how fast the player neglects garbage, not by a clock. But it is a real early-game difficulty change that no line of the P3 prompt asks for in those words, so **Gate A question 1 should be read with it in mind**: if Hunters arrive at level 2, that is the new system working, not a bug.

- **CS024 P1 — `shieldBounce()` NOW HAS NO LIVE CALLER, AND IS DELIBERATELY LEFT IN PLACE FOR P2.** Its only call site was CS021 P1b's `else if (h.orbitCenter) shieldBounce(h)` arm in the hazards-vs-ship block — the "a hazard on a rail cannot be pushed, so bounce the SHIP instead" case — which this phase removes as unreachable. The function, its two constants (`SHIELD_BOUNCE_RESTITUTION`, `SHIELD_BOUNCE_MIN`) and `test-cs023-p2.js`'s byte-unchanged pin on it all still stand. **This was NOT deleted, on purpose:** it is a dead-code removal, which is **CS024 P2's** scope (§1.8), not P1's, and the phase prompt's own removal list does not name it. Flagged here so P2 picks it up rather than it rotting quietly. The codebase precedent for leaving an unread symbol standing with a note (`ORBIT_RADIUS_STEP_PAD`, `DEBRIS_COUNT_MAX`) is the same idiom — the difference is that this one has a named phase to collect it.

- **CS024 P1 — A PHASE-SCOPED "DOCS UNTOUCHED" TRAP FIRED ON A SPEC EDIT NOBODY MADE IN THE SESSION, AND THE TRAP WAS THE THING THAT WAS WRONG.** `test-cs023-p2.js` §A checked `git diff --name-only HEAD` for any file matching `GDD|DIFFICULTY-LEVERS|VERSION-HISTORY|PLANNED-FEATURES|IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES`. `IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-CS024.md` carries an uncommitted working-tree edit to **P4's own prompt** (a new "only drivers may wrap" restriction on the odometer) that predates this session and has nothing to do with any code change. `PLANNED-FEATURES-*` and `IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-*` are **spec** documents, authored outside build sessions and legitimately edited between them; what every phase's TRAP actually protects is the shipped-behaviour set — the GDD, its version history, and `DIFFICULTY-LEVERS.md` — which is exactly the list CS024 P1's own TRAP 3 names. The filter is narrowed to those three in `test-cs023-p2.js` and `test-cs024-p1.js` uses the same narrowed list. **That P4-prompt edit is left uncommitted and unstaged — it is not this phase's to commit.**

- **CS024 P1 — TOMBSTONE COMMENTS BREAK NAIVE SOURCE PINS, AND THE FAILURE IS RED IN THIS PHASE BUT WOULD BE GREEN IN THE NEXT.** This phase leaves a comment at every deletion site naming what stood there — which means a raw `scriptSrc` scan for `orbitCenter`, `R reroll orbit start angles`, `levelDef(n - 1)` or `spawnFieldSatellites(` now matches the **epitaph**. Four assertions (three in `test-cs024-p1.js`, one in `test-cs018-p1.js` §B) were caught by this while being written and are all on comment-stripped source now. **The direction of the hazard matters:** here it made a *removal* check fail loudly, but the same pattern makes an *existence* check pass falsely — a later phase pinning "X is still present" against `scriptSrc` would be satisfied by a comment saying X was removed. Use `codeOnly` for both directions.

- **CS023 P4C — THE RAIL-TO-RAIL CLEARANCE IS NOW GOVERNED BY THE SEAM, NOT THE CORRIDOR, AND IT IS 68 px.** Correction C11's "two rail-borne satellites can never touch" still holds, but the number behind it fell from 440 px to **68 px** and the reason changed. A 1,000 px outer ring in a 2,160 px-tall world nearly folds onto itself: ring 4's antipodal pair (6 satellites, so k = 3 always exists) standing vertically is 2,000 px apart the long way and 160 px the short way. The clearance is exactly `2 × (wrap-clean budget − outer satellite edge) + 40` — the C16a margin doubled — so every geometry the clamp accepts keeps it positive (44 px at the 204 px step ceiling) and `debrisBounce`'s rail/rail branch stays unreachable by construction. **Not a defect, and nothing to fix at P5**; recorded because it is a tight margin nobody specced, it was found by a test rather than by the spec, and the next person to widen this geometry needs to know the seam is what binds first. `test-cs023-p2.js` §E asserts it directly.

- **CS023 P4C — TWO OF THAT SECTION'S ASSERTIONS HAD BEEN PASSING ON LUCK, AND WOULD HAVE KEPT DOING SO.** `test-cs023-p2.js` §E's same-ring snapshot checks and its 21-occurrence real-spawn sweep both measure a single frame, so whether they see the 68 px worst case depends on a random ring start angle; only the 1,800-frame live sweep (which rotates the rings through a full relative revolution) reaches it every run. Both are repointed onto the derived seam floor now. **The general lesson, worth carrying:** a snapshot assertion about a rotating shell is a sample, not a bound — if the bound matters, either drive the rotation or assert the closed form.

- **CS023 P4C — WRAP FOLDING SILENTLY WEAKENS STAGED-DISTANCE TESTS, AND THE FAILURE IS GREEN, NOT RED.** Seven placements across `test-cs023-p4.js` had to move because a nominal distance is not the wrap-aware one once it exceeds a world half-extent: 1,200 px at bearing 1.4 rad is 816 px in a 1,440-tall field world, 1,500 px at bearing 1.9 is 885 px on the orbit torus. A body that lands inside `DEBRIS_DRIFT_TRIGGER_R` **blocks the arming pass for the entire level**, so the proof it sits in goes vacuous rather than failing. Every re-sited placement now either stays inside a bearing the world cannot fold or asserts `dockDist === nominal` at setup. **Any future trigger-radius change has to re-check every staged distance in that file, not just the ones that go red.**

- **CS023 P4B — TWO NEWLY-OBSERVED PRE-EXISTING UNPINNED-`Math.random()` FLAKES, NEITHER CAUSED BY THIS SESSION.** `test-starfield.js` §D ("a fractional camera shift moves the near layer by `STAR_PARALLAX_FACTOR` × the shift") failed once in a full-suite run and passed 8/8 standalone — its own header already names the module-load starfield as this file's one deliberately-unpinned `Math.random()` site. `test-p5.js` §C ("hooking in time mode spends no budget") failed once in a full-suite run — a DIFFERENT assertion from the already-documented §G flake below, and neither file was touched this session. Recorded so neither is mistaken for a P4b regression next round; each needs the same seeded-`Math.random()` fix as the seven already-documented files.

- **CS023 P4 — THE ARMING PASS'S PER-BODY TARGET-RADIUS TEST IS UNREACHABLE IN THIS BUILD, AND THAT IS CORRECT RATHER THAN DEAD WEIGHT.** Spec §4.7 gives arming two distance tests: a GLOBAL one (nothing live inside `DEBRIS_DRIFT_TRIGGER_R`, 814 px) and a PER-BODY one (this body is beyond `DEBRIS_DRIFT_TARGET_R`, 676 px — **renamed from `ORBIT_GRAVITY_TRIGGER_R`/`ORBIT_GRAVITY_TARGET_R` by CS023 P4B**, spec C15; same radii, same derivation, new name). Because the target sits INSIDE the trigger, **any body in the 676–814 band blocks the whole pass, itself included** — so whenever arming runs at all, every live debris body is already at ≥ 814 px and the per-body test can never exclude anything. Both are implemented exactly as specced: the per-body test is what keeps the rule self-consistent if a future geometry retune ever put the target OUTSIDE the trigger, and removing it would make the mechanic silently wrong at that point rather than merely redundant. **The consequence for anyone reading the code or the docs is that the observable arming rule is the TRIGGER alone**, and `test-cs023-p4.js` §C says so in its own sub-block rather than asserting a boundary that cannot be reached — now proven on both archetypes (P4b). P5's GDD §2.13.1 paragraph should describe the band, not just quote the two radii, and should use the `DEBRIS_DRIFT_*` names throughout.

- **CS023 P4 — TWO PRE-EXISTING UNPINNED-`Math.random()` FLAKES ARE NOW CHARACTERISED AND SHOULD BE PINNED BY WHOEVER NEXT TOUCHES THOSE FILES.** `test-cs017-p1` §F ("level 6: forcing the spawn timer produced exactly one saucer") failed on **both** pre-edit baseline runs — before a single line of P4 existed — and gives roughly one failure in three standalone. `test-p5` §G ("one hooked canister spends exactly one piece", the B-5-a magnet double-spend check) failed once in twenty runs against a `git stash`-ed HEAD build carrying no P4 code at all. **Neither is caused by CS023 P4**, and neither is in the long-documented RNG/wall-clock noise set, which is why they are recorded here rather than left to be re-discovered as a "new" failure next round. Each needs a seeded `Math.random()` around its own probe, the same fix the seven documented files already have.

- **⛔ CS023 P2 — A `Saucer` HAS NO `size` FIELD, SO SPEC §4.6's `debrisBounce(a, s)` IS A NaN BOMB AGAINST A FREE SATELLITE. P3 MUST SETTLE THE SAUCER'S MASS BEFORE IT WRITES THAT LINE.** `Saucer`'s constructor carries `small` / `radius` / `damage` / `dead` and no tier field of any kind (verified against the class body, not assumed). `debrisBounce`'s free/free branch reads `DEBRIS_MASS[a.size]` and `DEBRIS_MASS[b.size]`, so a free debris satellite bounced off a saucer evaluates `DEBRIS_MASS[undefined]` → `undefined` → `1 / undefined` → **NaN propagated through the impulse, the floor and the overlap push**, leaving that satellite with `NaN` position and velocity for the rest of the run. Against a RAIL-BORNE satellite the same call happens to behave correctly — the saucer takes the "free" role and the rail is untouched, which is exactly what §4.6 wants — so this would surface as an intermittent corruption rather than a clean crash, and only on levels where a UFO meets loose debris. **Spec §4.8's constants table gives no saucer mass, so this is a genuine gap and P2 deliberately did not invent one** (TRAP 3: no saucer path this phase). The options are a saucer entry in `DEBRIS_MASS`-adjacent form, a `size`-like field on `Saucer`, or a mass argument on `debrisBounce`; whichever P3 takes is a design call, not a fix.

- **CS023 P2 — SPEC §7's "EVERY FIELD LEVEL'S PLAY COMES OUT BYTE-IDENTICAL" IS NOW FALSE, AND IT WAS A CARRY-OVER RATHER THAN A RULE.** The debris↔debris pass in spec §4.5 has no archetype gate and §1.1's "Debris ↔ Debris → elastic bounce" row has no level qualifier, so two free satellites bounce on a field level exactly as on an orbit one — which is the intended behaviour and is what makes the mechanic more than an orbit-level curiosity. The sentence reads as inherited from CS022's §7, where the changeset genuinely was orbit-only. **What IS still true, and is what the existing pin actually asserts:** `test-cs023-p1.js` §I compares field-level SPAWNS (positions, velocities, sizes, the absence of orbit state) against a pinned pre-P1 build and is unaffected, because nothing there drives an `update()` frame. **P5's doc sweep should correct §7 rather than leave two documents disagreeing**, and FORK-CS023-H's orbit-only gate applies to the DRIFT alone, never to the bounce.

- **CS023 P2 — SPEC §4.6's "`debrisBounce` … writes only to `a`" IS FALSE OF THE SHIPPED HELPER, and harmlessly so.** The free/free branch must write both bodies — a mass-weighted elastic exchange that writes one of them conserves nothing — and the phase prompt's own test requirement ("momentum conserved at the `DEBRIS_MASS` ratios") is what forces it. It is harmless for §4.6's own use because the saucer is already `dead` when `debrisBounce(a, s)` is called, so writes to it are discarded by the end-of-frame filter. Recorded so P3 does not build a claim on the sentence; the mutation sweep carries "free/free writes only `a`" as a permanent red control (2,456 failures).

- **CS023 P2 — A PAIR-WALK RESOLVES ONE PAIR PER FRAME, SO A PILE OF THREE OR MORE OVERLAPPING BODIES TAKES A FEW FRAMES, AND THAT IS BEHAVIOUR, NOT A DEFECT.** "Separation within one frame" holds exactly per PAIR (asserted on all 540 free/free and 480 free/rail trials). It does not hold for a pile: a coincident 3-way split is apart after one frame and fully clear after three, and a free body wedged between two rings — 138 px apart with a 92 px body — can be resolved against one ring and land inside the other, which is why §D's 300-frame probe ends geometrically clear on ~273–286 of 300 frames while being deflected on all 300. It converges, it is bounded (asserted under 60 frames), and it costs nothing beyond the pass that is already running. **Do not "fix" it with an iterative solver** — that would multiply the O(n²) pass by an iteration count for a case the frame budget currently absorbs for free.

- **CS023 P1 — THE ARRAY-POSITION WARNING BELOW IS RETIRED, AND SO IS THE LEVEL-3-STAGING ONE, BUT ONLY THE FIRST IS SAFE TO FORGET.** FORK-CS023-A inverted the ramp, so `layout.rings[k].index === k` at every occurrence again and the CS022 P3 note that array position and ring index DIVERGE is false (it is corrected at both its source sites and its assertion sites). **Keying a per-ring snapshot by `r.index` or `r.radius` is still the right habit and every test that adopted it stays correct unchanged — it is simply no longer load-bearing.** The level-3-staging warning is NOT safe to forget: it has flipped sign rather than gone away. **Occurrence 1 now lays RING 1 ALONE**, so a level-3 setup that wants the outer ring, the fast ring (`ORBIT_FAST_RING` is `[2, 4]`, so the innermost fast ring is ring 2 and arrives at occurrence 2), or more than one ring still gets none of them — `test-cs021-p1b.js` §K crashed outright on exactly this. **The idiom to copy is unchanged and still right:** `let n = ORBIT_LEVEL_EVERY; while (activeRingsFor(n).length < wantedCount) n += ORBIT_LEVEL_EVERY;` — or, to name a ring on the board at a given level, read it off `activeRingsFor(level)` rather than off `ORBIT_RING_COUNT`.

- **CS023 P1 — `ORBIT_FAST_RING` IS AN ARRAY NOW, AND FOUR TEST FILES WERE SILENTLY MIS-WIRED BY THE RENAME WITHOUT FAILING.** `generateOrbitLayout`'s parameter is `fastRingIndices` (plural, 0-based list); the retired scalar `fastRingIndex` key is simply an unknown property, so passing it makes `fastIdx` empty and **every ring in that layout comes out slow** with no error. `test-cs017-p1/-p2/-p3` and `test-cs018-p3` each carried a private `orbitTotalAt()` copy doing exactly that; all four are fixed, and none of them would ever have failed because they only read `.total`. **Any new code or test that builds generator arguments by hand must pass `fastRingIndices`, and must map `ORBIT_FAST_RING` through `.map(n => n - 1)` rather than subtracting 1 from it.** Related, and the reason CS023 P4's speed cap is specified the way it is (spec C14): **nothing may assume the fast ring is the outermost one or that there is exactly one** — at a list of `[1, 2]` the fastest satellite on the board is on ring 2.

- **CS023 P1 — THE `orbitCount` CLAMP IS NO LONGER REACHABLE FROM THE DEBUG PANEL, AND `clampShown` IS ONLY OBSERVABLE PAST THE REGISTRY'S OWN CEILING.** Spec C6: at 400/138 a fifth ring's satellite edge is 998 px against the size-9 world's 1,060 px budget, so `orbitEffectiveCount(5) === 5` and it is a requested SIX (1,136 px) that walks back down. The registry entry's `max` is 5, so **no value a player can dial clamps any more** — a five-ring request genuinely spawns five rings and the ramp completes at occurrence 5 (level 15). The hook is not dead and is still proven: `applyDebug` does not pre-clamp to the registry `max`, so `applyDebug("orbitCount", 6)` still round-trips to 5 through `debugShown`, `DEBUG` and the persisted blob (`test-cs021-p3.js` §F). Recorded so nobody reads "the clamp never fires" as "the clamp is broken" — and so a future geometry change that re-arms it inside [3,5] is recognised as a return to the old state rather than a new bug.

- **PRE-EXISTING NONDETERMINISM, measured during CS020 P1, not caused by it and deliberately NOT "fixed" — SPREAD RE-MEASURED AND WIDENED IN CS021 P1: `scratchpad/test-cs018-p4.js` now runs anywhere in roughly 403–423 assertions, run to run** (over 20 consecutive isolated runs: 16 × 421, 2 × 423, 1 × 407, 1 × 405; a later full sweep also produced 403), **always 0 failures**. The count is RNG-dependent — some loop in that file executes a few extra checks depending on the draw — and CS021 P1 widened the spread rather than causing it: the file drives real `nextWave()` calls, and every 3rd level now lays 40 orbiting satellites instead of `junkCount` scattered ones, which changes both the RNG stream and the debris the file's loops iterate. Still never a pass/fail flake. The original CS020 P1 measurement, for the record: **either 420 or 422 assertions** (17 × 420 and 3 × 422 over 20 runs), reproducing identically on the unmodified `09d443f` build under `git stash`.

- **SUPERSEDED, kept for history — the CS020 P1 wording of the entry above: `scratchpad/test-cs018-p4.js` runs either 420 or 422 assertions, run to run** (17 × 420 and 3 × 422 over 20 consecutive isolated runs), always **0 failures**, and the same spread appears on the unmodified `09d443f` build under `git stash`. It is an RNG-dependent assertion COUNT — some loop in that file executes a couple of extra checks depending on the draw — not a flaky pass/fail like `test-p5.js`. Harmless, but it means a sweep's total assertion count is not a stable number to diff on; diff per-file pass/FAIL counts instead. Worth folding into the same future administrative pass that seeds `test-p5.js`.

- **FRAGILE TEST HARNESS (measured during CS019 P1, not caused by it, deliberately NOT "fixed"): `test-cs017-p6.js` §A's "exactly two `breakChain` call sites" check passes by a cancellation, not by counting call sites.** It computes `matches(/(?<!function\s)\bbreakChain\(/g) - matches(/function breakChain\(/g)`. The lookbehind ALREADY excludes the definition, so the subtraction removes a second occurrence — and the number only comes out right because the shipped source happens to contain exactly one doc COMMENT with the substring `breakChain()` in it (in `update()`'s dock-offload block, "…/scatterChain() also zero deliveryCount but never reach here…"). The live arithmetic is therefore **3 matched − 1 = 2**, not "2 call sites". Two consequences, both real: **(a)** any new comment anywhere in the file containing the substring `breakChain(` inflates the count and fails the test — CS017 P7 tripped exactly this and had to reword a comment, and CS019 P1 wrote its comments to avoid it (say "breakChain" or "the top of breakChain", never with an open paren); **(b)** *deleting* that one comment would ALSO fail it, from the other direction. `test-cs019-p1.js` §A re-pins the same metric with a comment explaining the cancellation, so the trap is documented at both sites. Left as-is on purpose: repairing another phase's harness is outside a bug-fix phase's scope, and the check does still catch a genuinely added or removed call site. A future administrative session should replace it with a comment-stripped count.
- **FLAKY PRE-EXISTING TEST (found during CS018 P1, not caused by it): `scratchpad/test-p5.js` fails intermittently on one assertion — "C: hooking in time mode spends no budget (and still hooks)".** Measured at roughly **1 failure in 30 runs** (30 consecutive runs: 29 × `90 passed, 0 failed`, 1 × `89 passed, 1 failed`); **re-measured in CS019 P1 at a similar rate — 1 in 15 isolated runs, plus one hit inside a full sweep and none in two other full sweeps, all on the same lone assertion** ("C: hooking in time mode spends no budget"), with nothing in CS019 P1 touching the powerup-budget/time-mode path that file exercises. Still undiagnosed, still suspect-flake-until-reproduced. The file also **exits 0 even when an assertion fails**, so a CI-style `rc` check would miss it entirely. Nothing in CS018 P1 touches the powerup budget/time-mode path it exercises, and the phase's own file is deterministic. It is almost certainly an unseeded-RNG dependence in the test's setup rather than a game bug, but **that has not been diagnosed** — treat a lone `test-p5.js` failure in a later phase as suspect-flake-until-reproduced, and re-run before chasing it. Worth a targeted fix (seed the setup, and make it exit non-zero on failure) in a future administrative moment.
- ~~**TEST HARNESS BUG (found during CS017 P4, not caused by it): `test-cs017-p3.js` section (F)'s `git show HEAD:asteroids-deluxe.html` control is now stale and will fail on every future run, regardless of what a later phase changes.**~~ **RESOLVED in CS017 P6** — repointed to the fixed pre-P3 commit `683de82` instead of the moving `HEAD` ref, restoring the assertion's original meaning. Re-verified green through CS017 P7 (72 test files, 10,272 assertions, 0 failures). Original note, for history: It builds "the pre-P3 build" from `git show HEAD:...` and asserts the sawtooth levers (debris count, Hunter speed/turn) genuinely diverge from it — true only for the one session where it was written, when `HEAD` really was the pre-P3 commit. Once the P3 commit landed (which is what shipped this very test file), `HEAD` **is** the P3 build, so the control now compares "P3-committed" against "worktree" — and diverges only if the CURRENT session's uncommitted changes happen to touch the sawtooth group. CS017 P4 touches only the frozen saucer group, so it reproduces byte-for-byte on a totally clean checkout with zero P4 changes (`git stash` + rerun, confirmed before making any edit). **Not a P4 regression — a structural flaw in a test that pins to the moving `HEAD` ref instead of a fixed commit SHA.** Every other assertion in the file (1,929 of 1,930) is unaffected and still passes. Left unfixed because repairing another phase's test harness is outside this phase's scope (CLAUDE.md: build only what the phase prompt scopes); a future session should either re-point section (F) to a fixed commit hash (e.g. the P2 commit, which predates the sawtooth wiring) or accept it needs re-verifying by hand against a real pre-P3 diff each time it's touched.
- ~~**A carried Hunter fights at its spawn wave's difficulty forever (flagged at CS015 P3, unresolved).**~~ **RESOLVED as ACCEPTED DESIGN in CS017 P3** — Paul's explicit call. Speed and turn rate are still sampled once at spawn and never re-sampled, so a Hunter that survives across waves (and now across a *cycle boundary*) keeps its spawn cycle's values for life. The stale code comment that used to justify this with the false claim *"game.wave is fixed for a Hunter's lifetime (Hunters block wave-clear)"* — untrue since CS015 P3 removed Hunters from the wave-clear gate — was rewritten at the same site to say what is actually true and that the behaviour is intentional. GDD §2.5 no longer carries the "flagged, not resolved" note. Do not "fix" it by re-sampling per frame; if it ever needs revisiting, that is a conversational design decision, not a bug fix.

- **DELIBERATE, NOT A BUG (CS016 P2): Achievements and High Scores are unreachable mid-run and at gameover.** Single-parent IA (FORK-CS016-A) gave both the title menu as their sole parent, which **reverses two prior deliberate decisions** — CS010 P4 §8b nested High Scores under Options specifically so it would be reachable from pause, and CS012 P4 made Achievements Options-only for the same reason. Paul signed this off; recorded here so a future session doesn't "fix" it by re-adding either row to `MENU_OPTIONS`. The two mitigations that make it acceptable both exist and are untouched: the gameover screen renders the top-10 table inline with the fresh entry highlighted, and achievement unlocks fire as toasts during play. **If Paul dislikes it in playtest, the cheap reversal is to add the two rows back to `MENU_OPTIONS` and repoint the two Back destinations — the handlers are label-dispatched, so nothing else moves; but that reintroduces the two-parent problem this round designed away, so it wants the return-context decision made properly rather than a quick row re-add.**
- **INVARIANT, held through CS016 and to be preserved beyond it: at the title, `game.menu.screen` is never `null` (CS016 P2).** A null screen there renders a cursor-less title and reads `menuActive()` false — a screen dead to input. Enforced at three sites: the `game` literal boots `"titlemenu"`, `quitToTitle()` sets it (and clears `index`/`row`/`col`/`scroll`), and `closePause()` delegates to `returnToTitleMenu()` when `game.state === "title"`. **P3 adds `game.menu.modal` and P5 adds a tab field — both must be cleared/reset without nulling `screen` at the title.** **P3 DONE and the invariant held:** `modal` is cleared at seven sites, none of which touches `screen` except `closePause`/`quitToTitle`/`returnToTitleMenu`, which all still land on `"titlemenu"` (asserted in `test-cs016-p3.js` §J). **P5 DONE and the invariant held:** `achTab` is reset only in `gotoScreen` (which sets `screen` to a real name, never `null`) and in `setAchTab`/the two `game.menu` literals, none of which touch `screen` at all; `test-cs016-p5.js` §H drives back/confirm out of the viewer and asserts it lands on `"titlemenu"` unpaused. **The invariant is intact at the end of CS016 — carry it into the next round.**
- **LATENT (pre-existing, found in passing during CS016 P2 — deliberately NOT fixed): `startGame()`'s `game.menu` reset literal omits `scroll`.** `game.menu = { screen: null, index: 0, row: 0, col: 0, rebinding: null }` — the `scroll` field CS013 P3 added to the `game` literal is missing here, so it is `undefined` between `startGame()` and the next `gotoScreen()`. Harmless today because every reader of `game.menu.scroll` is only reachable via `gotoScreen`, which always sets it to 0. Left alone because fixing it is outside this phase's scope. **Directly relevant to CS016 P3 and P5**, which both add a new `game.menu` field: add each one to BOTH the literal and this reset, or inherit the same divergence. **P3 FOLLOWED THIS:** `modal` is in both literals, with a comment at the reset site citing this note; `test-cs016-p3.js` §A asserts it by regexing every `game.menu` literal in the source, so a future field added to only one will not silently pass. **P5 FOLLOWED THE SAME RULE:** `achTab` is in both literals, with the reset site's comment citing this note, and `achTabIndex()` additionally defends against a bad/missing id by falling back to tab 0. **`scroll` itself is still the lone offender and is still deliberately unfixed** — P5 left it alone rather than widening its scope (fixing it is one word in one literal and remains harmless-to-do; the next session touching `startGame()`'s menu reset should just take it).
- **LIMITATION, deliberate and logged (CS016 P3): a modal opened at `game.menu.screen === "titlemenu"` would take input but draw nothing.** `drawModal()` hangs off `drawMenu()`, and `drawTitleMenu()` deliberately bypasses `drawMenu()` (chrome-less title, CS016 P2). **No code opens one there today** — both consumers live on `"controls"` and `"root"`, and the Controls screen reached *from* the title still routes through `drawMenu`, which is covered by the tests. Not fixed, because the fix (a second `drawModal()` call in the title branch) is untestable dead code until something needs it. **A future phase adding a title-menu confirmation must add that call**; the gap is recorded in the GDD §2.16 modal subsection and the `draw()` Architecture Map row as well as here.
- **PLAYTEST (CS016 P3): the modal's ten `MODAL_*` numbers are first-pass, browser-unverified.** `MODAL_MIN_W` (420) / `MODAL_MARGIN` (28) / `MODAL_H` (300) / `MODAL_TEXT_SIZE` (20) / `MODAL_TEXT_Y` (110) / `MODAL_ROW_Y` (172) / `MODAL_ROW_STEP` (46) / `MODAL_ROW_SIZE` (24) / `MODAL_HINT_Y` (272). The height/step/size trio is `drawRootMenu`'s one-for-one on purpose (so the dialog reads as the same family of panel), which means **the dialog and the play root are exactly the same height and both vertically centred** — over the root they overlay precisely, and the dialog is only visibly a *dialog* because its measured width is wider (~632px for the Quit prompt vs. the root's ~478px) and it fully occludes what is beneath. That reads fine headlessly; whether it reads as "a dialog appeared" or "the panel changed" is a browser judgement. If it reads wrong, the cheapest fixes in order are: shrink `MODAL_H` (the two rows + prompt need far less than 300px), or offset the box vertically. **Also unverified: the prompt is a single un-wrapped line** — there is no word-wrap, so a prompt much longer than the current 48-character Quit line will keep widening the panel toward the viewport edges rather than breaking. Fine for both shipped prompts; a constraint to remember before adding a third.
- **PLAYTEST (CS016 P4): the Save row's dim rendering and the Difficulty lock's help text are first-pass, browser-unverified.** Whether "Save" reads clearly as *"coming soon"* rather than *"broken"* at a glance is a look-call — the only signal is colour (`COLOR.dim`, same value used everywhere else for "unavailable"), no strikethrough or icon. `DIFFICULTY_LOCK_HELP` ("Difficulty can't be changed during a run.") is the prompt's suggested wording verbatim — **Paul's to change**; editing it is a one-line constant edit next to `DIFFICULTY_ROWS`, no code path depends on its exact text. Both are headless-solid (rendered and asserted via the recording-canvas tests) but not eyeballed in a browser.
- **PLAYTEST (CS016 P5): the two-tab Achievements layout is headless-solid but browser-unverified.** Three look-calls to eyeball. (a) **`ACH_COL_W` is 1140**, so a row's name sits at the far left and its right-aligned status/medal string sits ~1140px away at the far right, with the description under the name — that is the intended "status column on the right" read, but at ×1.5 with a short name it may look like two unrelated things on one row. The cheap fixes if so: shrink `ACH_COL_W` (the gutter constant is the only edit), or — since the full width removed the CS015 P2 collision risk that put status on its own line in the first place — put the status back on the name's baseline. (b) **`ACH_TAB_STEP` (260) and `ACH_TAB_Y` (108)** place "WEEKLY" and "LIFETIME" left-aligned from `ACH_COL_X`; at 15×`ACH_SCALE` monospace, "WEEKLY" is ~81px wide, so the gap between the two labels is large — it reads as a tab strip headlessly, but whether it reads as *selectable tabs* rather than two headings is a browser judgement. If it doesn't, the cheapest signals are a `◄ ►` pair flanking the active label or a separator between them; both are `drawAchievements`-only edits. (c) **The Lifetime tab now scrolls 923px** (~13 `ACH_SCROLL_STEP` presses top-to-bottom, up from the old half-column's much shorter travel) — the `▲`/`▼` cues still track it correctly, but whether 20 rows of continuous scroll feels tolerable without paging is only answerable by using it. `ACH_SCROLL_STEP` (60) is the knob.
- **PLAYTEST: the title menu's four y-offsets are first-pass numbers (CS016 P2).** `TITLE_MENU_Y` (342) / `TITLE_MENU_STEP` (38) / `TITLE_MENU_SIZE` (24) / `TITLE_MENU_HINT_Y` (530). Rows land at 342/380/418/456. **The tightest measurement on the screen is the 24px gap between the last row's baseline (456) and `BEWARE THE HUNTER SATELLITE`'s (480)** — a 24px row's descender reaches ~463, so it clears, but only just. If it reads cramped in a browser the cheapest fixes in order are: drop `TITLE_MENU_STEP` to 34–36, raise `TITLE_MENU_Y` a few px, or move the flavour line down (it is currently pinned at its original `VIEW_H/2 + 120` on purpose, since §1.2 called it unchanged).
- **Real-`localStorage` persistence is unverified across the board (v1.9 + v2.0 + v3.0 P7/P8).** Both the settings key (`afd_settings_v1`) and the achievements key (**`afd_achievements_v2`** — bumped in P7 from `_v1`, **unchanged in P8** since the seven new counters ride the existing known-key load/save) round-trip headlessly against a stub, but real cross-session persistence can only be confirmed in a browser running the file locally (the claude.ai artifact sandbox has no `localStorage`). All access is `typeof`-guarded + try/caught, so the sandbox worst case is "progress just doesn't persist," never a crash. **The P7 `_v1→_v2` migration (keep counters, re-derive tier badges, drop old unlock sets — FLAG A-2-a) is best-effort and browser-unverified: a returning v2.1 player's lifetime counters should survive and tiers recompute, but their current-week weekly unlocks may reset once.** **Top persistence playtest item.**
- **A few starter achievements are intentionally hard or degenerate (v2.0), by design of the starter pool — not bugs.** *Waste Not* ("zero canisters expired in a game") is near-impossible to earn legitimately (a lineage sheds 39–66 canisters and the chain caps at 12), yet is trivially satisfiable by dying before anything decays — it's implemented literally per F9's list. Curating/balancing the pools (retiring degenerate ones, tuning thresholds) is a **post-playtest pass**, deliberately not done this phase.
- **Weekly achievements reset each calendar week (v2.0), by design.** Unlocking a weekly one records it only for the current ISO week; when the week rolls over the 5 change and weekly-unlock progress starts fresh (lifetime never resets). This is the intended "weekly challenge" model — flagged so a mid-week reset (which won't happen — the week only changes on Monday UTC) isn't mistaken for data loss.
- ~~**Achievements viewer is reachable only from Pause → Options (v2.0).**~~ **RESOLVED in v3.0 P4** (and superseded again by CS016 P2 — the viewer's sole parent is now the title menu; see the first Known-issues entry above). The Options/Achievements system menu now opens directly from the title/gameover screen (keyboard "O", controller B); its root offers Achievements as a first-class entry. The old "start a game to browse achievements" limitation (and the parallel F8 "menu reachable only via pause" note) is closed.
- **Gamepad rebind drops that action's analog axis (v1.9), by design.** Capturing reads a button, not a stick direction; Return to Defaults restores the axis.
- **Audio unlock on a controller-only start is browser-dependent (v1.8).** Best-effort `AudioSys.init()`/`resume()` on Start/A, but the Web Audio autoplay policy generally wants a keyboard/pointer gesture — a controller-only player may hear nothing (incl. the new achievement fanfare + menu blips) until one keyboard press.
- **Soft weapon cap (v1.7), by design:** a Triple volley can transiently exceed the 12-bullet cap by up to 2.
- **Performance watch (v1.4/v1.6; sharper as of v3.2 P3):** heavy `shadowBlur`; garbage-dense late waves are the stress case (a cleared *timer-spawned* Hunter lineage sheds 66 canisters; a scrap-born one sheds 0). **v3.2 P3 removed the decay timer that used to bound coexistence — garbage is permanent now**, so late waves can carry more standing salvage; render is viewport-culled (draw cost is bounded), and the O(n²) `coalesceGarbage` pass is the watch item (FLAG D-2). Levers: a global glow toggle, then the drop-volume constants (`DEBRIS_GARBAGE`/`HUNTER_SMALL_GARBAGE`), then (only if a stress test shows it) a spatial hash for coalescence. The v2.0 achievement layer adds negligible per-frame cost (≈17 simple `cur()` compares, already-unlocked short-circuit).

## Balance notes

- **CS026 P5 — FOUR FIRST-GUESS BANNER NUMBERS, NOW KNOBBED, HAVE NEVER BEEN SEEN IN PLAY.** `levelBannerTime` (2.2 s), `levelBannerFade` (0.5 s), `levelBannerSize` (72 px) and `levelBannerY` (24 px) are all CS025 P5's untested guesses, tuned by eye against nothing. Does "Level N" at that size, held that long, with that fade, read as *briefly, large, easy to read, fading* — or too big, too long, too easy to miss in a busy frame? It sits at screen centre, where the ship usually is — does it ever obscure something that matters at the instant a level starts? All four are sliders now, in the GLOBAL registry section right after `Start level`. **Report the numbers you land on.**

- **CS026 P4 — TWO FIRST-GUESS DELIVERY-FLOATER NUMBERS, AND ONE READOUT REMOVED WITH A NAMED RISK.** `deliveryFloatRise` (300 px/s) and `deliveryFloatLife` (0.55 s) are both untested guesses picked from the arithmetic alone — at the shipped `DOCK_OFFLOAD_INTERVAL` (0.05 s), consecutive floaters separate by 15 px and each travels 165 px before fading. Watch a full 24-canister haul, ideally with a Magnet running (that's what puts incidentals back-to-back too): does it read as a rising column, or does it still smear at the top of a long haul? Both knobs live in the DELIVERY registry section, right after `dockComboGrace`. **Separately, and not fixable by these two knobs:** the HUD `COMBO n/24` row is gone, and it was the only thing showing the delivery **denominator** — if the cargo ceiling starts feeling arbitrary without a number attached to it, that is the first thing to reconsider, not the floater tuning.

- **CS026 P3 — THE EARLY WORLD IS 44% VISIBLE INSTEAD OF 25%, AND NOT ONE COMBAT NUMBER MOVED.** Levels 1–5 run at 1920×1080 rather than 2560×1440. The lever table, the spawn counts, the speeds, the scores and the drop weights are all untouched and pinned so at every level 1..200 — this is a **legibility** change, not a difficulty one. What it actually changes is the tail: the farthest a body can be from the ship drops from **1,469 px to 1,101 px**, and the viewport covers nearly half the board instead of a quarter, so hunting the last two or three smalls is a shorter walk. The band is `DEBUG.earlyWorldLevels`, default 5, and **0 turns it off completely** for a same-session A/B. Two things to watch for at the gate that the numbers cannot answer: whether five levels is the right length for the on-ramp (it is a knob, and 1–3 was the other candidate in §2.3), and whether the **one** world change at 5 → 6 reads as a moment or as a glitch — the field is re-homed around the ship, so a Hunter that was 900 px behind arrives 660 px behind on the far side, which is the `dmax` shell working as designed but is also the most likely thing to feel odd.

- **CS024 P7 — ENGINE IS NOW 10 SECONDS OF THRUST, AND IT IS THE ONLY NUMBER GATE B MOVED.** `ENGINE_BURN_SECONDS` 5.0 → **10.0**, per Q11. The unit is unchanged and is the thing to keep in mind when reading it: **seconds of FORWARD THRUST, not wall time** — the tank drains only on frames where thrust is actually applied, so 10 s of fuel is far more than 10 s of powerup in practice, and a player who coasts keeps it indefinitely. It does not taper as the tank empties (deliberate — a tapering multiplier makes the last second read as the effect breaking rather than ending), and it banks on re-pickup like every other budget. **The full budget table as it now ships:** Rapid **40 trigger pulls** · Triple **30 pulls** (a 3-fan is one pull) · Magnet **40 canisters hooked** · Guard **3 intercepts** · Engine **10.0 s of thrust**. The 5.0 it replaced was FLAG-CS024-f's own admission — a conversational example, never a design call — so this is the first tuned value the Engine has ever had.

- **CS024 P7 — THE ODOMETER SHIPS EXACTLY AS P6b LEFT IT, WHICH IS WORTH STATING AS A POSITIVE RESULT RATHER THAN AN ABSENCE.** Gate B was run against a panel with 51 live lever rows (floor / ceil / steps for all 17) and came back "fine" on the sawtooth reading as breathing (Q7), on the carry being legible (Q8), on three chains breathing on different periods (Q9), and on every lever's floor and ceiling (Q10) — with the standing instruction to report a number, not a yes/no, in force the whole time. **No number came back for any lever.** The two structural oddities the tables contain are therefore CONFIRMED as shipped, not merely unexamined: `ufoAppearFreq` cycles forever and never permanently tightens (level 100 reads as level 1), and the nine UFO step counts stay uneven on purpose, staging speed → fire rate → evasiveness → shot velocity → **accuracy last** across L33–L65.

- **CS024 P6b — THE UFO CHAIN, PLOTTED, SO THE NUMBERS ARE ON THE PAGE AND NOT ONLY IN THE TABLE.** Every value is `leverState(n)`; the driver wraps every 8 levels and every dependent steps on that boundary and only there. `ufoAppearFreq` 25 s at L1 → 12 s at L8 → 25 s again at L9, forever. `ufoFlightSpeedSmall` 150 / 165 / 180 / 195 / **210** at L1 / 9 / 17 / 25 / 33, then flat. `ufoFlightSpeedBig` 100 → **150** on the same schedule. `ufoFireFreqSmall` 1.8 → **0.6** by L41 (a 3× faster reload); `ufoFireFreqBig` 1.8 → **0.7**. `ufoDirChangeSmall` 1.8 s → **0.7 s** between zigs by L49; `ufoDirChangeBig` 2.2 → **1.0**. `ufoShotSpeedSmall` 320 → **470 px/s** by L57; `ufoShotSpeedBig` 300 → **430**. `ufoAccuracySmall` 30° → 27.25 → 24.5 → 21.75 → 19 → 16.25 → 13.5 → 10.75 → **8°** at L65. **The chain finishes at level 65 and the game is flat on the UFO axis after that** — junk saturates at L41 and hunters at L33, so past 65 all three chains are pure sawtooth on their drivers with nothing escalating underneath (FLAG-CS025-b; gate question 4 in `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS025.md` §4 owns it, and the fix if wanted is a step-count increase, a table edit, no mechanism change).

- **Every UFO lever's debug slider got FINER, automatically, and that is the derivation working rather than a registry change.** `leverKnob()` computes a knob's `step` as one odometer step of its own curve, so restaging e.g. `ufoAccuracySmall` from 4 steps to 9 moved its slider from 7.33°/press to 2.75°/press. `def`/`min`/`max` are untouched on all nine. Worth knowing before Gate B: retuning a UFO lever live now takes more presses to cross the same range.

- **CS024 P6 — WHAT EVERY POWERUP NOW COSTS AND WHAT IT IS WORTH, since Gate B Q11/Q12 are asking exactly this.** Rapid **40 trigger pulls** (at `RAPID_FIRE_COOLDOWN` 0.09 s that is ~3.6 s of continuous fire, or far longer in practice), Triple **30 pulls** (a 3-fan is ONE pull — 90 bullets), Magnet **40 hooked canisters** counted at the hook (a 6-piece clump costs 6, so a clump is neither a bargain nor a penalty against six singles), Guard **3 intercepts** (`DEBUG.chainGuardIntercepts`), Engine **5.0 seconds of FORWARD THRUST** (`DEBUG.engineBurnSeconds`). **The Engine number is the one with no precedent to compare against** — every other budget is a count of discrete player actions, but fuel is consumed continuously and only while a specific key is held, so 5.0 s is a first-pass figure rather than a tuned one. All five BANK, with no ceiling.

- **The Engine's magnitude did not change and is deliberately not on the table this phase:** `ENGINE_MASS_MULT` is still a flat 0.5 (towed mass halved), now readable live as `DEBUG.engineMassMult` (range 0–1, step 0.05, LOWER is stronger). It does NOT taper with remaining fuel — a tapering multiplier would make the last second of the powerup indistinguishable from not having it, which reads as the effect breaking rather than ending.

- **Timed expiry's removal changes the FEEL of Rapid and Triple more than the numbers suggest, and that is Q12's subject.** The shipped default until this phase was the 15-second clock; the count budgets existed but were opt-in through a Difficulty toggle most runs never touched. So for a typical player this phase does not "remove a mode" — it swaps the one they had for the one they did not. A held-fire player exhausts 40 pulls in under four seconds; a deliberate one can carry the same pickup across a whole level.
