- **CS024 P4 — THE WHOLE ODOMETER'S SHAPE, MEASURED FROM THE SHIPPED TABLE RATHER THAN READ OFF THE SPEC, BECAUSE GATE B WILL NEED IT.** Per lever: **first level at `ceil`** / **what happens after**. `junkCount` 3→12 over 10 steps: **L10**, then wraps to 3 at L11, L21, L31, L41… forever. `junkSpeedLarge/Medium/Small` 60→110 / 95→165 / 140→240: all **L41**, then **plateau permanently**. `coalescePause` 5.0→1.5 over 8 steps: **L8**, wraps at L9, L17, L25, L33… `hunterSpeedMedium/Small` 60→110 / 90→160: both **L33**, then plateau. `ufoAppearFreq` 25→12 over 8 steps: **L8**, wraps at L9, L17, L25, L33… `ufoFireFreqBig/Small` 1.8→0.7 / 1.8→0.6: both **L25**, then plateau. `ufoDirChangeBig/Small`, `ufoShotSpeedBig/Small`, `ufoAccuracySmall`: all **L97**, then plateau. **Note the spec's §2.4 prose says the junk speeds top out "at level 40" and the hunter speeds "at level 32"; the shipped tables put them at 41 and 33.** The prose computed wraps × period without the +1 — consistently, in both places — and the code follows the *semantics* the phase prompt gave rather than either prose figure. Nothing was retuned to match either number.

- **CS024 P4 — `ufoFlightSpeedBig`/`Small` DO NOT PLATEAU. THEY SAWTOOTH FOREVER, EVERY 32 LEVELS, AND THAT IS THE MOST SURPRISING THING IN THE SHIPPED TABLE.** They reach `ceil` at **L25** and then wrap back to `floor` at **L33, L65, L97, L129…** — because they carry (to the direction-change and shot-speed levers) and only a lever with an EMPTY `carriesTo` plateaus. So the UFO's flight speed drops from 150 back to 100 at level 33, having been at its fastest eight levels earlier. This is `FLAG-CS024-d`'s two-generation carry doing exactly what it was specified to do, and it is the concrete thing Gate B question 9 ("do three chains breathing on different periods feel rich, or arrhythmic?") should be watched for. **The one-line fix if it reads badly is to empty either lever's `carriesTo` and give its children their own `everyNLevels`** — a table edit, no code change. It also means the two second-generation groups take until **L97** to saturate, which is deep but is the direct consequence of the same structure.

- **CS024 P4 — GATE A's RETUNE MADE THE HUNTER PIPELINE SUBSTANTIALLY SLOWER, AND ALL THREE NUMBERS MOVED THE SAME WAY.** Inert window 3.0 → **5.0 s** (+67% of a piece's life spent unable to clump), mutual attraction radius 180 → **160 px** (−21% of the *area* a piece can find a partner in, since the search is a disc), pull 40 → **30 px/s²** (−25% acceleration once it does). Compounding, that is a large change to the only Hunter producer in the game — which is what Paul was after, and it is worth stating plainly because **§2.5 froze `HUNTER_COALESCE_COUNT` (12), `DEBRIS_GARBAGE` (3) and `HUNTER_GARBAGE` (3/2/1) as non-levers**, so the *supply* side is untouched and every bit of the slowdown lands on the *clumping* side. If Gate B finds hunters now too rare, the radius is the cheapest thing to move (area scales quadratically) and `coalescePause`'s `ceil` of 1.5 s is the thing that is supposed to bring them back as levels climb.

- **CS024 P3 — THE GARBAGE ECONOMY HAS NO SINK LEFT EXCEPT THE PLAYER AND A CEILING NOBODY SHOULD SEE.** Before this phase a canister had three exits: recycled, coalesced, or **aged out**. Decay was doing most of the work, and it is gone. The three exits now are recycled, coalesced, or **culled at 220 live pieces** — and the cull is meant to be unreachable in practice, which makes the player the only real sink. Two numbers are worth watching together: `DEBRIS_GARBAGE` (3 per satellite kill, at every tier, so one large satellite's full line yields 3 + 9 + 27 = **39 canisters**) and `HUNTER_GARBAGE` (3/2/1, so a full Hunter lineage yields 3 + 3×2 + 9×1 = **18**). §2.5 froze both as constants rather than levers. At a level-1 `junkCount` of 3 a fully-harvested field is ~117 canisters from the satellites alone — **over half the soft ceiling from one level**, with nothing removing them but the dock. That arithmetic is exactly why Gate A question 3 exists.

- **CS024 P3 — `GARBAGE_SOFT_MAX = 220` IS A PURE GUESS (FLAG-CS024-c) AND IT IS THE FIRST NUMBER TO MOVE.** It is a live knob (`garbageSoftMax`, range 20–1000, step 10), as is `garbageHardMax`. **The frame-budget arithmetic says the cost of raising it is quadratic, and here are the actual numbers so a retune is not blind:** the O(n²) pair walk costs 24,090 visits/frame at 220, 44,850 at 300, **99,900 at 450** and **~500,000 at 1,000**. Doubling the ceiling roughly quadruples the pass. If Gate A says the field reads as noise, lower it and the pass gets cheaper for free; if it says the cull is visibly firing, raising it is affordable to about 300–350 and expensive beyond that. **The escape hatch, already recorded as out of scope: a spatial grid for `coalesceGarbage()` (spec §7) would make the ceiling near-free and is a CS025 conversation.**

- **CS024 P3 — `LARGE_HUNTER_MAX = 100` IS A BACKSTOP, NOT A BALANCE NUMBER, AND IT SHOULD NEVER BIND.** The cap it replaces topped out at 12 and was a real difficulty lever (CS018 P4 expressed rising Hunter pressure as count). 100 is deliberately far above anything reachable: each one costs 12 neglected canisters, so a full ceiling would take 1,200 pieces on a board capped at 220 live. **If the overflow-destroy branch ever fires in normal play, something upstream is wrong** — most likely `coalescePause` (the HUNTER chain's driver in §2.4, wired in P4/P5) tuned far too short. Under §2.4 Hunter pressure is carried by that pause and by the two pursuit-speed levers, not by this number; treat 100 as a runaway guard and do not retune it as a lever.

- **CS023 P4C — THE SHELL IS 18 SATELLITES, FLAT, AND THE OCCURRENCE CURVE IS NOW COMPLETELY INERT.** Ring counts are **3 / 4 / 5 / 6** at every occurrence from 1 to 21 (they were 3/3/4/5 rising to 3/4/4/5 at P1's step), so the ramp totals **3 / 7 / 12 / 18** and the level-21 peak is **31** (18 ring + 13 field). That is +3 on the shell and +2 on the peak, bought by wider rings at an unchanged 0.12 density — **no density was touched to compensate** (TRAP 3), and 31 is still well under CS022's 84 peak and its 27-satellite level-3 shell. The four density sliders remain the first lever if the gate says 18 is a grind. Separately: `ORBIT_GAP_MULT`'s occurrence curve now buys **zero** satellites across the whole game rather than one, so **ring COUNT 1 → 4 across levels 3–12 is the only escalation axis the archetype has left**, and levels 12–63 are flat by construction. The curve is recorded, not retuned and not deleted (spec C5's own instruction) — the four densities are what would make it audible again.

- **CS023 P4C — THE DRIFT'S SPEED CAP IS 314.2 px/s AND IS NOW PURE GUARD RAIL.** `maxOrbitSpeed()` rose with ring 4's radius (814 → 1,000, so 255.7 → 314.2 px/s) while the target radius moved outward and shortened every fall, so **nothing in the shipped game reaches it**: the longest armed falls are 1,403 px on an orbit level (~290 px/s) and 669 px on a field one (~200 px/s), and a piece would have to start ~2,445 px from the dock to be clamped, which neither world allows. **This is headroom for gate question 12, not dead weight** — `debrisDriftAccel` can be raised to ~35 px/s² before the cap starts limiting the longest fall at all, and well past that before it limits a typical one. It costs one dot product per drifting body and it is derived, so a gate retune of `orbitAngVel`/`orbitFastMult`/`orbitCount` carries it automatically.

- **CS023 P4C — THE DRIFT RECLAIMS LESS OF EACH WORLD THAN IT DID, AND THAT IS THE ONE PLACE THIS PHASE MADE SOMETHING WORSE (spec C16c).** The trigger moved 814 → 1,000 px with the rings, so the dead zone the drift sweeps back in shrank from 75% to **62%** of an orbit world and from 46% to **29%** of a field world. Paul chose derived-not-pinned deliberately and this is the price. **Not compensated for here.** If the gate reports the field drift has gone quiet (question 14), the fix is decoupling `DEBRIS_DRIFT_TRIGGER_R`/`DEBRIS_DRIFT_TARGET_R` from the ring geometry — two lines, and a CS024 decision rather than a P5 retune.

- **CS023 P4 (renamed by P4b, spec C15) — THE DRIFT HAS EXACTLY ONE TUNABLE NUMBER, AND THE CAP IS DELIBERATELY NOT IT.** `DEBRIS_DRIFT_ACCEL` (was `ORBIT_GRAVITY_ACCEL`) **30 px/s²** is the one pure guess in the mechanic (FLAG-CS023-d) and is a live slider (`debrisDriftAccel`, was `orbitGravityAccel`, 0–200 step 5); **0 disables the drift outright**, which is the gate's A/B for question 11. The arithmetic Paul should have in hand at the gate: at 30 px/s² a piece needs **8.5 s and 1,090 px of fall** to reach the cap, so **the cap only binds for pieces starting beyond ~1,766 px from the dock** — real on a 3840×2160 orbit torus whose farthest point is 2,203 px away, but not the common case, and **never at all on a field level, whose farthest point is only 1,469 px** (P4b, C15 — the longest possible field fall is 792 px, arriving at ~218 px/s against the 255.7 px/s ceiling). If the pull reads as too slow, **raise the acceleration first**; the cap is not the thing holding it back for a typical straggler on either archetype. The two derived radii (814 / 676) are NOT knobs and should not become knobs — they are ring 4's and ring 3's radii, and a retune of `ORBIT_INNER_RADIUS`/`ORBIT_RADIUS_STEP` moves them automatically, on every level, not just orbit ones. **The cap is not a knob either, and that is the design**: `maxOrbitSpeed()` derives it from `orbitAngVel` × `orbitFastMult` × the ring radii, all of which already have sliders, so moving the orbit motion at the gate moves the drift's ceiling with it — **255.7 px/s today, and any retune of gate question 4's two velocity knobs changes that number**. A fourth knob here could only ever disagree with the three it was copied from.

- **CS023 P4 — `debrisBounceRestitution` IS NOW A LIVE SLIDER (0–1.5, step 0.05, default 1.0), WHICH IT HAD TO BE FOR GATE QUESTION 9 TO MEAN ANYTHING.** `debrisBounce` reads `DEBUG.debrisBounceRestitution` rather than the constant; `DEBRIS_BOUNCE_RESTITUTION` stays the registry's `def`, so shipped behaviour is byte-identical until the slider moves and a P5 retune of the constant carries the knob automatically. `DEBRIS_BOUNCE_MIN` (40 px/s) deliberately got **no** knob — the spec specced none, and it is a separation floor rather than a feel dial. See this session's block for why the wiring is a derivation from the established registry convention rather than an invention.

- **CS023 P2 — THE BOUNCE'S THREE KNOBS, TWO OF THEM PLAYTEST MATERIAL AND ONE OF THEM STRUCTURAL.** `DEBRIS_MASS` **{3: 9, 2: 3, 1: 1}** is NOT a tuning knob — it is fixed by mass conservation through the 3-way split (a large becomes three mediums; a medium becomes three smalls), so changing it makes a shatter create or destroy mass and breaks the one property the number was chosen for. `DEBRIS_BOUNCE_RESTITUTION` **1.0** and `DEBRIS_BOUNCE_MIN` **40 px/s** are both first-pass and both browser-unverified, exactly as `SHIELD_BOUNCE_RESTITUTION`/`SHIELD_BOUNCE_MIN` were at CS021 P1b. **The measured consequences, so the gate has numbers rather than a yes/no:** a rail satellite sweeping into a parked free one throws it at exactly twice its own speed — **83.8 px/s off ring 1, 338.0 off the inner fast ring (ring 2), 141.6 off ring 3, and 511.5 off the outer fast ring (ring 4)**. That 511.5 is the fastest anything in the game will shove a satellite, it is nearly double the 255.7 px/s cap CS023 P4's drift is derived from, and it is the number to watch if the shell reads like a pinball table. Lower the restitution if a hard hit reads as a trampoline; lower the floor if a graze shoves too much. **Neither is a debug slider yet — `debrisBounceRestitution` is P4's knob (FLAG-CS023-o), so a gate answer here is currently a source edit, not a live drag.** The one genuinely new feel this phase adds and the arithmetic cannot judge: every orbit-level split now visibly breaks its three children apart instead of leaving them travelling as one, which is a change to the commonest event in the archetype.

- **CS023 P2 — THE SECOND O(n²) PASS COSTS ABOUT THE SAME AS THE FIRST, AND THE COMBINED HEADROOM IS STILL ENORMOUS.** At level 21 — the peak wave, 29 satellites — a real progressive full harvest peaks at **33,930 debris pair-checks** over 262 bodies and **33,901 `coalesceGarbage` checks** over 287 canisters, in a 0.6 ms median `update(dt)` with 615 peak entities. The two passes are within 0.1% of each other in count, which is a coincidence of this shell rather than a law: the debris population is bounded in closed form by the split cascade (29 → 87 → 261) while the garbage population is governed by how fast coalescence merges. **`coalesceGarbage`'s own worst frame moved 36,816 → 33,901, DOWN**, because bouncing debris spreads the garbage it later emits — a small, real, and slightly surprising side benefit. **The debris ceilings are 250,000 (structural) and 100,000 (realistic), both derived before measuring**, from the cascade's C(377,2) = 70,876 hard bound rather than by scaling CS022's coalesce measurement — the entity-count ratio does not carry across to garbage (CS023 P1 measured six times what it predicts) but does carry across to debris, which has no merge dynamics. **The realistic gate now runs at 33.9% of budget, which is the first frame-budget gate in this project with genuine teeth** — CS022's ran at 7.4% and CS023 P1's at 0.46%. **Do not tighten either coalesce ceiling** (standing rule); a future phase that adds a third pass, or a density retune upward, is what the remaining slack exists for. The lever order is unchanged: the four density sliders first, then `garbageAttractRadius`, then a global glow toggle, then a spatial hash as its own changeset. **The browser question is unchanged and still unanswerable headlessly** — `draw()` is not in the measured loop.

- **CS023 P1 — THE WHOLE ORBIT ARCHETYPE'S TUNING SURFACE AS IT NOW SHIPS, AND WHAT IS DELIBERATELY FLAT.** Geometry: `ORBIT_INNER_RADIUS` 400, `ORBIT_RADIUS_STEP` 138, `ORBIT_RING_COUNT` 4 → radii **400 / 538 / 676 / 814**, outer satellite edge **860 px** against a **1,060 px** budget (200 px of headroom — a fifth ring at 998 px now fits and is reachable via the debug knob; a sixth at 1,136 does not). Density **flat 0.12** on all four rings → counts **3 / 3 / 4 / 5** at occurrence 1 becoming **3 / 4 / 4 / 5** from occurrence 3, lanes **746 / 1035 / 970 / 931 px**. **Every ring is a wide ring now** — the tightest lane on the board is ring 1's 746 px, against CS022's 101 px. Ring 1 clears the permanent 88 px dock by 266 px; the inter-ring radial corridor is 46 px, deliberately narrower than the 65 px in-ring fairness floor. Motion: `ORBIT_ANG_VEL` 6 °/s and `ORBIT_FAST_MULT` 3.0 unchanged, with `ORBIT_FAST_RING` now `[2, 4]` → tangential **41.9 / 169.0 / 70.8 / 255.7 px/s**, alternating slow/fast from the inside out. **The escalation axis is the RAMP, not density** (spec C5): ring count 1 → 4 across levels 3–12, then **deliberately flat** at 16 ring satellites through level 63, with the level total breathing 19–29 on the junk cycle alone. **All four densities and both motion values are live sliders in the hidden Debug panel's ORBIT section — retune in-session at the gate and report the numbers, don't answer yes/no.** The three questions the numbers cannot answer: whether ring 1 alone at level 3, 266 px off the dock, reads as a shell arriving *around* the player (the whole point of the inversion) or as too little to be a set-piece; whether the flat 12–63 plateau reads as repetitive once the ramp has finished (the four density sliders are the dial if so); and whether a 746 px lane still reads as a *ring* at all rather than as four loose satellites in a circle.

- **CS023 P1 — THE FRAME BUDGET IS NOW ENORMOUSLY SLACK, AND THAT SLACK IS THE POINT RATHER THAN AN ACCIDENT.** At level 21 — still the peak wave, now 29 satellites rather than 84 — a real progressive full harvest peaks at **36,816 `coalesceGarbage` pair-checks** over 297 standing canisters, against the derived worst-case ceiling of 8,000,000 (**0.46%**) and the tighter realistic ceiling of 500,000 (7.4%). CS022 measured 49,203 at 84 satellites, so the drop is real but far less than 5× — coalescence keeps up either way and standing garbage is what dominates. **Neither ceiling is tightened**, per the standing Known-issues rule: they are derived from arithmetic rather than fitted to a measurement, and the headroom exists precisely so CS023 P2/P3 can add two new O(n²)-shaped passes on top (spec FLAG-CS023-g projects ~30k pair-checks for the debris↔debris pass at a fully-split level-12+ board). **The deliberately-unreachable BLITZ probe fell from 8.27M checks / 372 ms to 975,046 / 36 ms with the shell**, which is the honest measure of what a spatial hash would still buy and is now a much weaker argument for one. **The lever order if the browser says otherwise is unchanged:** the four density sliders first, then `garbageAttractRadius`, then a global glow toggle, then the spatial hash. **The open question is still the browser one and still unanswerable headlessly:** `draw()` is not in the measured loop and `shadowBlur` scales with these counts — though at a 5× smaller shell it is a materially smaller worry than it was at CS022's 84.

- **CS023 P1 — A LEVEL-3 ORBIT LEVEL IS NOW LIGHTER THAN THE DENSEST FIELD LEVEL, WHICH REVERSES A PROPERTY THAT HELD THROUGH CS021 AND CS022.** Measured, not predicted: `test-cs021-p1.js` §K's probe is unchanged code, and a level-3 orbit wave now spawns **8** satellites (3 ring + 5 field) against a level-4 field control's 13, peaking at 317 simultaneous entities to the field level's 420 — a 0.75× ratio where CS021 measured 1.67×. The archetype's first occurrence is a *quieter* level than an ordinary one now, and its escalation is entirely in the ramp. Whether that reads as a gentle introduction or as an anticlimax is a gate question the arithmetic cannot answer; it is the direct consequence of pairing the ~5× count cut with an inverted ramp, and pulling ring 1's density up is the cheapest single change if it lands badly.

Play levels 1 → 12, including at least one level in the 8–11 band. No doc obligation carries forward beyond what the gate itself resolves — P6 owns the retune, the version bump and the full doc sweep once the answers are in.

**⛔ CS026 P4 HAS LANDED — DELIVERY FEEDBACK IS SPREAD, KNOBBED AND QUIETED; NEXT IS P5.** The delivery floaters separate at the shipped defaults (`deliveryFloatRise` 300 px/s, `deliveryFloatLife` 0.55 s — both first-guess, both playtest-gate knobs), the HUD `COMBO n/24` row is gone (closing FLAG-CS020-i's readout, not its claim), and the incidental floater is dimmed/shrunk rather than folded into the towed tally. **Two things the gate should specifically be asked, per this phase's own recorded risks:** whether the cargo ceiling (`/cargoMax`) is still legible without the readout's denominator, and whether 300 px/s / 0.55 s reads as "a rising column" or still smears at a full 24-canister haul with a Magnet running. Both are also recorded under `## Balance notes`. **P5 should assume the DELIVERY registry section now ends `dockComboGrace, deliveryFloatRise, deliveryFloatLife` before JUNK** — no doc obligation carries forward (GDD §2.10 is P6's).

**⛔ CS026 P3 HAS LANDED — THE WORLD HAS TWO SIZES AGAIN, AND `resizeWorld()` IS BACK ON THE CRITICAL PATH.** Levels 1–5 are 1920×1080, level 6 on is 2560×1440, and the six-step carried-entity re-homing pass now fires **once per run**, at the 5 → 6 boundary, with a full field crossing it. **P4 and P5 should assume nothing about the world period**: a test that stages a board must ask `worldDims(game.worldSize)` rather than reading the module-load `WORLD_W`/`WORLD_H` snapshot, or set `DEBUG.earlyWorldLevels = 0` and say why — see this session's recap (8a) for which treatment fits which case, and (8b) for what the wrong one costs. **P6 owes one doc obligation from this phase**: the GDD §2.11.1 world-size rewrite (the GDD still describes a single 2560×1440 torus, true only from level 6 on). `DIFFICULTY-LEVERS.md` needs nothing — the world size is not a lever and must not become one; `test-cs026-p3.js` §G pins `LEVERS` and `leverState` byte-identical to the parent at every level 1..200 in both directions. **The gate should be asked whether five is the right band length and whether the single 5 → 6 change reads as a moment or a glitch** — both are recorded under `## Balance notes`, and the knob at 0 is the clean A/B for the whole feature.

**⛔ CS026 P2 HAS LANDED — THE SPLIT COUNT IS NOW A LEVER, AND P6 INHERITS A THIRD OBLIGATION FROM IT.** The debris tree is 2-way through level 10 and 3-way from 11 on, forever; level 1 is 21 bodies rather than 39. **P6's doc sweep owes three things, itemised in this session's recap entry above so they are not rediscovered:** a `DIFFICULTY-LEVERS.md` §3 row for `junkSplit` (adding it will trip `test-cs026-p2.js` §J's deliberate "no row yet" assertion — repoint it, don't delete it), the GDD's present-tense "3-way split" passages at lines 55/56/118 and the §3 Flow-functions row at 547, **and nothing else** — the Hunter passages at 67/78/204 say 3-way and are still CORRECT. **Half of those passages are true; the sweep is a debris/Hunter distinction, not a find-and-replace.** ⛔ **And this phase is the largest single lever on the garbage density ceiling** (`GARBAGE_SOFT_MAX` 220): a 3-way level 10 shed 624 canisters, a 2-way sheds 336. §1.7 says the split count and the ceiling must be answered in the **same gate** or neither answer means anything — the blocking playtest gate between P5 and P6 is where that happens.

**✅ CS025 IS COMPLETE — P0 through P5 all landed, `GAME_VERSION` `"1.0.0.25"`, gate closed and clean.** The changeset's whole subject was one defect — a full tow chain turning a live Magnet into a Hunter factory at the player's own position — and it is fixed in two halves: **suppression** while cargo is full with a 250 ms resume (P1), and an **outward burst plus `coalesceDelay` re-arm** for the cloud already gathered at the instant the chain fills (P2), where the re-arm is the fix and the velocity is the legibility. Q3 confirmed it in play. **P3's scoop tell shipped and was backed out by the gate (P5); the mechanic ships with no on-ship indicator, deliberately.** P4 narrowed the drop-not-queue non-negotiable with Paul's sign-off, and P5 made the level announcement critical and gave it a centre-screen banner. All docs are swept; `STATUS.md` covers CS023 + CS024 + CS025 (three changesets — the window; **no prune was due**).

**⛔ CS026 P1 HAS LANDED — THE TEST-INFRASTRUCTURE PHASE. NO GAME CODE TOUCHED, SO NOTHING IN THE BUILD MOVED.** `scratchpad/_phase-ref.js` and `scratchpad/_seeded-random.js` now exist, and **every later CS026 phase should use them** rather than re-inlining git plumbing: `PARENT_SHA` stays a hardcoded literal in each phase's own test file, `ownCommits(PARENT_SHA, "cs-26 pN:")` resolves that phase's own commit, `changedFiles(PARENT_SHA, sha)` (or `changedFiles(PARENT_SHA, null)` for the pre-commit working-tree reading) writes the "nothing else moved" pin. **A phase whose test drives the game and asserts on counts should `installSeed(SEED)` at the top, before its factory** — that ordering is the requirement, not a style choice. **Next: P2, the split count becomes a carried `junkSplit` lever** (FORK-CS026-A: 2 through L10, 3 from L11).

**⛔ P6 INHERITS TWO OBLIGATIONS FROM P1, AND ONE OF THEM IS A DECISION.** (a) FORK-CS026-H's resolution (c) makes the **closing phase** assert the suite runs with **zero skips** — grep for the single exported literal `SKIPPED (no git history)`, which is why it is exported rather than written out per file. (b) That assertion is only worth having once the files that *should* skip actually can: **ten more suite files still hard-fail on a shallow clone**, listed in `## Known issues`. Converting them is mechanical and all the same shape as the four already done; not converting them is also defensible. **Decide it explicitly — do not let it be decided by accident.**

**A standing test-authoring rule, learned the expensive way this changeset.** Any trap of the form "nothing else moved" must be written **against the phase's own parent commit (a fixed sha), in that phase's own file** — never against `HEAD`. Nine files had to be repaired in P7 alone for exactly this, on top of the two the interrupted session had already fixed. A `HEAD`-relative pin does not test the phase; it tests whatever happens next.

**⛔ CS024 P6f IS LANDED AND THE NEXT STEP IS STILL GATE B — WITH ONE NEW QUESTION AND A CHANGED EARLY GAME.** The registry is **72** rows now (69 + `hunterCapMax`, `hunterCapLevelsPerStep`, `heldClumpMax`, all three in the HUNTER section under `Hunter last-stand speed`). **What a Gate B run will actually feel differently:** levels 1–2 can now hold **one** large Hunter and no more, rising one per two levels to a plateau of six at level 11 — where before the ceiling was effectively unbounded. And a clump that saturates while the cap is full no longer vanishes: it **sits there wearing a pulsing teal halo**, waiting for a slot, and can be scooped or shot apart in the meantime. **Both are retunable live** — if levels 1–3 now read as too quiet, raise `hunterCapLevelsPerStep` toward 1; if the mid-game plateau is too soft, raise `hunterCapMax`. Report the numbers you land on, per the standing instruction.

**Three things P7 must not rediscover from this phase.** **(1) The cap is not a lever and must not be made one** — §2.5's not-a-lever list records it, and the doc sweep should carry that wording into the GDD rather than filing it under the odometer. **(2) `drainHeldClumps()` honouring `game.sweepPause` was a judgement call, not a spec requirement** (recorded in the P6f entry above) — if Gate B says held clumps should snap in immediately after a Super Mega Delivery, that is one line, but the CS018 P9 rationale it would be overriding should be read first. **(3) `test-cs024-p3.js` and `test-cs018-p4.js` now assert the OPPOSITE of what their own phases shipped** on the overflow rule, each with an inline note saying so. That is correct — a test file tracks the build, not its phase — but a doc sweep reading those files for history will find the reversal, not P3's original claim; `archive/`-bound STATUS history is where P3's own account lives.

**⛔ CS024 P6d IS LANDED AND GATE B WAS ALREADY THE NEXT STEP — BUT THE SPOT-CHECK STEP CAN NOW JUMP STRAIGHT THERE.** P6d adds `startLevel`, a GLOBAL debug knob (registry now **68** rows, up from 67) that seeds `startGame()`'s wave counter so a run can begin at any level 1–99 instead of being played up to. **It is gate tooling, not a lever, and it VOIDS RECORDING**: any run started above level 1 sets a sticky `game.debugRun` flag (a visible `"DEBUG RUN"` HUD marker confirms it) that suppresses the three persistence points — no high-score entry, no achievement-unlock save, no lifetime-stats save — while leaving in-memory score/stats/counters and the HUD/gameover screen behaving exactly as a real run would. **Use it for the gate's own spot-check**: Gate B's questions 9 and the levels-41+ flatness question (Playtest asks, below) ask about levels 41–99, which a played-through run reaches only after ~40+ minutes; dragging `startLevel` to e.g. 41, 60 or 90 and starting a fresh run samples that band directly — remembering the fidelity limit (a level-N field with a level-1 ship: no scoop upgrades, no banked powerups, no towed load) before drawing conclusions about anything that depends on player progression rather than pure spawn tuning. Gate B's seven questions and the **Play levels 1 → 45** ask (a real, played-through run, for anything progression-dependent) both stand exactly as P6c left them — full briefing in `## Playtest asks`.

**One design note P6c leaves for whoever touches the odometer next.** `leverState()` has **no in-game caller** — every consumer reads `liveLevers()`. That is deliberate and documented at the site: `leverState` is the pure statement of the shipped ramp, the bare-context test surface, and the reference the untouched-panel invariant is pinned to. A cleanup pass that deletes it as dead code deletes the specification and the only thing that can catch the panel drifting off it.

Next is CS024 P6** (`IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-CS024.md`): count-only powerup expiry (deleting `powerMode()`/`powerDuration()`/`game.powerFx`/`POWERUP_DURATION`/`MAGNET_DURATION` and the three dual-mode settings fields) and Engine-as-fuel (`ENGINE_BURN_SECONDS = 5.0`, `ENGINE_MASS_MULT = 0.5`, both new debug knobs, decremented in `Ship.update()`'s thrust branch — gated on forward thrust actually being applied that frame, never in the main timer block). **Four things P6 should know before it starts.** **(1) THE `POWERUPS` REGISTRY HEADER DOES NOT EXIST YET** (see Known issues) — P6 adds it at the same time as its first knob, not before. **(2) THE DEBUG-OVERRIDE MECHANISM P5 BUILT (`def: null`, `DEBUG.<id> ?? leverState(w).<id>`) IS UNCONFIRMED, NOT SETTLED** — it's a real design decision this session made and flagged, not yet validated against Paul's actual expectation for what "a live slider" means at Gate B; P6 doesn't touch it, but whoever runs Gate B should read that Known-issues entry first. **(3) `game.powerBudget.engine`'s decrement site is the one place TRAP 2 from THIS phase's own prompt matters for P6**: P5 was explicitly told not to touch `powerMode`/`powerFx` (verified untouched, `test-cs024-p5.js` §H), so P6 is starting from the exact pre-P5 shape of that system. **(4) `POWERUP_DROP_TYPES` stays append-only** — Engine joins it as a new entry, never inserted, per the standing rule (§2.14 of the GDD, reaffirmed at CS017 P6).

**CS024 P1–P4 ARE ALL LANDED — the orbit/drift excision, the dead-constant sweep, the three gameplay removals plus the density ceiling, and now the odometer. Next is CS024 P5** (`IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-CS024.md`): wire every lever to its call site through `leverState(game.wave)` AT THE POINT OF USE, split the UFO's four per-size quantities apart (§4.6), and rebuild the debug registry around one knob per lever (§5). **Gate A is CLOSED and its one code-carrying answer is already in** — P5 inherits no retune. **Five things P5 should know before it starts.** **(1) THE FROZEN BLOCK IS THE WORK LIST.** `FROZEN_JUNK_COUNT` and its eight siblings sit in one banner-marked block in the constants section; every one of the nine has exactly one consumer, and when the ninth is repointed the block is deleted whole. Nothing else in the build reads them. **(2) `junkSpeedMul()` IS NOT A REPOINT, IT IS A DELETION.** The shared-ratio derivation (`px / 70`, applied to all three sizes) is exactly what §4.6 removes — the three sizes become three independent levers — so its two call sites in `nextWave()` and `destroyDebris()` each read their own lever instead, and the helper goes. **(3) `logDifficultySnapshot()` NEEDS THE COLUMN LIST DECISION P4 DELIBERATELY DID NOT MAKE.** `phase` and `rel` currently log `null` because the structure they described is deleted; P5 owns whether `DIFFLOG_FIELDS` loses them or gains lever columns. **(4) TWO WEAKENED CONTROLS ARE WAITING TO BE PUT BACK** — `test-cs023-p2.js` §H's vacuity floors and `test-cs017-p3.js` §G's binding-level control, both marked ⛔ in place, both restorable the instant the junk count is live again. **(5) `leverState` IS PURE AND MUST STAY PURE** — it takes a level, reads no `game` and no `DEBUG`, and the bare-context slice test enforces that structurally. The debug override belongs at the CALL SITE (`DEBUG.junkCount ?? leverState(w).junkCount`-style, the standing "constant supplies the def, consumer reads DEBUG" idiom), never inside the function.

**CS024 P1, P2 AND P3 ARE ALL LANDED — the orbit/drift excision, the dead-constant sweep + `leverScale` bake + `freqJitter` freeze, and now the three gameplay removals plus the density ceiling. That rationale is now void — the value 3.0 has no live argument behind it — so P4/P5 should re-derive it rather than inherit it.

P4's `leverState(wave)` inherits that rule verbatim, and the slice test is already written to prove it for whatever function occupies that block.

**CS023 P1–P4c ARE ALL LANDED — the shell retune, the bounce primitive, mutual collision damage, the inward drift, the correction that makes the drift run on every level, and now the ring respacing. Next is the ⛔ PLAYTEST GATE, which is BLOCKING: P5 must not run until its questions are answered in the Playtest asks section below.** All code phases are in; nothing else in CS023 is buildable without Paul's answers. P4c changed three of the gate's own questions, and `IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-CS023.md` already carries them (P4c touched no design doc — TRAP 1): the shell is 18 satellites rather than 15 (question 2b), the radial corridor is 108 px rather than 46 (question 3), and the drift's reclaimed area shrank on both archetypes (question 14). Question 12's advice to raise `debrisDriftAccel` first is now backed by a measured 314 px/s cap that does not bind until ~35 px/s².

**CS023 P1–P4b ARE ALL LANDED — the shell retune, the bounce primitive, mutual collision damage, the inward drift, and now the correction that makes the drift run on every level. Next is the ⛔ PLAYTEST GATE, which is BLOCKING: P5 must not run until its fifteen questions are answered in the Playtest asks section below.** All code phases are in; nothing else in CS023 is buildable without Paul's hands on it. When the gate closes, P5 is retune + version bump (`"1.0.0.22"` → `"1.0.0.23"`) + doc sweep, with no new logic. **P5 should note the drift is no longer orbit-only when it writes up the gate answers — a field-level playtest can now show it too, not only an orbit-level full harvest.**

- **⛔ THE DRIFT IS THE ONE MECHANIC IN CS023 THAT A NORMALLY-PLAYED LEVEL NEVER SHOWS, ON EITHER ARCHETYPE NOW.** Its trigger fires only once nothing at all is left inside 814 px of the dock — on an orbit level that means the whole shell plus every split child in that radius; on a field level (P4b, C15) it means the level's own scatter population, which in practice clears late in a level's life for the same reason. **At least one level played to a FULL HARVEST is needed, orbit or field, or the gate cannot answer questions 11, 12 or 13.** Setting `debrisDriftAccel` (renamed from `orbitGravityAccel` by P4b) to 0 gives the A/B.

- **P5 should carry four things its own prompt does not name.** (1) The `debrisBounceRestitution` wiring derivation — the knob is live because `debrisBounce` reads `DEBUG.*`, which is worth one sentence in GDD §2.19 beside the 44 → 46. (2) The trigger-dominates-target finding in Known issues — §2.13.1 should describe the 676–814 band rather than quoting two radii as if both were independently observable, and should use the `DEBRIS_DRIFT_*` names (P4b renamed them from `ORBIT_GRAVITY_*`, spec C15). (3) Moving `ORBIT_ANG_VEL`/`ORBIT_FAST_MULT` still moves the drift's cap; **moving `ORBIT_INNER_RADIUS` or `ORBIT_RADIUS_STEP` still moves the drift's two RADII as well as the ring geometry**, unchanged by the rename — same warning, new names. (4) **The drift is now a universal, every-level mechanic, not an ORBIT-section curiosity** — P5's GDD write-up should say so plainly (C15) rather than describing it only under the orbit archetype's own section, even though its two knobs stay physically inside the ORBIT debug block (append-only discipline, not a scoping claim).

- **The registry-count pin lives in the same twelve files it did after P4, plus the id `orbitGravityAccel` itself needed repointing to `debrisDriftAccel` in eleven of them** (`test-cs018-p4`/`p6`/`p7`, `test-cs020-p1`/`p1b`, `test-cs021-p1`/`p1b`/`p3`, `test-cs022-p3`, `test-cs023-p1`/`p2` — `test-cs023-p4.js` itself, the twelfth, was extended in place rather than repointed). Any future changeset RENAMING a debug knob id, not just adding one, should **grep for the id string across the whole `scratchpad/` tree**, not just the registry-count pins — an `/^orbit/i`-style filter is exactly the kind of assertion a rename silently changes the count of.

**CS023 P1 AND P2 ARE BOTH LANDED — the shell retune, and now the bounce primitive and the satellite↔satellite pass. Next is P3** (mutual collision damage: ship rams and UFO impacts), then P4 (the inward drift), then the ⛔ playtest gate, then P5. **What P3 and P4 inherit from P2, so they do not have to rediscover it:**

- **⛔ P3 MUST SETTLE THE SAUCER'S MASS BEFORE IT WRITES `debrisBounce(a, s)`.** See the first Known-issues entry: a `Saucer` has no `size` field, so the free/free branch would NaN a free satellite. Spec §4.8 gives no saucer mass, so it is a design call rather than a fix, and the rail-borne case working correctly by accident is what makes it easy to miss.
- **`debrisBounce`'s free/rail branch is the one to lean on for the UFO rule, and it already does what spec §4.6 describes.** A saucer carries no `orbitCenter`, so against a rail-borne satellite the saucer takes the free role and the rail is left untouched — exactly §4.6's "a rail-borne satellite is untouched and a free one is knocked off course". The helper is order-agnostic (both argument orders are asserted equivalent), so `debrisBounce(a, s)` and `debrisBounce(s, a)` behave identically.
- **The P4 SEAM is a comment at the top of `debrisBounce`, not a stub.** One line — `a.drifting = b.drifting = false;` — drops in before the rail dispatch and covers both of the interrupts that converge there. No body carries the field, no branch reads it, and `test-cs023-p1.js` §I still pins that `drifting` does not appear in the source outside comments.
- **The free/free floor and overlap push have forms the spec did not specify**, and both are argued at the function: the floor acts on the RELATIVE separation speed as an equal-and-opposite impulse pair (so momentum survives it), and the overlap push is split in inverse proportion to mass. Neither is a knob and neither should be "simplified" into `shieldBounce`'s world-frame/absolute form, which is only well defined when the partner cannot move.
- **The frame-budget headroom after P2 is 33.9% of the realistic debris ceiling and 6.8% of the coalesce one.** P3 adds no new O(n²) pass (its ship and saucer loops are O(n) and O(n·m) over small arrays) and P4's drift pass is O(n). **Neither existing ceiling may be tightened**, and if a later phase does add a third quadratic pass it should derive its own ceiling the same way P2 did: from a closed-form population bound, not by scaling a measurement of a differently-governed population.
- **Two spec sentences are now wrong and P5's doc sweep owns them** — §7's "every field level's play comes out byte-identical" and §4.6's "writes only to `a`". Both are in Known issues with the reasoning.

**What P2/P3/P4 inherit from P1, so they do not have to rediscover it:**

- **`ORBIT_FAST_RING` IS AN ARRAY AT EVERY READ SITE.** P4's `maxOrbitSpeed()` must scan every ring (`angVel × radius`) rather than assuming the outermost is fastest — spec C14 — and P1's own test file already carries the `[1, 2]` counterexample as a live control, so a "use the outer ring" implementation will fail `test-cs023-p1.js` §D as well as P4's own file. `generateOrbitLayout`'s parameter is `fastRingIndices` (plural, 0-based); the retired scalar key is silently ignored rather than rejected.
- **The two derived drift radii P4 needs are `ORBIT_INNER_RADIUS + 3 × ORBIT_RADIUS_STEP` = 814 and `+ 2 ×` = 676**, and spec §4.7 is explicit they must be written as those derivations rather than as literals. At the shipped geometry ring 4 sits at *exactly* 814, which is what makes FLAG-CS023-a's trigger ("nothing within ring 4's radius") never block itself.
- **The frame budget has enormous headroom and P2's gate should be derived against the NEW shell, not CS022's.** 36,816 pair-checks against an 8M ceiling; FLAG-CS023-g's ~30k projection for the new pass is the same order as the existing one, and the two together are still far under. Do not tighten either existing ceiling.
- **`test-cs023-p1.js` §I pins the four TRAPs positively, including that P2/P3/P4's future symbols do NOT exist yet** (`debrisBounce`, `drifting`, `ORBIT_GRAVITY_*`, `maxOrbitSpeed`, `DEBRIS_MASS`, `destroySaucer`'s parameter). Each of those assertions must be repointed to its positive successor by the phase that lands it — the standing mirror-image treatment, exactly as CS022 P3 did to CS022 P1's TRAP 3.
- **The registry is at 44 and P4 takes it to 46** (`orbitGravityAccel`, `debrisBounceRestitution`), **appended after the existing ORBIT entries** per FLAG-CS023-o. Three files pin the count today (`test-cs018-p4/-p6/-p7` and friends — grep, do not trust this list; the surface has been wider than predicted five rounds running).
- **P5's doc sweep inherits a large GDD job and should not have to rediscover it.** §2.11.1 needs the world size 16 → 9 and the `dmax` 1380 → 1020 correction (spec C7). §2.13.1 needs the whole archetype restated again — radii 460/736/1012/1288 → **400/538/676/814**, outer edge 1,334 → **860** against a **1,060** budget, dock clearance 326 → **266**, the density curve → **flat 0.12**, the ramp direction → **innermost first**, the per-occurrence total table → **8/9/24/25 rising to a 29 peak**, `ORBIT_FAST_RING` → **a list**, and the new 46 px inter-ring corridor fact. `DIFFICULTY-LEVERS.md`'s **orbit world size**, **orbit ring ramp** and **orbit-gap-multiplier** rows all move (the last one to record C5: the curve is kept, running, and no longer observable), and §5 of the spec names two further new rows for P2/P4's mechanics.

**CS020 IS COMPLETE — all phases landed, per `IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-CS020.md`.** P2 (retune + version bump + doc sweep) landed the session it shipped in — see the phase log above for the full breakdown; the playtest gate between P1b and P2 was cleared with all four questions answered "no change" (Q3, the cap, came back "Better" — positive feedback with no code implication), so no delivery-combo constant moved. **CS021 is now the open changeset (see above).** **The CS020 archive move IS already done** — verified by `ls archive/` in CS021 P1: `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS020.md` and `IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-CS020.md` are both there and repo root now holds only the CS021 pair, so `IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-CS021.md`'s P5 item 5 ("verify, do not re-run") will find nothing to do.

- **What CS020 P1b leaves for whoever touches the delivery combo next.** (a) **The counter is bounded STRUCTURALLY, not by a clamp** — `deliveryCount <= game.cargoMax` falls out of the towed-hook reset plus the pickup gate's `cargoMax` test, and there is deliberately no `Math.min` anywhere. Any future code that increments the counter outside the offload block, or that lets a chain exceed `cargoMax`, silently breaks the guarantee; `test-cs020-p1b.js` §F is the assertion that will catch it. (b) **The two rules are load-bearing TOGETHER and neither is sufficient.** The window is generous *because* the hook reset bounds the counter — widening the window is safe, dropping or ungating the reset is not. `test-cs020-p1b.js` §H is the annulus-farm assertion that fails the moment the reset stops discriminating. (c) **The `!inRing` guard on the reset is the fairness half.** Resetting on every hook makes a magnet grab at the dock kill the player's own run mid-offload — pinned by §G's ±1 px pair. (d) **The re-arm is at the RING (`+40`), not the `+10` offload radius**, on purpose: once inside the neighbourhood the run is safe indefinitely so the player can take their time closing the last 30 px. §D's 12-second annulus loiter is what fails if that moves. (e) **`DOCK_COMBO_GRACE` and `DEBUG_VARS.dockComboGrace.def` must move TOGETHER** (`def` is the const × 1000); §A asserts they agree, which is exactly the mismatch P2's retune could otherwise introduce.

- **What CS020 P1 leaves for whoever touches the dock next.** (a) **Chain nodes now carry a `towed` field, and its default is `!== false`, not truthiness.** Any new code that constructs a chain node must either set it or accept the TOWED default deliberately; any new code that READS it must keep the `!== false` form, or the 22-file hand-seeded-node surface silently reclassifies. (b) **The tag radius and the combo-reset radius are one constant on purpose** (`DOCK_NEIGHBORHOOD_PAD`). Splitting them apart re-opens the farmable annulus; if either ever needs to move, both move. (c) **The `+10` offload radius is a genuinely different number** and was left as a bare literal deliberately — do not "finish the job" by hoisting it into the same constant. (d) **Everything keyed on `game.deliveryCount` is now correct by construction**, because incidentals never advance it. Do not add a second guard at the SMD trigger, the P8 reward tiers or either achievement latch; that would create two sources of truth for one rule.

- **`GDD-VERSION-HISTORY.md` has no CS018 entry.** Noticed while appending this session's CS019 entry (grepped, not found) — CS018 P10's own doc sweep never added a consolidated round entry for CS018, unlike every round before and after it. Not backfilled this session (out of a bug-fix changeset's scope); worth a one-line administrative fix whenever a future session is touching that file for its own reasons.

## Playtest asks (Paul — can't be checked headlessly)

**⛔ CS026's PLAYTEST GATE IS OPEN AND BLOCKING. CS026 P6 MUST NOT RUN until the eight questions below are answered here, inline.** All code phases (P1–P5) are landed. The questions are reproduced **verbatim** from `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS026.md`'s "§10 — ⛔ THE PLAYTEST GATE" section, which is the single source; write the answers inline under each one (`Paul says: …` is the established style), or paste them into the next session's opening message and tell it to record them here first.

**THE STANDING INSTRUCTION: for anything on a slider, retune live and REPORT THE NUMBER YOU LANDED ON, not a yes/no. "Fine" is a complete answer** — four closing phases on record have been clean (CS020 P2, CS022 P4, CS024 P7, CS025 P5). Do not manufacture changes to justify the gate.

**⛔ THE BRIEFING — WHAT CHANGED ACROSS P2–P5.**

**P2 — the debris split count is now a carried `junkSplit` lever: 2-way through level 10, 3-way from level 11 on, forever.** Level 1 is 21 bodies instead of 39; a 2-way level 10 (84 bodies) is still busier than a 3-way level 1 (39) was. `destroyHunter()` still splits 3-way always — only debris moved. Slider: the JUNK section's split knob (set back to 3 for the clean A/B).

**P3 — levels 1–5 run in a 1920×1080 world instead of 2560×1440; level 6 on is unchanged.** The farthest a body can be from the ship drops from 1,469 px to 1,101 px, and the viewport covers 44% of the board instead of 25%, so hunting the last two or three smalls at the end of an early level is a shorter walk. No combat number moved. Knob: `earlyWorldLevels` (GLOBAL), default 5 — 0 turns the whole feature off for a same-session A/B. Watch the 5 → 6 transition itself: the field re-homes around the ship (the `dmax` shell), so something that was 900 px behind arrives 660 px behind on the far side.

**P4 — delivery floaters spread instead of stacking, the HUD `COMBO n/24` row is gone, and an incidental delivery (hooked while already parked at the dock) is dimmed/shrunk rather than sharing the towed tally's colour.** At the shipped knobs, consecutive floaters separate by 15 px (was 1.5) and each travels 165 px before fading (was 33). Sliders: `deliveryFloatRise`/`deliveryFloatLife` (DELIVERY section, right after `dockComboGrace`). **Named risk:** the HUD row was the only thing showing the `/cargoMax` denominator — the dock floaters show none.

**P5 (this phase) — the level banner's four look-call constants are now GLOBAL knobs, so the gate below can finally put numbers to them.** Nothing about the banner's behaviour changed, only that it is now retunable live. Sliders: `levelBannerTime`/`Fade`/`Size`/`Y` (GLOBAL section, right after `Start level`).

**THE GARBAGE DENSITY CEILING (§7, live since CS024, never moved) IS URGENT THIS SESSION SPECIFICALLY BECAUSE OF P2** — the split change moved the shed volume by roughly half at every level, so the ceiling that was tuned against the OLD volume needs re-checking against the new one in the SAME session, or neither answer means anything. Sliders: `Garbage soft max` / `Garbage hard max` (GARBAGE section). Frame budget bounds any raise: 24,090 pair visits/frame at 220 (the current soft max), 44,850 at 300, ~99,900 at 450 — quadratic, so ~300–350 is affordable; beyond that wants a spatial grid, not a bigger number.

**WHAT TO PLAY: levels 1 → 12 minimum, including at least one level in the 8–11 band** — `junkCount` is 10–12 there and the tree is four times level 1's, so the pacing question (Q1) is not answerable from levels 1–3 alone. `startLevel` (GLOBAL) can sample a deep level directly but gives a level-N field with a level-1 ship — no scoop upgrades, no banked powerups — so it is the wrong tool for anything reading on scoop level or banked powerups.

**CS026's eight questions, verbatim from `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS026.md` §10:**

**Q1 — pacing, the changeset's central bet.** With the split at its new value, does a level *end* at about the right time? Play level 1 and a level in the 8–11 band and say which one is wrong, if either. Slider: the split knob (JUNK section) — set it back to 3 for the clean A/B. **Report the number.**

**Q2 — did the tail fix do the work?** With the split change alone the last few satellites are still somewhere in the other 75% of the world. Does the tail fix make "where is it" into "go there"? If it is the chevron: does ≤3 remaining feel like the right trigger, or should it show earlier? Slider if built. **Report the number.**

**Q3 — the score curve.** Whatever FORK-C resolved to: does the run feel like it pays what it should, and does the hull repair often enough? This is the question that catches a wrong answer to FORK-C.

**Q4 — garbage density, and it must be answered in this session.** The split moved the shed volume by roughly half at every level. Do you ever *see* a canister vanish? (The cull is silent by design; catching one means `garbageSoftMax` is too low.) Does a level-10 field read as salvage-rich or as noise? Sliders: `Garbage soft max`, `Garbage hard max`. **Report the number.** ⛔ Do not raise past ~350 without saying so — beyond that the O(n²) walk, not readability, is what binds.

**Q5 — the delivery payoff, the other central bet.** Deliver a full haul. Does the escalation *read* now — can you see it climbing? Is the dock the right place for it, or does it want to be nearer the ship? And the specific failure to watch for: does it collide with `SALVAGE BONUS` (at 8) or `MAX HAUL` (at 24)?

**Q6 — the incidental case.** Park at the dock with a Magnet and let it feed you. Whatever FORK-G resolved to: does that read as sensible, or as the same smear in a new place?

**Q7 — the level banner, first sight.** It has never been playtested. Does "Level N" at **72 px**, held **2.2 s** with a **0.5 s** ramp each end, read as *briefly, large, easy to read, fading*? Too big, too long, too easy to miss in a busy frame? It sits at screen centre, where the ship usually is — does it ever obscure something that matters at the instant a level starts? **All four are sliders as of P5. Report the numbers.** Also: `P` exports a frame with the banner across it for the first 2.2 s of every level (FLAG-CS026-c). Correct, or annoying?

**Q8 — the look-call backlog, in words.** No sliders here, so words are the answer: the modal dialog's proportions; the title menu's row spacing (the tightest in the game); the two-tab Achievements layout; and specifically — **does the Options screen's dim "Save" row read as *coming soon* or as *broken*?**

All six questions were answered by Paul and applied by CS025 P5; the answers are preserved inline below, under each question, as the record of what was decided and why.** **The numbers came back CLEAN — nothing moved** (250 ms / 120 px/s / 45° all confirmed, the Hunter-on-top-of-you problem reported gone, the defensive fill loop reported fine). **Two answers were not numbers**, both were put to Paul before any code was written, and both were approved: **Q4 backed out CS025 P3's scoop energy tell outright**, and **Q6 asked for the level announcement to be made critical and given a large fading centre-screen "Level N"** — both shipped in P5. **⛔ Q6 did NOT report a stale `cargo_full`**, so the phase prompt's inherited-defect clause never fired.

Kept for the record of the gap between CS025 P5 shipping the banner and CS026 P5 giving it sliders.]** ~~THE ONE THING CS025's GATE COULD NOT ANSWER, BECAUSE IT DID NOT EXIST WHEN THE GATE WAS WRITTEN — THE LEVEL BANNER HAS NEVER BEEN PLAYTESTED.~~ P5 built it *out of* the gate's own Q6 answer, so it arrived after the gate closed and no one has yet seen it in motion. All four were look-call constants, not registry rows, so there was no slider — until CS026 P5.

**THE STANDING INSTRUCTION, RESTATED BECAUSE IT IS WHAT MAKES THE ANSWERS ACTIONABLE: for anything driven by a debug slider, open the panel, retune live, and REPORT THE NUMBER YOU LANDED ON — NOT A YES/NO.** "Too fast" costs the next session a guess; "I settled at 140" costs it nothing. **"Fine" is a complete answer** — a clean gate means P5 is bump-and-sweep only, which has now happened three times (CS020 P2, CS022 P4, CS024 P7). Do not manufacture changes to justify the gate.

**⛔ THE BRIEFING — WHAT CHANGED ACROSS P1–P4, BECAUSE THREE OF THE FOUR FEATURES ARE INVISIBLE UNTIL CARGO IS FULL WITH A MAGNET RUNNING.**

**P1 — the Magnet's attraction is INACTIVE while the tow chain is full**, and resumes `magnetResumeDelay` (250 ms) after a slot opens. The defect: at `cargoMax` the pickup gate blocks every hook, so a live Magnet dragged garbage onto the ship where it had nowhere to go — and coalescence is a separate system that kept running, so that loitering cloud converted into a Hunter **at your own position**. There is no HUD change; the intended tell is P3's.

**P2 — on the frame the chain BECOMES full with a Magnet active, every garbage piece within `MAGNET_RANGE` is pushed outward on a randomly-fanned vector and has its `coalesceDelay` re-armed.** Suppression alone does not disperse a cloud that has *already* gathered. The re-arm is what actually prevents the Hunter; the velocity is what makes it legible.

**P3 — while the pull is genuinely live, the scoop mouth strokes magnet-blue** (`POWERUP_COLOR.magnet` `#8ab6ff`) at a heavier width and blur; a banked-but-suppressed Magnet draws the ordinary `COLOR.dock` mouth. **At `scoopLevel` 0 a small fixed nose-V appears while pulling and nothing at all while not** — a tell nobody has seen yet.

**P4 — `health_low`, `health_relief` and `cargo_full` can no longer be silently lost.** They may wait on a three-deep queue instead of being dropped, they are exempt from the 1.2 s post-line gap, and a waiting line is spoken **only if its own condition is still true** when the channel frees — otherwise it is discarded, never spoken late. Pre-emption is unchanged and `cargo_full` is still the lowest priority, so it can never cut off "hull integrity is critical." **Nothing to tune here — Q6 is a does-it-work question, not a slider question.**

**THE THREE NEW SLIDERS, ALL IN THE POWERUPS SECTION, APPENDED AFTER `Engine mass multiplier` (registry 72 → 75, and P4 added none):**

- **`Magnet resume delay`** — `ms`, default **250**, range 0–3000, step 50. **⛔ At 0 it disables the WHOLE full-cargo suppression, not just the delay** — the build then behaves exactly as CS024 shipped, which makes it the clean on/off A/B for P1 (and a genuine surprise if you expect "resume instantly"; see `## Known issues`).
- **`Magnet full-cargo push`** — `px/s`, default **120**, range 0–600, step 10. **At 0 the burst's velocity is disabled but the `coalesceDelay` re-arm still fires**, which is the honest A/B for Q2: it leaves P1's suppression and P2's re-arm and removes only the shove.
- **`Magnet push spread`** — `°`, default **45**, range 0–180, step 5. A **per-piece half-angle**, not a whole-burst rotation: 0 is a coherent expanding shell, 180 is fully random scatter.

**None of the three is a lever** — no floor/ceil/steps rows, no `▼`/`↳`, no `carriesTo`. They are ordinary knobs and persist through `afd_settings_v1.debug` like everything else. **`r` resets the selected row; the "Overrides Applied" toggle at the top of the panel A/Bs the whole registry against shipped defaults without losing your edits.**

**THE FOUR LOOK-CALL CONSTANTS BEHIND Q4 ARE NOT KNOBS AND ARE NOT MEANT TO BE — they live in the SCOOP block of the constants section in `asteroids-deluxe.html`:** `SCOOP_MAGNET_W` (2.6, the pulling stroke width), `SCOOP_MAGNET_BLUR` (18, its glow), `SCOOP_MAGNET_NOSE_W` (10) and `SCOOP_MAGNET_NOSE_D` (22, the level-0 nose V's half-width and depth). All four can be changed freely on a look-call; **the hue is a real decision**, so say if it should move rather than just "make it brighter."

**WHAT TO PLAY (from §7, verbatim):** enough levels to hold a **full cargo** repeatedly with a Magnet running — so at least to the point where `payloadSlots` has grown and the field is dense. Levels **1 → 12** is sufficient for everything here; this changeset touches nothing level-scaled, so there is no reason to reach 45. `startLevel` (GLOBAL) can jump you, remembering it gives a level-N field with a level-1 ship — **no scoop upgrades and no banked powerups**, which makes it the wrong tool for Q3 and Q4 specifically. Those two need a real played-through run.

**CS025's six questions, verbatim:**

**Q1 — the resume delay.** Fill cargo with the Magnet up, deliver one canister, and feel the moment the pull returns. Is **250 ms** right — long enough that the field does not snap back onto you mid-offload, short enough that it never feels broken? Slider: `Magnet resume delay` (POWERUPS). **Report the number.**

Paul says: Yes, 250 ms seems good as a default. I still want a debug knob for this setting, if I don't already have one.

**Q2 — the push, two numbers.** `Magnet push kick` (default **120 px/s**) and `Magnet push spread` (default **45°**). Set the kick to **0** for the clean A/B — that disables the burst entirely and leaves only §1's suppression, which is the honest way to judge whether the push is earning its place. Then: does the cloud read as being *shoved off* you, or as quietly switching off? Is the fan wide enough to look like scattering rather than a shell expanding? **Report both numbers.**

Paul says: 120 px/s seems good for magnet push kick, and 45 degrees for magnet push spread, but give me debug knobs if I don't already have them.

**Q3 — does the Hunter-on-top-of-you problem actually go away?** This is the changeset's central bet and the only question that can invalidate it. Park in a dense field with a Magnet, fill cargo, and stay there. Does a Hunter still coalesce on top of the ship? A Hunter forming *nearby* after the pieces have drifted apart is the system working; one forming *on you* is not.

Paul says: Yes, this problem seems to have mostly gone away.

**Q4 — the scoop energy tell.** Two parts. **(a)** Does the magnet-blue (`POWERUP_COLOR.magnet`, `#8ab6ff`) read as *charged*, or does it just read as the hull? It sits close to `COLOR.ship` (`#9fd8ff`), and that is the specific risk. **(b)** Does the tell *inform* — do you notice the scoop go dark when cargo fills, and does that teach you the magnet has stopped? All four constants (`SCOOP_MAGNET_W` 2.6, `_BLUR` 18, `_NOSE_W` 10, `_NOSE_D` 22) are look-calls and can be changed freely; the hue itself is a real decision, so say if it should move. Play at **scoopLevel 0** for at least a stretch — the nose V is a different tell and is the one nobody has seen.

Paul says: The scoop energy tell is not working. It is impossible to tell the difference between charged or not. I would like to just get rid of this function altogether. So this is backing out of a change I originally asked for in cs025. We just don't need another visual thing on the player ship.

**Q5 — the defensive fill loop.** §2.2 accepts that filling cargo now resets nearby coalescence clocks, making it a usable defensive move. Does that read as a smart play the game is rewarding, or as an exploit you feel obliged to spam? If it is the latter, the lever to reach for is `magnetPushKick` toward 0, not a new rule.

Paul says: This seems fine. 

**Q6 — the critical voice lines, and one specific failure to watch for.** Do `health_low`, `health_relief` and `cargo_full` now reliably speak? And the thing re-validation is supposed to prevent: does a `cargo_full` ever arrive so late that it is confusing — after you have already started offloading? It should be discarded rather than spoken in that case (§4.4d). **If you hear a stale one, that is a bug, not a tuning question** — say so plainly and P5 inherits a fix it does not currently carry.

Paul says: This is working well. However, we need to add the announcement of the next level as a critical line. So when we go to "Level 2" for example, that line is definitely spoken and we definitely see a "Level 2" on the screen. Minimum is to have that "Level 2" show as a caption for speach. Ideally a "Level 2" would appear briefly in the middle of the screen, large, easy to read, with some effect of fading in or out.

**⛔ CS025 P1 ADDS AN A/B SWITCH TO THE DEBUG PANEL THAT THE GATE WILL WANT: `Magnet resume delay`, POWERUPS section, default 250 ms.** Setting it to **0 turns the whole full-cargo suppression off** and the build behaves exactly as CS024 shipped, so the feature can be compared on and off without a rebuild. **The gate questions this phase raises, for §7's list:** does the Magnet going quiet at full cargo read as *the game working* or as *the powerup breaking*? (There is deliberately **no HUD change** — the player's intended tell is P3's scoop glow going dark, which does not exist yet, so a P1-only playtest has **no tell at all**.) And is 250 ms the right resume delay — does the pull snapping straight back the instant a canister is delivered feel abrupt at 0, or sluggish at 1000?

**✅ CS024's GATE B IS CLOSED — answered by Paul, and P7 has run on those answers.** **Outcome: seven of eight "fine", one number (Q11, Engine 5.0 → 10.0 s of thrust), and one NEW MECHANIC REQUEST that P7 deliberately did not build (Q13, the half-strength Mega Delivery at levels 6–11 — see `## Known issues` and `## Next up`).** The eight questions and Paul's inline answers are preserved verbatim below, along with the full briefing and lever table the gate was run against; nothing has been edited or summarised. **No lever moved.** The one thing a future reader should take from this gate beyond its answers: the standing "report the number you landed on, not a yes/no" instruction worked exactly as intended in both directions — Gate A came back with three numbers and all three shipped into P4, and Gate B came back with one number and one design ask, both of which are traceable to a specific question.

**⛔ CS024 P6f CHANGES WHAT A GATE B RUN WILL FEEL LIKE AT LEVELS 1–11, AND ADDS ONE NEW QUESTION AT THE BOTTOM OF THE LIST.** It exists because of Gate B's own playtest — **too many large Hunters at levels 1–3** — and the fix is count-shaped rather than a `coalescePause` retune, because that lever governs how fast one clump forms while nothing limited how many formed at once.

**THE CAP NOW SCALES: 1 large Hunter at levels 1–2, 2 at 3–4, 3 at 5–6, 4 at 7–8, 5 at 9–10, and 6 from level 11 on, flat forever after.** The flat `100` it replaces was never a real ceiling. **Two knobs, both in the HUNTER section under `Hunter last-stand speed`** — **`Hunter cap max`** (6; the plateau) and **`Hunter cap levels per step`** (2; how many levels between each extra Hunter — set it to 1 for one more per level, 8 for one more per eight). **Neither is a lever**: no floor/ceil/steps, no `▼`/`↳`, no chain. A ceiling on how many things can hunt you at once is a stability guarantee, not a difficulty dial, so it deliberately sits outside the odometer.

**A SATURATED CLUMP NO LONGER VANISHES AT A FULL CAP — IT HOLDS, AND IT IS YOURS TO DEAL WITH.** Before this phase, a 12-piece clump reaching the threshold while the cap was full simply blew up. Now it **sits on the board wearing a slowly pulsing teal ring** (the Hunter's own colour, on a body drawn in the salvage yellow-green), waiting for a slot to open. **It is ordinary salvage in every other respect: scoop it — partially or wholly — for a single large payload, or shoot it and get all twelve canisters back.** Scooping a full 12-clump is the biggest single haul in the game and is roughly what the removed bonus canister used to hand you, except you have to earn it. One accepted consequence you may notice: **loose singles drifting near a held clump will sit there rather than merging into it** — a saturated clump stops absorbing entirely, so it can't quietly grow to 40 pieces and still only produce one Hunter. A third knob, **`Held clump max`** (4), caps how many can be held at once; past that, the old destroy behaviour returns as a backstop so the field can't fill with pending Hunters.

**Gate B's seven questions are unchanged and still answerable in the same run.** The new question is **14**, at the bottom of the list.

**CS024 P6b ADDS NO NEW GATE, BUT IT ADDS ONE ASK TO THE GATE B SESSION: GET PAST LEVEL 33.** The defect P6b fixed — a small UFO that was *slower* at level 33 than at level 25 — is invisible below L25, and the stagger it replaced it with (speed → fire rate → evasiveness → shot velocity → accuracy, saturating L33 → L65) is invisible below L33. Gate B's seven questions are unchanged and still answerable in its stated 1 → 20 window; this is an addition, not a replacement. **If you do reach the 25–45 band, the single most useful thing to report is whether the UFOs read as continuously escalating, with no point where they feel like they got easier.** `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS025.md` §4 has the full five-question version for CS025's own gate later.

A run that stops at 30 sees roughly the first half of one chain and none of the stagger.

**CS024 P6d's `startLevel` debug knob (GLOBAL section, registry) can shortcut the spot-check.** Rather than playing 40+ minutes to reach L41–L99, drag `startLevel` to the level in question (e.g. 41, 65, 90) and start a fresh run — `leverState`/`liveLevers` land exactly where a played-through run would, since every lever is a pure function of `game.wave`. **Any run started this way voids recording** (a `"DEBUG RUN"` HUD marker confirms it; no high score, no achievement, no lifetime-stat write) and gives a level-N field with a level-1 SHIP — no scoop upgrades, no banked powerups, no towed load. That's fine for judging saturation/pacing/tuning questions (7, 8, 9, and the flatness-past-L41 question below), which are about the field, not the player's build — but for anything that reads on the ship's accumulated state (scoop level, powerup bank size, chain length), a real played-through run is still the only honest source, and the two should not be conflated when recording an answer below.

**CS024 P6e ADDS FOUR RECOVERY/RESET TOOLS TO THE SAME PANEL — READ THIS BEFORE AN EVENING OF RETUNING.** (1) **`r` resets the currently SELECTED row** to its registry default — works identically on a lever's floor/ceil/steps row, a millisecond-unit row, anything; it's inert while you're mid-way through typing a number. (2) **A "Reset all debug knobs to defaults" row** now sits near the bottom of the panel (scroll down past the last knob) — confirm it to snap the WHOLE registry back to shipped defaults in one shot, same confirm-dialog pattern as the Controls screen's "Reset all controls to defaults?". (3) **A new toggle at the very TOP of the panel, "Overrides Applied," defaults ON.** Flip it OFF and every slider you've touched goes inert — the game plays on shipped defaults — WITHOUT losing a single edit; flip it back ON and everything you'd dialed in returns exactly as you left it. This is the A/B switch: retune, flip off, feel the shipped baseline, flip back on, no re-entry needed. **The panel tells you when it's off** — the title bar says so, and every row's number dims except the toggle itself. **⛔ One timing catch: the toggle (and every knob) is read at the point of use — next wave's spawn, next saucer spawned — so flipping it mid-wave won't change anything already on screen. The A/B only reads clean BETWEEN waves.** (4) **A second, separately-worded confirm row, "Reset saved scores," wipes the local high-score table.** This is IRREVERSIBLE and ONLY touches the score table — it does not touch achievements, unlocks, or lifetime stats in any way. Useful if a `startLevel`-jumped debug run or a wild retuning session leaves the table looking unrepresentative and you want a clean board before a real playtest pass.

**THE STANDING INSTRUCTION, RESTATED BECAUSE IT IS WHAT MAKES THE ANSWERS ACTIONABLE: for anything driven by a debug slider, open the panel, retune live, and REPORT THE NUMBER YOU LANDED ON — NOT A YES/NO.** "Too fast" costs the next session a guess; "I settled at 140" costs it nothing. Gate A proved this works: Q1 came back with three numbers and all three shipped verbatim into P4. **"Fine" is also a complete answer** — a gate that comes back clean means P7 is bump-and-sweep only, which has happened twice before (CS020 P2, CS022 P4). Do not manufacture changes to justify the gate.

Paul says: this is fine

**14. (ADDED BY CS024 P6f, out of this gate's own playtest.) Two parts, both worth a separate sentence.** **(a) Does the early game now read as SURVIVABLE at levels 1–3?** 
Paul says: yes, this is now fine. 

One large Hunter at levels 1–2, rising one per two levels to six at level 11 — if that is now too quiet rather than merely survivable, drag `Hunter cap levels per step` toward 1 and **report the number you land on**, not just "too easy." **(b) Does a held clump read as PENDING rather than as ordinary salvage?** 
Paul says: this reads fine, I like the special appearance of these. 
It is the one wearing the pulsing teal ring, sitting at 12 pieces waiting for a Hunter slot. The question is whether that ring makes you treat it as a threat you can still defuse — scoop it, shoot it — or whether it just looks like another big canister you happen not to have collected yet. If it reads as ordinary salvage, the tell is a LOOK-CALL and can be changed freely.

Paul says: 

**⛔ THE BRIEFING — WHAT CHANGED UNDER THIS GATE IN CS024 P6c, BECAUSE THE PANEL YOU WILL OPEN IS NOT THE ONE THIS GATE WAS WRITTEN FOR.** The previous version of this block described **one flat slider per lever**, showing `auto`, which pinned that lever to a constant the moment you touched it. **That is gone.** A lever is a floor, a ceiling and a step count, so it now has **three rows each — 51 in all** — and moving any one of them re-derives that lever's whole ramp immediately, at every level.

**The three rows, and what each one actually does:**

- **`· floor`** — the value the lever holds at **level 1**, and the value it returns to **exactly** on every wrap. Move this to change where the ramp *starts*.
- **`· ceil`** — the value at the lever's **top step**. For a **dependent** that is where it plateaus and stays forever; for a **driver** it is the last rung before it sawtooths back to floor. Move this to change where the ramp *ends*.
- **`· steps`** — how many discrete positions the ramp has, **minimum 2**, integers only. For a **driver** this is its **period**: `junkCount` at 10 steps wraps every 10 levels, at 7 it wraps every 7. For a **dependent** it is how many carries it needs to saturate. **This is the knob for "the ramp is the wrong LENGTH"** — the thing that previously could only be reported in words. Changing it moves your position in the odometer discontinuously, so **start a fresh run after changing a step count.**

**⛔ FLOOR EQUAL TO CEIL PINS A LEVER FLAT AT EVERY LEVEL.** That is exactly what the retired single row did, so nothing is lost — it is now one thing you can choose rather than the only thing a slider can do. Useful for isolating one quantity's feel; **not** what you want for answering Q10.

**⛔ SEVEN LEVERS ARE INVERTED, THEIR ROWS ARE LABELLED `(inv)`, AND A FLOOR NUMERICALLY ABOVE ITS CEIL IS CORRECT.** `coalescePause` (5 → 1.5 s), `ufoAppearFreq` (25 → 12 s), both `ufoFireFreq*`, both `ufoDirChange*` and `ufoAccuracySmall` all **descend**, because a shorter delay and a smaller aim error are HARDER. **An inverted lever's floor is its EASY end.** There is no floor ≤ ceiling validator anywhere in this build and there must never be one — and as of P6c the slider ranges are deliberately wide enough to drag either endpoint **past** the other, which is a supported configuration, not a mistake. (P5's sliders could not do this at all: `coalescePause`'s 5.0 s floor was also its slider's maximum.)

**THE PANEL SHOWS THE CHAIN.** In each of the JUNK / HUNTER / UFO sections, a **`▼`** row unindented is that chain's **DRIVER** — everything indented below it moves when the driver wraps. A **`  ↳`** row is a **DEPENDENT**: it never advances on the level clock at all, only on its driver's wraps. A row with **neither glyph nor indent** is a flat knob belonging to no chain (`Small UFO chance`, `Hunter last-stand speed`, the GARBAGE / CHAIN GUARD / DELIVERY / POWERUPS / GLOBAL rows) — **those are not part of Q10.** The glyphs are derived from the table, so they cannot disagree with the mechanism.

**THE FULL TABLE, computed from the shipped build rather than from the spec.** "Sat." is the level at which a dependent first reaches its ceiling and plateaus; a driver has none — it wraps forever, every `steps` levels.
