**Four things about that table worth knowing BEFORE you play, because each one will otherwise look like a bug.** **(1) THE SATURATION COLUMN IS WHY THE PLAY RANGE MOVED TO 45.** CS024 P6b restaged the nine UFO dependents so they saturate in a deliberate ORDER — speed (L33), then rate of fire (L41), then evasiveness (L49), then shot velocity (L57), and **accuracy last (L65)** — the UFOs get *faster* before they get *accurate*. At L20 six of those nine have not moved past their second carry; at L45 you have seen most of it. **The unevenness is the design and must not be "tidied" into one number.** **(2) `ufoAppearFreq` CYCLES FOREVER and never permanently tightens** — level 100 reads exactly as level 1. That is deliberate: it is the chain's driver, and a driver that stopped cycling would freeze all nine levers under it. UFO *pressure* escalates through those nine; UFO *rhythm* stays constant. **(3) The three chains breathe on 10 / 8 / 8, which is Q9's whole subject:** `junkCount` resets at L11, L21, L31, L41…; `coalescePause` and `ufoAppearFreq` both at L9, L17, L25, L33, L41… — the two 8-period chains reset together and the 10-period one drifts against them, meeting at L41. **Q9 is now answerable with a NUMBER**: if 8 and 8 landing together reads as a lurch, drag one of them to 7 or 9 on its `· steps` row and say which you preferred. **(4) `Junk count` is a COUNT, and the panel will happily show you a fraction on its floor/ceil rows** (they are ordinary sliders); the spawn rounds to the nearest whole satellite, and the difficulty log records what actually spawned.

**WHAT Q10 IS NOW ASKING.** Not "is a floor or a ceiling wrong" but any of three things, each with its own slider: **the ramp starts in the wrong place** (`· floor`), **ends in the wrong place** (`· ceil`), or **takes the wrong number of levels to get there** (`· steps`). Report the id and the number. `IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-CS024.md`'s copy of Q10 still says step counts are not sliders — that wording is stale as of P6c and P7's doc sweep owns it (Known issues).

**WHAT Q11 AND Q12 ARE ACTUALLY ASKING, given what P6 changed under them.** **Q11 (Engine-as-fuel):** the tank is **5 seconds of FORWARD THRUST**, not 5 seconds of wall time — rotating, coasting, firing and drifting all cost nothing, so in practice one pickup lasts far longer than five seconds of play, and how much longer depends entirely on how much you hold thrust. The effect while it lasts is unchanged (towed mass halved, so thrust, top speed and the momentum tug all lighten) and it is **flat, not tapering** — the last tenth of a second feels exactly like the first. Two live sliders under the **POWERUPS** header: `engineBurnSeconds` (default **5.0**, range 0.5–60, step 0.5) and `engineMassMult` (default **0.5**, range 0–1, step 0.05 — LOWER is stronger). **Q12 (count-only):** Rapid and Triple were already count-capable but shipped timed, so what changed for most runs is that they now end when you spend them rather than when a clock runs out — 40 pulls and 30 pulls, and a Triple 3-fan is ONE pull. Magnet is 40 hooked canisters, counted at the hook (a clump costs one per piece). The chain guard is 3 intercepts. **All of them BANK now, including Engine and Guard for the first time** — a second pickup adds a full lot on top of what's left rather than refreshing, and the HUD ring shows the surplus as a thin halo outside the main ring.

**✅ CS024's GATE A IS CLOSED — answered by Paul, and P4 has run on those answers.** **Outcome: five of six came back "fine" and carried no code; Q1 came back with a RETUNE, and it is baked into the build as of CS024 P4** — `GARBAGE_COALESCE_DELAY` 3.0 → **5.0 s**, `GARBAGE_MAGNET_RANGE` 180 → **160 px**, `GARBAGE_MAGNET_PULL` 40 → **30 px/s²**, all three exactly as reported, all three on their own slider's grid. **Q1 came back the OPPOSITE way round from the failure the question was written to catch:** it asked "do hunters appear at all?" and the answer was "yes, and too fast" — permanent garbage (P3) removed the pressure that the 3.0 s inert window had been tuned against, and nobody predicted that. **One thing worth carrying forward:** the new 5.0 s delay is *exactly* the `coalescePause` lever's `floor`, so P5's wiring keeps level 1 at the value the gate validated and the lever's job is to shorten it from there. `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS024.md` §2.5's parenthetical "180 px / 40 px/s²" is now stale — P7's doc sweep owns it (see Known issues). Kept below for the record; nothing here is outstanding.

**⛔ CS024's GATE A (the original questions, for the record) — P3 was landed and P4 was blocked until these six were answered here.** The questions live in `PLANNED-FEATURES-CS024.md` §6. Below each is the headless answer already in hand, so the gate can concentrate on what only playing can settle. **Q1 (do Hunters still appear at all? play at least 6 levels without deliberately farming — roughly how many levels before the first one?) is the one that can change the plan.** Headlessly, the pipeline works end to end and works at every level: 12 co-located canisters become a large core in well under a second of simulated time, at level 1 as readily as level 63. What headless cannot tell you is whether canisters in an actual played field ever get close enough, for long enough, to reach 12 — the inert window is 3 s per piece, the mutual attraction range is 180 px in a 2560×1440 world, and you will be shooting and scooping them the whole time. **If the answer is "never," say so plainly and P4 inherits a coalescence retune it does not currently carry.** Note also that levels 2–4 can now produce a Hunter where the retired cap table forbade it (see Known issues) — an early Hunter is the new system working, not a regression. 
Paul says: Yes, hunters are formed. However, they are formed too quickly. I used debug knobs to come up with a better feel. I changed Garbage attraction delay to 5,000 ms, Garbage radius to 160px, and Garbage force to 30 px per second (squared) Please confirm if you are able to make these the default values.

**Q2 (is the field readable? does a level-6 screen read as salvage-rich or as visual noise?) has arithmetic behind it worth knowing before you look.** One fully-harvested large satellite line yields 39 canisters; a full Hunter lineage yields 18; nothing removes any of them but you and the dock. At a level-1 `junkCount` of 3 that is ~117 canisters from the satellites of a single level — over half the 220 ceiling. 
Paul says: Q2 is fine. 

**Q3 (does the cull ever visibly fire? you should never see a canister vanish) is the direct A/B on that:** the cull is silent by design — no blink, no particle, no sound, verified — so if you catch one disappearing, `GARBAGE_SOFT_MAX` is too low. Both are live sliders in the debug panel (`Garbage soft max`, `Garbage hard max`, GARBAGE section) — **retune in-session and report the number you landed on, not a yes/no.** 
Paul says Q3 is fine, overriding your request for a number. 

**Q4 (frame rate, subjectively, at the worst moment you can produce) has a headless half already answered, and it is the half that bounds the retune.** The O(n²) coalescence walk costs 24,090 pair visits per frame at the 220 ceiling and 44,850 at the 300 backstop — both measured exactly, with a deterministic counter rather than a clock. **The cost is quadratic in the ceiling: 450 pieces would be ~99,900/frame and 1,000 would be ~500,000.** So if the answer to Q3 is "raise it," raising to ~300–350 is affordable and beyond that is not — at which point the spatial grid recorded for CS025 becomes the real answer rather than a bigger number. 
Paul says: Q4 is fine. 

**Q5 (does removing the bonus canister leave the early levels flat?) is a straight subtractive question with no headless component.** It was explicitly a reward-flavoured reason to keep playing the low-stakes early waves, worth 250 points at the scoop and inviting greed by fattening the chain. Nothing replaces it in this changeset.
Paul says: Q5 is fine. 

**Q6 (last stand: does a large Hunter resuming its old vector when debris reappears read as intentional, or as a bug?)** — this is unchanged behaviour that the phase deliberately preserved rather than tidied: the moment debris reappears the steering block simply stops executing and the core keeps whatever velocity it held at that instant, with no disarm and no restore. It is now a documented exception rather than an accident, and its speed is a live slider (`Hunter last-stand speed`, HUNTER section, default 50 px/s). 
Paul says: Q6 is fine. 

**⛔ CS023's PLAYTEST GATE IS NOW OPEN AND BLOCKING — all code phases (P1–P4b) are landed, and P5 must not run until these are answered here.** The fifteen questions live in `IMPLEMENTATION-PHASES-CS023.md` under "⛔ PLAYTEST GATE"; write the answers into this section, and **report the number you landed on, not a yes/no**, for anything driven by a slider. Play levels **3, 6, 9 and 12** for one full pass of the ramp, plus **21** for a full field component on top, plus at least one FIELD level now that P4b makes the drift live there too (Q14 below). **Eight things are live sliders in the hidden Debug panel's ORBIT section: the four densities, `orbitAngVel`, `orbitFastMult`, and `debrisBounceRestitution` and `debrisDriftAccel`** (the last renamed from `orbitGravityAccel` by P4b, spec C15 — same slider, same position, new id; a value saved under the old id was reset to default once).

**⛔ THE ONE PIECE OF SETUP THE GATE ACTUALLY REQUIRES: play at least one level to a FULL HARVEST, orbit OR field.** The inward drift's trigger fires only once **nothing at all** is left inside 1,000 px of the dock (P4C: was 814 px — the trigger followed the rings out) — the whole population, plus every split child still in that radius, on EITHER archetype now (P4b, C15 — the gate that used to make this orbit-only is gone). A level played to a normal finish never shows the drift, so questions **11 (does it read at all)**, **12 (is the pull fast enough, is the cap right)** and **13 (do the interrupts read as knocking something off course)** are simply unanswerable without one full harvest. `debrisDriftAccel` at **0** disables the drift outright and is the A/B.

What CS023 P4/P4b have left you looking at, with the headless answers already in hand so the gate can concentrate on what only playing can settle:

- **The drift is slow on purpose, and the number to move first is the acceleration, not the cap (Q11/Q12).** REVISED BY P4C: at the shipped 30 px/s² a piece needs **10.5 s and 1,645 px of fall** to reach the now-314.2 px/s ceiling, so **the cap only binds for pieces starting beyond ~2,445 px from the dock — and NEITHER WORLD IS THAT BIG** (orbit's farthest point is 2,203 px, the field's 1,469). The longest falls either archetype allows are 1,403 px (orbit, arriving ~290 px/s) and 669 px (field, ~200 px/s), so **the cap never binds anywhere in the shipped game**, which is pure headroom for this question: raise `debrisDriftAccel` to ~35 px/s² before it starts limiting even the longest fall. If the field feels like it is taking forever to come back, that is the **acceleration**, and `debrisDriftAccel` is the slider — on either archetype. Headless says the arithmetic is exact to 1e-9 and that a piece released at 800 px coasts on more than 200 px further rather than parking; whether that reads as *the level re-forming around you* is the question, and now it is a question for a field level too, not only an orbit one.
- **The cap is derived, so question 4's answer moves it (Q4/Q12).** **314.2 px/s** (P4C: was 255.7 — it followed ring 4's radius out to 1,000 px) is ring 4's own tangential speed at the shipped `ORBIT_ANG_VEL` 6 °/s and `ORBIT_FAST_MULT` 3.0 — the fastest anything moves in orbit, which is the whole justification for the number, and it stays the SAME number on a field level (it derives from constants, never from the live layout). **If you retune either of those two velocity knobs at the gate, say so explicitly, because the drift's ceiling moves with them, on every level** and P5 has to record that. There is deliberately no separate slider for the cap, and no field-level variant of it either.
- **The interrupts are all real mechanics you already have (Q13).** Shooting a drifting piece, ramming one, watching two collide, or watching a UFO take one out — all four release it, and it keeps whatever velocity it had accumulated. Headless proves each one fires identically on a field level as on an orbit one; what it cannot tell you is whether the release *reads* as knocking something off course or just as the pull quietly switching off. A piece that has been drifting a while is moving fast enough to be legible, which is the bet.
- **`debrisBounceRestitution` is a live slider (Q9).** Default 1.0 (a perfectly elastic bounce, mirroring the shield's). Lower it if the shell reads like a pinball table; range 0–1.5, step 0.05. `DEBRIS_MASS`'s 9:3:1 is **not** a knob and should not become one — it is fixed by mass conservation through the split the game already performs, and it is what makes a small ricochet off a large.
- **Q14 IS ANSWERED BY THE CODE NOW, NOT LEFT FOR THE GATE: field levels get the drift too.** P4's own gate framed this as an open question ("is a field level too small to feel the pull, or does it want the mechanic as well?"); Correction C15 retracted the orbit-only recommendation that question rested on — measured against the real worlds, a field level's dead zone beyond the trigger (46%) is comparable to an orbit level's (75%), so the premise that it would be "on almost permanently" was wrong. **What the gate should now answer instead is whether the field-level FEEL matches the orbit-level one** (same trigger, same target, same acceleration, cap never binding) — not whether it should exist there.

**CS020 P1b — THE GATE BETWEEN P1b AND P2 (CLOSED, kept for the record).** Everything about the fix is headless-solid (187 assertions, mutation-tested against five deliberate breakages, and identical to a clean pass on every legitimate skirt) but nothing about how it FEELS has been seen in a browser. Four questions, priority order:

1. **FLAG-CS020-g — the window. This is the one value P2 exists to carry.** Skirt the dock at speed with a real load, several times, at different exit velocities. Does **4,000 ms** cover a normal overshoot-and-return? It ships at 4.0 s rather than the 2.0 first proposed because simulating `Ship.update`'s actual integrator says a 180° turn costs 0.75 s before thrust can even begin and a normal pilot exiting at 200–300 px/s with a real load is outside for 2.8–3.8 s — 2 s would have failed almost every real skirt. **Drag `Delivery one-effort window` in the hidden Debug Options panel** (new `DELIVERY` section, right after `CHAIN GUARD`) until it feels right and report the number. It is live: a change takes effect on the very next time you re-enter the ring, no restart. Since the hook reset is what bounds the counter, a generous window costs nothing — err long if in doubt.
2. **The one-effort rule in play (spec §5.4 — where this is STRICTER than the literal ask).** Skirt with 8, deliver 3, then hook one stray on the way back: the combo resets and the 5 originals deliver at 1…5 rather than 4…8, because you topped up mid-run and that makes it a new run. This is the load-bearing rule that keeps the counter capped, and it is the one case where the originals might have been expected to survive. **Does it ever read as unfair, or is it invisible?** If it reads badly, note it — but understand the fix is not "special-case it", it is a different bounding rule, and that is a CS021 conversation, not a P2 edit.
3. **The cap.** The combo ceiling is now exactly the payload capacity, by construction. Does that change how a full 24-load at level 12 feels — better (the ceiling is now something you can actually see yourself reach), or anticlimactic? Related: with no HUD readout of the combo anywhere (FLAG-CS020-i, deliberately CS021), is the ceiling even legible in play?
4. **P1 still good.** Does the parking behaviour still feel right after P1b, or did the one-effort rule make in-ring cleanup feel pointless? Headlessly it is unchanged — the 60-second level-1 park still pays exactly 37,500 with zero latches and no SMD, and the board still clears — but "unchanged number" and "still feels worth doing" are different questions.

**✅ CS020 P1's own gate CLOSED before this session** — FLAG-CS020-c, -d, -e and -f were all cleared in play and the parking exploit is dead. Its four questions are kept below for the record; nothing there is outstanding.

**CS020 P1 — THE GATE BETWEEN P1 AND P2 (CLOSED, kept for the record).** Everything about the fix is headless-solid (233 assertions, mutation-tested against five deliberate breakages, bit-identical to the pre-fix build on legitimate play) but nothing about how it FEELS has been seen in a browser. Four questions, priority order:

1. **FLAG-CS020-c — the repair residual, and this one comes with a measured number.** Incidentals still route through `addScore()`, which trips `REPAIR_MILESTONE` (10,000 points → 25 HP, or `REPAIR_FULL_BONUS` 2,500 points at full hull). Measured this session on the real path: a 60-second park fed ~10 canisters/s pays 30,000 flat and crosses **three** milestones; at the 20/s offload ceiling it would be one milestone every 10 seconds. **Park at the dock with a Magnet and a drained hull and see whether that reads as a healing station.** If it does, the fix is a new `DOCK_INCIDENTAL_SCORE` constant at ~10–15, a one-line change P2 can carry.
2. **FLAG-CS020-f — the straggler.** Tow a load in with a Magnet running. A piece dragged in during the final approach is tagged incidental and loses its multiplier, with no grace period. Does that read as unfair, or is it invisible? If unfair, the fix is a grace window and it is **not** a P2 change — it is CS021.
3. **FLAG-CS020-d/e — the feedback.** At high incidental throughput the dock can throw up to 20 `+50` floaters a second: legible, or confetti? And does the flat `AudioSys.deliver(1)` read as "recycled" or as "broken"?
4. **The feel of the fix overall.** Does parking still feel like a reasonable thing to do — clearing the board against the Kessler loop — or does it now feel pointless? Pointless would be a real loss; the mechanic was a nice find and the goal was to bound it, not kill it.

One thing the fix changes that is intended, not collateral, and worth watching for anyway: **Maxed Out is now effectively a level-12+ achievement** (a 24-piece TOW needs `payloadSlots` 24), and **Recycling Magnate / Salvage King are no longer farmable at the dock**.
